The normal<x-A l,u> is nonnegative but has a negative coefficient. Taking theta1 cancels an earlier remainder coefficient yet makes bridge weight b-C=k-s negative; no actual same-body membership of the extrapolated point was proved. The root square controls p-r but not the leftover r component. The positive root coefficient itself is not the problem: Theta>3C(C+k)/46 at all relevant higher levels. No all-depth certificate, uniform controlled loss, or actual counterexample to U was obtained.

These two signed identities identify an unpaid term; they are not a replacement theorem of equal strength and do not establish minimax progress by themselves. The next upper attempt needs an actual quantitative payment with all correlated child data.

## Research judgment and unallocated leads

Do not renew the bare area-preservation argument, rederive the disk recurrence, retune the already bounded nonreflecting/axis sectors, assume a difference-space objective, or declare theta1 feasible from convexity. None supplies the original target.

A more promising lower search, not yet allocated or proved, is the complete NONVERTEX evolution in a fixed weighted centered simplex. On a finite probability space with positive fixed weights p_i, use the mean-zero L2 space and K={g:E g=0,g_i>=-1}. This is a compact centered simplex. Projection has g_i=(X_i-t)_+-1, where t is uniquely chosen by E(X-t)_+=1. Thus one fixed function gives the actual update X'=X-h*g, preserving E X=0 and the original squared radius E X0^2. Its objective is F=t+1/2+E(X-t)_+^2/2. These parent manual starting formulas require independent checking before use; they are NOT a new lower bound or a universality theorem for arbitrary functions.

The potential advantage is simultaneous nonvertex face mixtures with fixed atom weights, rather than the vertex-by-vertex itineraries previously bounded. The starting law must be selected ONCE from the full prescribed word, and then all physical steps propagated without resetting atoms or child states. A useful new call must actually choose such a finite law and prove the original-scale payoff, first on the inherited family and then uniformly on all words. Merely outputting this probability reformulation would repeat the old gap. The existing R5 centered-simplex construction and R6/R7 restrictions are prerequisites, not proofs about this full nonvertex law.

For an upper attempt, one unallocated possibility is to quantify what fraction of an ACTUAL child maximization remainder is retained by its U gap. The relation E_L=V_L/A+RL suggests testing a genuinely proved relative-debt bound before trying another scalar terminal profile. No coefficient, base case or composition theorem for such a bound is known. It must not be assumed, and a clipped/extrapolated comparison point must have actual membership and all correction terms proved. These are directions, not results or renewed grants.

The parent also considered an interior shifted disk B(b e1,1),0<b<1, whose unique minimum and reversible horizontal drift differ from the tangent-ray geometry. No trajectory/payoff theorem or allocation was made for it. Its existence alone is not evidence that it can close the target.

# Actual projection normals and complete-child gluing

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation78 after actual ended baseline77.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance91f76640c669d4da.
One allocated invocation, controller cap1, completed ceiling78/attempted79.
No mathematical programs, numerical or recurrence replay, formal calls,
fact_submit, consults, installations or subagents. Original target OPEN.
This is one joint mathematical candidate, not a collection of accepted facts.

## Verdict and exact scope

The requested universal U_d block inequality is NOT proved or refuted.
In particular the low-d, arbitrary-start H2 stress test is NOT closed.
There is no full triangle upper bound and no full-function W upper bound.

This round develops ONE mechanism, retaining projection-normal reserves.
It derives an exact identity with all physical and scaled auxiliary points
in one body, and displays the precise H2 central debt. It pays that debt
uniformly for all bodies and starts when d>=2/epsilon-2(A+B), and pays
all depths and every d>=1 on the entire initial unit ball of the centered
triangle, including starts outside its quadratic core. It also pays the
previous actual strict-switch H3 family using a new terminal-norm bound.

A tempting strengthened child law, the terminal shifted-norm bound, would
suffice for the desired U law. It has an explicit two-child gluing identity.
But this stronger law is FALSE for general compact bodies on the ACTUAL
original H3, and indeed at every parent depth at least3: one static
asymmetric interval and one legal complete two-cap trajectory refute it.
The same entire interval family satisfies the U law with ratio below3/4.
Thus this counterexample rejects the strengthening, not U itself.
It must not be advertised as a counterexample for the centered triangle.

No second directional storage is introduced. Failure of the stronger norm
law has not supplied a decisive failure of the requested U law itself.
The remaining task is an actual low-d H2 debt estimate, followed by a
uniform same-m all-depth estimate retaining the nonlocal normal coupling.
A signed telescope or a sufficient-condition reduction is not that estimate.

## 1. Definitions, normalization, and accepted scope

Fix ONE integer m>=1536. Set

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
 c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a*c^j,
 S_j=sum H_j, n_j=2^j-1, D_m=1+2S_m.

All children inherit this same m and these same parameters. For the literal
H2 write A=1+a, B=1+beta, so H2=(A,B,A), S2=2A+B. The symbol A in this
H2 abbreviation is a physical step; below Cchild_j=1+S_j denotes a child mass.
It is NOT the original benchmark primitive capacity sequence.

For a nonempty compact convex subset D of a finite-dimensional Euclidean
space containing0, define

 P_D(x)=the Euclidean projection of x onto D,
 F_D(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
 U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>0,
 T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x).

For a finite nonnegative word h, T_hword is its actual chronological
composition. All uses of U with different d use this SAME body D.
The centered-triangle specialization is

 v1=(1,0), v2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),
 D_triangle=conv{v1,v2,v3}.

The original W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex
L-smooth functions with a minimizer, under x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i)/L.
The original normalization is W=2R. All physical leaves and the actual
terminal are retained here. Even a universal upper bound within the centered
triangle family would not by itself be an upper bound for that full W.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 and the CONDITIONAL
799/800 reduction if the full-function bound17/(16D_m) is established.
It does not supply that bound. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixes the ordered
interpolation residual and the actual unqueried-terminal convention.
Only those stated accepted scopes are used; no unaccepted general-body
representation theorem or previous unverified proof is a premise below.
All new assertions are derived together in this document.

## 2. Projection facts, legal objectives, and true minimizers

For g=P_D(x), squared-distance minimization along each segment in D gives

 <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                         (2.1)

Conversely, expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2 proves sufficiency of (2.1).
Adding the inequalities at x and y gives
||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2<=<P_D(x)-P_D(y),x-y>, proving1-Lipschitzness.
Completing the square identifies g as the unique maximizer in F_D.
The two maximizing inequalities bound
F_D(x+t)-F_D(x)-<g,t> between0 and ||t||^2. Thus F_D is differentiable,
convex, finite, and1-smooth, with gradient P_D.

Its complete minimum set is the polar cone

 K_D={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D},                     (2.2)

and its minimum is0. Membership in K_D makes every candidate in F_D
nonpositive, and g=0 attains0. If <z,g>>0 for some g in D, then t*g in D
for0<t<=1, and sufficiently small positive t gives positive objective.
This proves the converse. The closed convex set K_D contains0, so a closest
initial minimizer exists. We do not replace that distance by the origin norm.

For every d>0,

 U_d(x)=||x||^2/d-dist(x,dD)^2/d,
 U_d(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/d for EVERY z_star in K_D.      (2.3)

The first identity is completion of the square. For the second,
<z_star,g><=0 implies
2<x,g>-d||g||^2<=2<x-z_star,g>-d||g||^2
=||x-z_star||^2/d-d||g-(x-z_star)/d||^2.
Take the maximum. Consequently a proved U block law at j=m,d=1 would
have the true closest-distance consequence in this projection class.
This does not require a norm bound about the origin to be the true radius.

For actual queries and terminal put gi=P_D(xi), fi=F_D(xi). Then every
ordered original interpolation residual is exactly

 fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2
   =<xj-gj,gi-gj><=0.                                (2.4)

The same formula holds when either sample is any minimizer, with gradient0
and value0. Actual Euclidean vectors supply a PSD Gram. Scaling the objective
by L and using steps h/L preserves every point and the W normalization.

## 3. Exact one-step identity with BOTH normal reserves retained

Consider one actual step xplus=x-h*g, g=P_D(x), with h>0 and d>=1.
Put

 Dmass=d+2h, p=P_D(x/Dmass), q=P_D(xplus/d),
 theta=h/(Dmass-1), z=theta*g+(1-theta)*q,
 e=q-g,
 Nphys=<x-g,g-q>,
 Naux=<x-Dmass*p,p-z>.

Both normal quantities are nonnegative by (2.1). The mixture z is in D;
indeed0<theta<=1/2. Define

 Bcoef(d,h)=h*((2-h)*(d+2h)-2)/(d+2h-1)^2.

There is the EXACT identity

 U_(d+2h)(x)-U_d(xplus)
   = Bcoef(d,h)||q-g||^2 +2theta*Nphys
     +Dmass||p-z||^2+2Naux.                          (3.1)

To prove it, evaluate 2<x,z>-Dmass||z||^2 and subtract the final maximum
2<x-hg,q>-d||q||^2. With x=g+(x-g), the coefficient of <g,e> cancels
because theta*(Dmass-1)=h. The remainder is
-2theta<x-g,e>+[d-Dmass*(1-theta)^2]||e||^2.
The square coefficient is Bcoef(d,h). Finally the difference between the
initial maximum at p and its value at z is
Dmass||p-z||^2+2<x-Dmass*p,p-z>. This proves (3.1), including boundaries.

For0<h<=3/2, Bcoef>=0 because d+2h>=1+2h and
(2-h)(1+2h)-2=h(3-2h)>=0. More generally h<2 is paid when
(d+2h)(2-h)>=2. At h>2 the coefficient is negative for every d>=1.
The physical normal and the auxiliary normal are distinct. The auxiliary
square is also essential data; it is not another projection-normal inequality.

For a complete word of N steps let

 di=d+2 sum_(t=i)^(N-1)h_t,
 xi+1=xi-hi*gi, gi=P_D(xi), pi=P_D(xi/di),
 thetai=hi/(di-1), zi=thetai*gi+(1-thetai)*p_(i+1).

Then the exact whole-word gap is the sum, over EVERY physical i, of

 hi*((2-hi)*di-2)/(di-1)^2 *||p_(i+1)-gi||^2
 +2thetai<xi-gi,gi-p_(i+1)>
 +di||pi-zi||^2+2<xi-di*pi,pi-zi>.                   (3.2)

All pi, gi and zi belong to the same D. The normals at arbitrary other
physical or auxiliary points remain available through (2.1).
Equation (3.2) is an exact interface, not proof that the signed sum is
nonnegative at every depth.

## 4. The literal H2 stress test and its outstanding debt

Take x1=x0-A*g0, x2=x1-B*g1, x3=x2-A*g2. Define

 d0=d+4A+2B, d1=d+2A+2B, d2=d+2A, d3=d,
 gi=P_D(xi) for i=0,1,2; pi=P_D(xi/di) for i=0,1,2,3.

These are the THREE actual queried projections and FOUR scaled auxiliary
projections. No one is substituted by an independent maximizer from another
run or another body. The final actual projection P_D(x3) is also legal and
will be used in Section5, but p3 need not equal it when d>1.

Both leaf coefficients in (3.2) are nonnegative, since A<sqrt(2)<3/2.
The middle coefficient is

 Cmid=B*(epsilon*d1-2)/(d1-1)^2.                       (4.1)

It follows that, for every compact convex D containing0 and EVERY x0,

 d>=max(1,2/epsilon-2(A+B))
   => U_d(T_H2 x0)<=U_(d+2S2)(x0).                   (4.2)

This is a fully paid complete-block statement, with an explicit domain in d.
It does NOT handle the small trailing masses most relevant to terminal blocks.

When epsilon*d1<2 the exact unpaid central debt is

 Debt=B*(2-epsilon*d1)/(d1-1)^2 *||p2-g1||^2.         (4.3)

The required payment is the sum of the two positive leaf squares and ALL
three physical normals, three auxiliary mixture squares, and three auxiliary
normals in (3.2). Their actual nonlocal correlations may improve that payment.
No lower bound on this sum sufficient for EVERY x0 and every remaining
1<=d<2/epsilon-2(A+B) is proved here. In particular, the presence of these
nonnegative terms does not permit assuming they exceed (4.3).

The single long-step core obstruction is also exact: sufficiently small
nonzero x with x and (1-h)x in D_triangle has
U_d(T_h x)=(h-1)^2||x||^2/d>||x||^2/(d+2h) for h>2.
Section8 pays all such small inputs over each COMPLETE actual block.

## 5. A sufficient terminal shifted-norm certificate

For an arbitrary nonnegative complete word with mass S, input x, endpoint y,
and actual gradients gi, write y=x-sum_i hi*gi. For d>=1 put
q=P_D(y/d) and

 zbar=[sum_i hi*gi+(d+S)q]/(d+2S) in D.              (5.1)

All coefficients are nonnegative and sum to1. Evaluating the initial U at
this one feasible point and completing the square yields

 U_(d+2S)(x)-U_d(y)
   >= [||x||^2-||y+S*q||^2]/(d+2S).                 (5.2)

For verification of the algebra, x-(d+2S)zbar=y-(d+S)q; expand both squares
and subtract2<y,q>-d||q||^2. The numerator is exactly
||x||^2-||y||^2-2S<y,q>-S^2||q||^2.
The omitted difference is the nonnegative initial maximization remainder.

For a fixed y the two quantities ||P_D(y/d)||^2 and <y,P_D(y/d)> are
nonincreasing in d>0. Indeed if d2>d1 and qi=P_D(y/di), maximizing at the
opposite qi gives

d1*(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)
 <=2<y,q1-q2><=d2*(||q1||^2-||q2||^2).

The difference of squared norms must be nonnegative; the scalar product
difference then is nonnegative as well. Hence, for S>=0,
||y+S P_D(y/d)||^2 is nonincreasing in d.

In particular the stronger complete-block statement

 R_H(x):=||x||^2-||y+S P_D(y)||^2>=0                 (5.3)

would prove the desired U law for every d>=1, with retained reserve
R_H(x)/(d+2S). This is a sufficient strengthening, not an equivalence.
For F_D, its right-hand square is
||y||^2+2S F_D(y)+S(S+1)||P_D(y)||^2.
This explains its relationship to a primitive/s-composability potential.
Section7 gives a legal actual counterexample to (5.3) for general bodies.

Even the one-step thresholds show that (5.3) is stronger. For h>0, set
q=P_D(x-hg), e=g-q, N0=<x-g,g-q>, N1=<x-hg-q,q-g>.
Both normals are nonnegative and direct expansion gives

 ||x||^2-||x-hg+hq||^2
   =(2-h^2)||e||^2+2(1+h)N0+2N1.                   (5.4)

For the derivation, N0+N1=h<g,e>-||e||^2 and
||x||^2-||x-he||^2=2h<x,e>-h^2||e||^2.
Thus (5.3) holds for h<=sqrt(2). But for a core point and h=3/2 its shifted
endpoint is (1-h^2)x, of larger norm than x, whereas (3.1) still proves the
U law. This comparison is a logical warning, not an altered physical step.

## 6. Exact gluing through TWO actual children and their bridge

Let each real child be the same H_j with mass S=S_j, and b=b_j. For one
actual parent run define

 x --H_j--> y --b--> z=y-b*p --H_j--> t,
 p=P_D(y), q=P_D(t), w=p-q,
 e=z-(t+S*q)=sum_(right child i) hi*(gi-q),
 Rleft=||x||^2-||y+S*p||^2,
 Rright=||z||^2-||t+S*q||^2,
 Rparent=||x||^2-||t+(2S+b)q||^2.

Keep the actual nonlocal normals

 Ny=<y-p,p-q>, Nt=<t-q,q-p>,
 NR=-<t-q,e>=sum_(right child i)hi*<t-q,q-gi>.

All three are nonnegative; in particular NR uses the endpoint normal
against EVERY real right-child gradient. They are not independent reserves
from arbitrarily chosen runs.

The exact parent identity is

 Rparent = Rleft +(2S+b+1)/(S+1)*Rright
   -(S+b)/(S+1)*||e||^2 -2(S+1)<e,w>
   +[2(S+1)^2-(S+b)^2]||w||^2
   +2(2S+b+1)Ny +2(S+1)Nt +2(S+b)/(S+1)*NR.        (6.1)

Proof by displayed substitutions. First expand the three squared norms:

 Rparent=Rleft+Rright+(S+b)*[
   2<y,p>-2<t,q>+(S-b)||p||^2-(3S+b)||q||^2].

Since t=y-bp-Sq-e, the bracket is
2<q,e>+2<y+Sp,w>-(S+b)||w||^2. Next,

 Rright=||e||^2+2(S+1)<q,e>-2NR,
 Ny+Nt=(S+b)<p,w>-(S+1)||w||^2+<e,w>.

Substituting these two equalities gives (6.1) term by term.

The formula contains a negative ||e||^2 coefficient and a mixed term.
Even replacing both child residues and all three normals by unspecified
nonnegative numbers does not yield the parent sign. The w-square
coefficient itself changes sign at

 b-1=(sqrt(2)-1)(S+1).                               (6.2)

This is a concrete failure of an attempted composition from scalar child
signs. It is not a proof that the actual U debt in Section4 is unpaid.
The next section makes the stronger-law failure actual, rather than merely
assigning values to an unconstrained signed quadratic.

## 7. Actual all-depth counterexample to the GENERAL-BODY strengthening

Let r_s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda. Put Cchild_j=1+S_j and k_j=b_j-1=a*c^j.
For j>=2 define the ONE static interval

 D_j=[-r_j,1], r_j=k_j/Cchild_j,
 x0=Cchild_j, parent word H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+k_j,H_j).        (7.1)

This is a compact convex body containing0. It may depend on m and j,
but is fixed for the entire actual run. Its F_D has unique minimizer0,
since the interval contains a neighborhood of0. The true initial radius
is exactly Cchild_j.

Every left-child query is at least1: after physical prefix mass s<=S_j
it is Cchild_j-s>=1. Thus all its actual gradients equal1 and its endpoint y=1.
The central step sends y to z=-k_j. During the right child, at physical
prefix mass s<=S_j the point is -k_j+s*r_j<=-r_j. Its actual gradient
therefore equals-r_j at EVERY right query. At the actual terminal

 t=-k_j+S_j*r_j=-r_j.

Boundary projections at y and t are unique; no query is omitted. In the
notation of Section6, Rleft=Rright=0, e=0, Ny=Nt=NR=0. The parent shifted
endpoint has magnitude (2S_j+b_j+1)r_j. Therefore (5.3) fails precisely when

 k_j*(2Cchild_j+k_j)>Cchild_j^2,
 equivalently k_j/Cchild_j>r_s.                           (7.2)

This condition holds for EVERY j>=2 with j+1<=m and EVERY m>=1536.
To prove it first use the exact mass solution

 Cchild_j=(1-v)2^j+v*c^j, v=a/(c-2), 0<v<1.             (7.3)

The recurrence Cchild_(j+1)=2Cchild_j+a*c^j and v(c-2)=a prove the formula by
substitution, with Cchild0=1. The inequality0<v<1 follows from
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta.
Thus k_j/Cchild_j=a/[v+(1-v)(2/c)^j] is increasing in j.
At j=2, Cchild2=5-epsilon+2r_s*(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and
k2=beta*c=lambda*(1-epsilon^2). Since lambda=5r_s+2r_s^2,

 k2-r_s*Cchild2
   =epsilon*[r_s+4r_s^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda*epsilon]>0. (7.4)

For the final sign, epsilon<1/8, r_s>2/5 and lambda<5/2 imply
lambda*epsilon<5/16<2/5<r_s. This proves (7.2).
The counterexample begins with the literal H3 whose two children are H2.
It continues at every greater parent depth, not merely on a synthetic bridge.

This refutes the general-body terminal shifted-norm strengthening and any
universal proof of it using only valid projection normals. It does NOT
refute the requested U law. It also does NOT refute its centered-triangle
specialization: r_j<c-2<1/2, whereas the centered triangle's opposite
axial boundary lies at-1/2. The required negative cap is not a triangle cap.
This is a specific place where the triangle's fixed geometry matters.

### 7.1 The same complete two-cap family actually PASSES the U test

More generally keep x0=Cchild_j, y=1, z=-k_j and take a fixed interval[-r,1]
with0<r<=k_j/Cchild_j. The same complete-child guard proof gives terminal
 t=-k_j+S_j*r<=-r.
For any d>=1 its scaled initial maximizer is in the interval interior,
so U_(d+2Sparent)(x0)=Cchild_j^2/(d+2Sparent), where
Sparent=2S_j+1+k_j. Put S=S_j,k=k_j for this paragraph.
The exact terminal formula is

 U_d(t)=2kr-(2S+d)r^2 if r<=k/(S+d),
 U_d(t)=(k-Sr)^2/d if r>=k/(S+d),                    (7.5)

restricted throughout to0<r<=k/(S+1). The branches agree at their boundary.
The first is maximized at r=k/(2S+d), which is admissible because S+d>=1.
On the second branch k-Sr is positive and decreasing, so the maximum there
is at its left endpoint; its value is k^2*d/(S+d)^2<=k^2/(2S+d).
Consequently the exact family maximum is

 max_r U_d(t)=k^2/(2S+d),
 max_r U_d(t)/U_(d+2Sparent)(x0)
   =(k/(S+1))^2*(d+4S+2+2k)/(d+2S)<3/4.           (7.6)

For the last bound, (7.3) gives k/(S+1)<=c-2<1/2. Indeed
lambda<49/20 and epsilon<=1/6144 imply c<5/2. Since S>=S1>1,
k<(S+1)/2<=S. Thus d+4S+2+2k<=d+6S+2<=3(d+2S), using d>=1.
This comparison holds for EVERY j>=1 and every d>=1. For each selected r
there is one fixed body throughout the run; maximizing across that family
in (7.6) does not splice different projections into one history.
The failing strengthening is quantitatively separated from the U target.

The d=1 maximizing cap is the accepted single-cut asymmetric-Huber
construction in fact b98bb807d514e80d, rescaled from initial radius1 to
Cchild_j. That existing construction is not claimed as new. The additional
claims here concern the different endpoint-cap choice r=k/Cchild_j that refutes
(5.3), its exact same-m all-depth threshold, and the all-d U comparison
over the full legal cap interval. Their proofs above retain the actual
queries explicitly for this distinct diagnostic purpose.

## 8. Quantitative ALL-DEPTH payment on the centered triangle's unit ball

Here D=D_triangle. It contains the ball of radius1/2 and is contained in
the unit ball. For any h>=4/3 and any ||x||<=1,

 ||T_h(x)||<=(h-1)||x||.                             (8.1)

For h>=2 this follows for any D containing0 from
<x,P_D(x)>>=||P_D(x)||^2 and ||P_D(x)||<=||x||:
||T_h x||^2<=||x||^2+h(h-2)||P_D(x)||^2<=(h-1)^2||x||^2.
For4/3<=h<2, a core input gives equality. A vertex projection with ||x||<=1
can occur only at that vertex: the two vertex guards force its longitudinal
coordinate to be at least1. At that point equality holds as well.
For an edge projection write x=z*n+y*t where n is its outward unit normal,
t its unit tangent, z>=1/2, and P_D(x)=n/2+y*t. Here1/2<=z<=1.
With k=h-1, direct subtraction factors as

 ||T_h x||^2-k^2||x||^2
  =h(2-h)(z-1/2)*(z-h/[2(2-h)])<=0,

because h/[2(2-h)]>=1 when h>=4/3. This proves (8.1) on every cell.

All physical h_i are greater than4/3: a>1/3 follows from lambda<5/2 and
epsilon<1/11; the smallest step is1+a. Also a<r_s<1/2.
For a prefix of length ell>=1 in ANY same-m H_j, its exact product is

 p_ell=product_(t<ell)(h_t-1)=beta^ell*c^(-sbit(ell))<=a, (8.2)

where sbit(ell) is the number of1s in the binary expansion of ell.
The ruler step has h_t-1=beta*c^(nu2(t+1)-1), where nu2(k) is the exponent
of2 dividing the positive integer k. Counting multiples of powers of2 gives
sum_(k=1)^ell nu2(k)=ell-sbit(ell), proving (8.2) symbolically.
No trajectory or recurrence has been executed to obtain this identity.

Starting with ANY ||x0||<=1, apply (8.1) inductively. Every nonempty prefix
satisfies ||x_ell||<=p_ell||x0||<=a<1/2. Thus every query AFTER the first
is in the quadratic core, including all bridge inputs and outputs.
At a complete H_j endpoint, put p_jfull=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j). Then

 ||x_end||<=p_jfull||x0||,
 x_end equals the signed core multiplier after the first step times T_A x0.

In particular this covers initial points of the unit ball outside D itself.
The initial and terminal scaled points for U_(d+2S_j) and U_d are both core
when j>=1,d>=1. Formula (7.3) gives Cchild_j<=c^j and hence
Cchild_j*p_jfull<=beta^(2^j-1). Therefore, for every d>=1,

 U_(d+2S_j)(x0)-U_d(x_end)
 >=[1-beta^(2(2^j-1))]*||x0||^2/(d+2S_j).           (8.3)

The stronger shifted-norm reserve also obeys

 R_Hj(x0)>= [1-beta^(2(2^j-1))]||x0||^2.            (8.4)

At the endpoint P_D(x_end)=x_end, so its shifted norm is Cchild_j||x_end||.
These are uniform all-depth actual-block payments on the stated initial
unit ball. They are not arbitrary-start payments. They explain exactly

## 9. The actual H3 strict-switch calibration also passes the strengthening

We rederive this separately, without importing the previous unverified

u,v>=0 be the adjacent gaps of the zero-sum labeled coordinates
q_i=(2/3)<x,v_i>. Their true squared norm is

(1/6+u/2,1/6-u/2,-1/3); on a vertex they are(2/3,-1/3,-1/3).

Put K=beta*c, t0=(K+2)/4 and initialize

 u0=S2+K/2, v0=t0.

 7/5<A<17/12, 99/100<beta<1,

also

 (a*(beta*r-w0),a*w0),

 ||x7+S3 P_D(x7)||^2
  =(1+S3)^2||x7||^2<(29/2)^2/36<6<(6/25)||x0||^2.

 U_d(x7)<(6/25)U_(d+2S3)(x0).                       (9.1)

U law survives these counterexamples with a fixed margin.

For the centered triangle the norm strengthening is neither proved nor
refuted on arbitrary starts. The general-body interval counterexample
cannot be inserted into its different projection geometry. Conversely,
passing the unit ball and strict-switch families does not imply a general
triangle result. There is no permissible inference that a large initial
radius fixes all physical faces.

The exact open payment, already nontrivial at H2, is (4.3) against its
actual correlated leaf and normal reserves. At greater depths, the
retained signed sum (3.2) must be estimated through both child histories
and genuinely nonlocal normals without replacing the actual initial and
terminal orientations by independent maxima. The new identity (6.1) says
why one particularly natural stronger induction is unavailable for general
bodies; it supplies no substitute all-depth estimate.

## Fixed finite-law geometry, with original radius

On a finite space with positive fixed probabilities p_i, work in E={X:E X=0} with norm squared E X^2. The isometry X_i->sqrt(p_i)X_i makes this an ordinary finite-dimensional Euclidean problem. Let K={g:E g=0,g_i>=-1}; it is a compact centered simplex with0 in its relative interior. Its projection envelope F_K(X)=max_(g in K)(E[Xg]-E[g^2]/2) is globally convex1-smooth with unique minimum0.

The actual projection and physical update are

    g_i=(X_i-t)_+-1, E(X-t)_+=1,
    X_i'=X_i-h*g_i, E X'=0,
    F_K(X)=t+1/2+E(X-t)_+^2/2.

The threshold is unique and t>=-1. Original radius squared is E X0^2. Probability is only coordinate bookkeeping: this is ONE deterministic finite-dimensional function and trajectory, not an averaged performance claim over changing functions. Atom weights, function and initial values are fixed before the run. Backward atom splitting duplicates identical current/future values and therefore preserves every already-built future threshold and objective. All old/cross/terminal rows then come from the same projection body.

This complete nonvertex evolution is broader than the earlier one-vertex-per-phase constructions. No universality of this narrower simplex family for arbitrary functions is assumed.

A sup-norm transfer from the exactly folded continuous uniform law proves that its first actual output lies in[-57/64,57/64]. Every subsequent prefix remains in the quadratic core. With P_m=beta^(2^m-1)c^(-m), the law's payoff is<9P_m^2 and D_m times its payoff tends to0. This is a result for this specific finite law, not every law or every nonvertex trajectory.

An independent inverse-branch policy starting from terminal+/-2 is also completely specified. At each reversed h>1, d=h-1 and q=maxY, take a_*=q+d and omega=d/(q+d), independent of Y. It gives a finite fixed law with multiple active atoms at every preterminal time, but its original variance is too large. If current support is[-L,q],

## Constructive inverse laws and exact one-step optimization

For any finite mean-zero next law Y and h>1, d=h-1, choose a_*>=max(d,maxY), Z=a_*-Y, and conditional right probability omega(Y) satisfying E[omega Z]=d. The two actual preimages are

    X_left=Y-h, X_right=a_*-h+Z/d.

They have mean0 and physical projection threshold a_*-h. Both branches map exactly toY under the original h. Their original variance is

    V(X)=V(Y)+2h a_*-h^2+(d^-2-1)J,
    J=E[omega Z^2].

At fixed a_*, minimizing this variance is fractional knapsack: fill highest Y for1<h<2 and lowestY for h>2. At most one atom needs a fractional split. The threshold optimization is also exact. On an interval with fractional cutoff z and fully selected setA,

    J'(a_*)=d+E[1_A(z-Y)].

For h>=2 the optimized variance increases strictly with a_*, so its minimum is at max(d,maxY). For1<h<2 the value is convex piecewise affine, with slope

    h^2/d-(d^-2-1)E(Y-z)_+.

Its minimum is at the lower endpoint or a feasible upper-tail breakpointa_*=(d+E[Y1_A])/P(A). This identifies one-step useful thresholds without a solver. Equal current variance does NOT make resulting tail laws interchangeable for earlier physical steps.

The inverse is also explicit for every remaining nonnegative step. For0<h<1, choose a_* byE(Y-a_*)_+=1-h and use X=Y-h+[h/(1-h)](Y-a_*)_+. Forh1, with a_*=maxY and p=P(Y=a_*), take X=Y-1 offmax and X=a_*-1+1/p onmax. Forh0, useidentity. Thus a definite greedy reverse rule can use at mostn+2 atoms, all fixed before execution. Existence of that law is not the required performance estimate.

## A genuine link to the Zhang--Jiang retained potential

For1<h<2, set d=h-1 and gamma=dh/(2-h). Let z be the actual cutoff E(Y-z)_+=gamma, and select the upper tail abovez with total massp, optionally using boundary mass atz. Set a_*=z+(d+gamma)/p. Its EXACT real support guard is

All left gradients equal1; the bridge puts the right start at1+a; its first leaf hits0, and all subsequent steps stay0. Thenq0, r=(1+a)/D>0, RR0, but E_R=D||q-r||^2>0. Thus no finite multiple ofRR controls||q-r||^2. This is NOT a U counterexample and does not exclude using the full right gap. No all-depth full-class upper bound was obtained.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000390 -->
# R9 parent note: terminal scale and the correct multi-step inverse cost

2026-10-09. **UNVERIFIED manual research note. Original target OPEN.** No mathematical program, numerical array, solver, added model or formal submission was used. This note supplements the completed R9 finite-law report without changing its final bytes or hashes.

## 1. A fixed terminal law at plus/minus 2 cannot support a standalone all-word theorem

The R9 report specifies a greedy inverse candidate starting from the mean-zero terminal law Y=+2 or -2 with equal aggregate probabilities. It correctly leaves its original-variance estimate unproved. The following calculation shows that a standalone estimate V0<=3B_n/(1-o(1)) for that fixed terminal law is FALSE on unit-step words. This does not refute its inherited-H_m performance, a portfolio including standard Huber witnesses, or the general inverse construction with a different terminal law.

For any convex1-smooth function with minimizer x_star, unit-step GD obeys

\[
 \|x_i-x_\star\|^2-\|x_{i+1}-x_\star\|^2
 =2\langle g_i,x_i-x_\star\rangle-\|g_i\|^2
 \ge2(f_i-f_\star).
\]

The smooth-convex interpolation inequality at x_i,x_(i+1), together with x_i-x_(i+1)=g_i, gives

\[
 f_i-f_{i+1}\ge
 \langle g_{i+1},g_i\rangle+\tfrac12\|g_i-g_{i+1}\|^2
 =\tfrac12(\|g_i\|^2+\|g_{i+1}\|^2).
\]

Cocoercivity also gives

\[
 \|g_i-g_{i+1}\|^2\le\langle g_i-g_{i+1},g_i\rangle,
 \qquad\|g_{i+1}\|^2\le\|g_i\|^2.
\]

Consequently f_i-f_n>=(n-i)||g_n||^2. Summing the distance inequalities and then these value inequalities proves the universal terminal bound

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \|x_0-x_\star\|^2\ge
 \|x_n-x_\star\|^2+2n(f_n-f_\star)+n(n+1)\|g_n\|^2.
 \quad}                                                     \tag{1}
\]

For the fixed probability-simplex terminal law Y=+/-2, the unique minimum is0, ||Y||^2=4, its projection is g_Y=+/-1, and F_K(Y)=3/2. Refining atoms with identical terminal values changes none of these quantities. Thus EVERY admissible preimage trajectory with that same terminal law, not merely the greedy one, satisfies

\[
 V_0\ge4+3n+n(n+1)=(n+2)^2.
\]

Equality is achieved by the two-atom noncrossing Huber trajectory starting at +/-(n+2), taking n unit steps and ending at +/-2. Its exact best normalized payoff under the fixed terminal requirement is therefore

\[
 \frac3{(n+2)^2}.
\]

Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n<=(12/5)(n+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2)<2. Hence

\[
 B_n\frac3{(n+2)^2}
 \le\frac{36}{5}\frac{(n+1)^\rho}{(n+2)^2}\longrightarrow0.
\]

The fixed-terminal standalone estimate should therefore be marked **REFUTED on unit-step words**, rather than left as a conjectural all-word bound. Standard Huber already handles those words, so this does not reject a combined adversarial portfolio on words where that baseline is deficient.

## 2. A benchmark-scaled terminal law covers the entire low-mass case exactly

For a horizon n define

\[
 Q_n=(B_n+1)/2\ge1,
 \qquad \bar S_n=(B_n-1)/2=S_\bullet(n).
\]

Use the terminal law +/-Q_n with equal probabilities in the same two-atom projection simplex. Its exact terminal twice-value is B_n. For ANY prescribed nonnegative word of total mass S, the actual initial law +/-(Q_n+S) stays on its two affine Huber rays at every physical query and ends at +/-Q_n. The original variance is (Q_n+S)^2. If S<=barS_n, then

\[
 (Q_n+S)^2\le B_n^2,
 \qquad W_n(h)\ge\frac{B_n}{(Q_n+S)^2}\ge\frac1{B_n}.
\tag{2}
\]

All steps, including zero steps, are retained; no radius or minimizer is reset. Thus this choice of terminal scale covers the entire low-total-mass case. A natural sufficient target for a further inverse construction on the remaining words is original variance at most (1+o(1))*B_n^2 while preserving terminal twice-value B_n. No such high-mass estimate is proved here, and the two-point terminal shape is not assumed sufficient for every word. It may be changed if a genuine construction needs more terminal geometry.

## 3. One-step inverse optimization must account for the preceding prefix cost

This is a conditional attained identity within the same finite-law construction. It is a possible starting point for a further direct proof, not a composition theorem.

For a finite mean-zero next law Y, h>1, d=h-1, choose a_*>=max(d,maxY), t=a_*-h, Z=a_*-Y, and a conditional right probability omega satisfying E[omega Z]=d. The actual inverse values are X_left=t-Z and X_right=t+Z/d. Let J=E[omega Z^2]. Then

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 V(X)&=V(Y)+2h a_*-h^2+(d^{-2}-1)J,\\
 F_K(X)&=a_*-h+\tfrac12+J/(2d^2),\\
 \|P_KX\|^2&=J/d^2-1.
 \end{aligned}                                               \tag{3}
\]

Fix local nonnegative cost coefficients A,B, distinct from the benchmark B_n, and define

\[
 \mathcal C_{A,B}(X)=V(X)+2A F_K(X)+B\|P_KX\|^2.
\]

Substitution into (3) gives the EXACT full cost

\[
 \mathcal C_{A,B}(X)
 =V(Y)+2(h+A)a_*-h^2-2Ah+A-B
       +\left(\frac{1+A+B}{d^2}-1\right)J.
\tag{4}
\]

In particular, minimizing only V(X) at the latest reverse step can choose the wrong branch for the cost imposed by earlier physical steps. The sign deciding which tail to allocate is now 1+A+B-d^2, not merely 1-d^2.

Suppose

\[
 C_0=1+A+B>d^2,\quad T=h+A,\quad w=C_0/d^2-1>0,
 \quad\gamma=d+2T/w.
\]

Choose the unique z with E(Y-z)_+=gamma, select all upper-tail atoms above z and optionally some boundary mass at z, and let p be the total selected mass. The actual threshold parameter is

\[
 a_*=z+(d+\gamma)/p.
\]

Its support guard is p(maxY-z)<=d+gamma. Mean zero also gives z>=-gamma, so a_*>=d. When that REAL guard holds, the construction is admissible and

\[
 J=(d-\gamma)(a_*-z)+\mathbb E(Y-z)_+^2.
\]

The coefficient of a_* in (4) then cancels. This is the slope-zero condition for the corresponding upper-tail fractional-knapsack optimization; if a boundary atom supplies an interval of equally minimizing thresholds, its mass may affect earlier-step costs even when (4) is unchanged.

With U_gamma(Y)=2gamma F_K(Y/gamma), the attained cost is

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \mathcal C_{A,B}(X)
 =V(Y)+T U_\gamma(Y)+B_\gamma\|P_K(Y/\gamma)\|^2,
 \quad B_\gamma=w\gamma^2-T\gamma=\gamma(wd+T)>0.
 \quad}                                                       \tag{5}
\]

To verify the constant term, E(Y-z)_+^2=gamma^2(||P_K(Y/gamma)||^2+1), and w gamma=wd+2T. The leftover constant in (4) is

    -h^2-2Ah+A-B + w*gamma*d = 0,

because w*gamma*d=C_0-d^2+2Td and h=d+1. This proves (5) by exact algebra.

For A=B=0 and 1<h<2, gamma=h(h-1)/(2-h), B_gamma=h^2/(2-h), recovering the R9 native identity. At h=sqrt2 it reduces to

    V(X)=V(Y)+2sqrt2 F_K(Y)+(2+sqrt2)||P_KY||^2

under the corresponding real support guard. This is the constructive connection to the primitive terminal potential.

The evaluations at Y/gamma in (5) are auxiliary evaluations of the SAME function on the SAME refined finite probability space. They are not permission to run the remaining physical word from Y/gamma, change its steps, or replace its body. The resulting auxiliary-threshold cost is not automatically closed under another application of (4). Propagating the actual tail guards, boundary masses, and such cost data through the full word remains unproved.

### The primitive concatenation root appears in this attained cost algebra

Take the local cost coefficients A=C_L-1 and B=(C_L-1)C_L, so C_0=C_L^2. For a physical bridge h=1+d with 0<d<C_L, formula (5) gives

\[
 T=C_L+d,\qquad
 \gamma=\frac{d(C_L+d)}{C_L-d},\qquad
 B_\gamma=\frac{C_L(C_L+d)^2}{C_L-d}.
\]

If d is the Zhang--Jiang primitive joining root

\[
 d(C_L+C_R+d)=C_L C_R,
\]

then EXACTLY

\[
 \gamma=C_R,\qquad
 B_\gamma=(C_L+d)(C_L+C_R+d).
\]

Indeed the root equation is d(C_L+d)=C_R(C_L-d). Substitution proves both identities. For balanced capacities C_L=C_R=C, d=(sqrt2-1)C; the support guard in (5) becomes p(maxY-z)/C<=sqrt2 at the actual stop-loss level C. Thus the primitive joining root, not merely its leaf value, appears in the conditional actual inverse-cost calculation.

This identifies the scalar coefficients under a REAL tail guard. It does not prove that an earlier arbitrary prefix has cost exactly C_(A,B) for every endpoint law, or that the same law remains attainable through the two complete children. Those joint attainability and concentration requirements are still unproved. The displayed root identity is not an all-word converse to the Zhang--Jiang theorem.

## 4. Research judgment

The general inverse construction is still a concrete finite-dimensional mechanism: backward atom splitting fixes one law and one function before the forward run. The weak fixed terminal scale and local-variance-only choices should not be retained as universal sufficient policies. The next direct attempt should choose terminal scale/shape from the exact benchmark and control the entire inverse cost, with actual feasible tail choices, rather than merely rediscover the one-step formulas.

No high-mass all-word estimate, inherited-H_m target-scale witness, or original-target proof is claimed. All new statements in this note remain UNVERIFIED, and no formal review has been requested.

# Existing shared mathematical Python runtime

This launcher invokes only the already-provisioned Python executable:

```
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/workers/max4/.venv-pep/bin/python
```

Run a caller-owned script, retaining the caller's working directory:

copy scripts, install packages, start services, or edit configuration. Only
the software runtime is shared; private worker directories are not a data
interface. Use your own scripts and output paths. Existing discovery budgets

# Cyclic interpolation tools for the legal-trajectory lane

Main-authored source triage. Optional support, not a new fact, instruction to
solve an LP, or replacement of the current tasks. Checked September27,2026.

## Strict finite matching and smoothing

del Barrio, Cuesta-Albertos, Hallin and Matran,
[Smooth Cyclically Monotone Interpolation, explicit v1](https://arxiv.org/html/1806.01238v1).
Proposition2.1 relates finite cyclic monotonicity to supporting weights; its
strict version corresponds to uniqueness of the matching. Theorem2.2 and
Corollary2.4 retain Assumption(A). For bounded output vectors, the latter
constructs an interpolating Moreau envelope with gradient Lipschitz bound
1/epsilon_0, where epsilon_0 is half the minimum strict supporting gap.
Equations2.11-2.12 formulate gap selection and a circulation dual.

Potential use: test whether a concrete proposed legal repair has a uniform
supporting-gap bound, rather than fixing one violated pair. This is only a
possible construction tool. Relabeling gradients by an optimal matching
does not preserve a prescribed GD trajectory automatically. A small gap may
cause an unacceptable smoothness/normalization loss. The optimum jet, actual
terminal value and true initial distance still need explicit checks. Use the
strict stated theorem with its hypotheses; no unqualified extension statement
is imported here, and no minimum-cycle algorithm or optimal Lipschitz theorem
is asserted beyond the displayed source statements.

## Value-free interpolation and the metric it actually bounds

Lee and Seiler,
[Finite Step Performance of First-order Methods Using Interpolation Conditions Without Function Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2005.01825).
Lemma4 states the transformed cyclic-monotonicity criterion for the stated
strongly convex regime0<mu<L. Lemma8 identifies the cone generated by I-P,
with P a permutation matrix, with zero-excess doubly hyperdominant matrices.
Theorem1's displayed performance metric is iterate distance, not last-iterate
objective gap; the paper's trajectory reconstruction also has explicit
feasibility/nondegeneracy conditions.

Possible use is a combinatorial organization of ALL cycle constraints, not
a free import of its performance theorem. For the present convex objective
problem, derive the exact needed specialization from accepted1252b8823bb5a290
and keep function-value completion. An exact value-free reformulation alone
is already known and is not a target-specific lower bound. No finite distance
bound, strongly convex rate or lossless-S-procedure slogan closes this goal.

## Retrieval boundary

The old ULB bitstream redirected to INVALID_ID. The arXiv v1 PDF retrieval
returned Internal Error; the explicit v1 HTML and abstract worked and were
read. Searching that HTML for Karp returned no matching text. The note uses
the displayed strict-gap/circulation statements, not an unobserved named
algorithm. Lee-Seiler's full PDF was available. No software was installed.

# Directed graph tools: exact matching still required

Main-authored optional source triage, September27,2026. No theorem import,
new assignment, solver allowance or accepted fact.

Yibin Zhao, *Fully Dynamic Spectral and Cut Sparsifiers for Directed Graphs*,
[ICALP2026 paper](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/storage/00lipics/lipics-vol374-icalp2026/html/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.157/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.157.html):
Definition2 controls the directed bilinear error relative to the associated
undirected Laplacian. Lemma10 identifies preservation of each vertex's degree
imbalance. Theorem3 allows auxiliary vertices, with guarantees after their
Schur elimination, and assumes polynomially bounded weights. The balanced-cut
results apply to cuts satisfying their specified balance condition.

Potential connection for max/max4, still UNMATCHED: actual interpolation
coefficients form a directed array and value cancellation imposes flows.
Test whether any exact flow-preserving operation also controls the physical
chronological moment terms in the FULL objective remainder. Degree balance
alone is not that conclusion. Auxiliary graph vertices do not automatically
represent legal extra queries; arbitrary multiplier weights do not meet a
polynomial-bound hypothesis automatically. An approximation theorem needs
an explicit error budget below the original constant threshold, including
all repeated levels and singular kernels. Do not remove queried constraints
under the label of graph elimination.

Cohen et al., *Solving Directed Laplacian Systems in Nearly-Linear Time
through Sparse LU Factorizations*,
[full paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1811.10722): Section4 explicitly notes that
the symmetric part of a directed Schur complement can grow relative to the
original. Lemma4.3 gives a conditional general-matrix bound; Lemma4.2 supplies
a bounded-elimination statement for the paper's alpha-RCDD subsets of an
Eulerian Laplacian. This is not a general monotonicity principle for arbitrary
certificate matrices or arbitrary eliminated blocks.

Peng and Song, *Sparsified Block Elimination for Directed Laplacians*,
[full paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2111.10257): Theorem3.3 concerns strongly
connected Eulerian Laplacians with an alpha-RCDD block. Section4 uses augmented
matrices to encode and bound errors of repeated block elimination. A possible
method analogy is explicit storage of errors across recursion, but neither
its Laplacian hypotheses nor its algorithmic approximation guarantee has
been matched to the present GD remainder. No claim of constant preservation
or full-certificate positivity follows merely from the analogy.

Disposition: keep as a source lead if a concrete coefficient/cut identity
exposes the required structure. Continue the existing exact calculations
first; a generic Laplacian reformulation with the hard PSD inequality still
unproved is a blocked route, not a solved bridge.

# Discrete Hardy tools for the chronological-kernel interface

Read-only primary-source search by main, 2026-09-27. This note is contextual
strategy, not an accepted Danus fact, a matched theorem, or a proof.

1. Laurent Miclo, *An example of application of discrete Hardy's inequalities*,
[author PDF](https://perso.math.univ-toulouse.fr/miclo/files/2012/04/Hardy.pdf).
Proposition1 on PDF page3 concerns positive sequence weights mu,nu and the
best squared-norm constant A for weighted prefix sums. Its tail-prefix
quantity B satisfies B<=A<=4B. The paper applies such estimates to spectral
gaps of birth-and-death processes. These are quantitatively explicit but
factor-loss bounds. They are not sharp constant-one domination for our
time-inhomogeneous kernel. A claimed application must exhibit the actual
weights, boundary conditions and transformation, preserving terminal clock,
constant column and all signed remainder terms. The presence of a birth
representation alone is not enough. Total positivity likewise does not
remove the loss or prove covariance domination.

2. Ali Barki, *Sharp Weighted Discrete p-Hardy Inequality and Stability*,
[arXiv2501.00299v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2501.00299v1).
Section2 treats two positive measures. Proposition2.1 bounds the best Hardy
constant between B and p^p/(p-1)^(p-1) times B. Proposition2.3 separately treats
the zero-sum sequence class; Remark2.2 explains why this distinction matters.
Section3's Theorem3.3 builds an optimal graph Hardy weight from a positive
superharmonic function, assuming a connected graph, finitely supported
nonnegative potential, properness and bounded neighbor oscillation.
This is a possible source of an exact supersolution method, not a theorem
already matched to our finite, horizon-dependent, signed birth-coordinate
matrix. Any use needs an explicit finite specialization and boundary proof;
neither graph positivity nor bounded oscillation is automatic here.

Current worker-local-square context is publicly mirrored in
shared_evidence/3d57065c9de5e34b.md, with boundary extension d6e2788596a23c4f.
The actual unanswered question is uniform bulk/right-boundary coefficient
signs and the full alternating constant load, for every original m>=128.
Simple diagonal dominance already fails on the displayed row2 limit. The
first vanishing coefficient r1 scales as1/m, so a fixed positive lower bound
from m128 is false. A Hardy comparison may be useful only if its quantified
loss fits an actual available margin, or an exact supersolution replaces the
lossy comparison. It must not be called constant preserving by analogy.

Search boundary: no posted solution to the exact user benchmark was sought.
The additional BNU-hosted paper81.pdf returned Internal Error on opening and
is NOT relied on. No optimizer, kernel evaluation, installation or verifier
call was made. This note does not renew any resource allocation.

# Primary source leads for nonlocal original-row transport

2026-10-02. Main source retrieval and scope record, not new mathematics.
Original exact-profile Goal ACTIVE/OPEN. Sol/max. All existing worker
allocations and the consumed ONE formal attempt remain unchanged.

## Directed degree-preserving elimination

Cohen, Kelner, Kyng, Peebles, Peng, Rao and Sidford,
Solving Directed Laplacian Systems in Nearly-Linear Time through Sparse
LU Factorizations, arXiv:1811.10722v1.
Primary full paper: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1811.10722 .

Read the introduction's elimination discussion, Section3 Algorithm4,
Section4 Definition4.1 and Lemmas4.2--4.4. For Eulerian directed
Laplacians, eliminating a vertex creates an incoming-to-outgoing biclique.
Their nonnegative equal-mass coupling preserves weighted degrees while
matching that dense object in expectation. Their analysis also identifies
Schur-complement growth; arbitrary elimination is not protected by a
uniform constant, and controlled sets/norms are needed. The main output
is an approximate LU/linear-system guarantee, not an exact PSD
certificate for an arbitrary signed symmetric matrix.

## Chordal positive/semidefinite completion

Grone, Johnson, Sa and Wolkowicz, Positive Definite Completions of Partial
Hermitian Matrices, Linear Algebra and its Applications58 (1984),109--124.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/0024-3795(84)90207-6 .
Author-hosted original paper:
https://www.math.uwaterloo.ca/~hwolkowi/henry/reports/pdcomplGJSW84.pdf .

Read Section2 definitions, Proposition2, Section6 Theorem7 and Section7.
The theorem concerns partially specified Hermitian matrices: compatible
positive data on specified cliques admit completion for chordal patterns.
The paper also treats the semidefinite case. Unspecified entries are
free completion variables. This does not assert positivity of an already
fully specified matrix, or realizability of a completion by a prescribed
nonnegative multiplier/flow map.

## Missing project interfaces, inference only

These are possible organizing tools for NEXT planning; no imported
Danus fact or successful law follows. A worker would still need an
explicit mapping to every original interpolation row, correct value
flow, nonnegative signs, attainable free entries, all initial mixed
coordinates, and a paid whole-word constant. No such mapping is supplied
by this main-agent note. Random averaging of illegal primal trajectories
or graph completion outside the actual multiplier cone remains invalid.

Current max75 has at most3 explicit laws and may use source orientation
within that allocation; this note does not authorize a fourth law or
extra invocation. max3r67/max4r70 remain unchanged, as does max2r64's
SAME in-flight formal call (used1/limit1, remaining0). All mathematical
program/numerical/optimizer/DP/replay allowances remain zero.

## Retrieval record

Two standard-theorem web queries, primary-source opens and exact section
lookups were used. The first Wisconsin PDF endpoint and a2008 publisher
open returned Internal Error. The1984 original PDF was recovered through
the author's verified publications-page link. The2008 text was not
imported. No posted solution to the exact benchmark was searched.
No new theorem/proof, formal call, model-backed auxiliary, mathematical
evaluation, publication, finalization, revocation or shutdown occurred.

# Source methods revisited, 2026-10-08

These are primary-source method leads, not Danus facts or imported proofs of the target. No posted solution to the exact benchmark was sought.

## Dense interpolation multipliers

[Chikhi, arXiv2609.13765v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v2), Section4, constructs nonnegative interpolation multipliers from two monotone scalar sequences for a specially balanced constant schedule. The source also distinguishes terminal smooth-gradient performance from the proximal objective evaluated at a different point. It was already inspected on October1; there is no literature-newness claim.

The new root work allowed varying steps and nonnegative diagonal slack, and derived a sufficient17/16 terminal penalty. The independently checked extension is in dense_history_search_20261008_r2/REPORT.md. Its entire specified monotone A/D family fails the actual hierarchy's central bridge. Thus merely varying those two scalar sequences cannot supply the desired full-class upper. Non-diagonal remainders remain outside the exclusion.

## Laplacian sum-of-squares structure

[Bok and Altschuler, arXiv2412.05497](https://arxiv.org/html/2412.05497), Section3.2 and Lemma14, prove positivity of a recursive slack matrix using a Laplacian and its Schur complement. This is a method lead for retaining structured cross terms.

The source analyzes proximal gradient updates and the original Silver schedule. This project's projection-gradient dynamics and relaxed proximal interpretation do not automatically match those updates. No theorem or coefficient table was transplanted. Any future use needs an exact update/criterion translation and a new identity for the altered same-m word.

Retrieval notes: the guessed v3 HTML path returned404; the unversioned arXiv HTML was available. One optional web request failed at transport level and the PMLR PDF attempt returned an internal error; no unavailable file was treated as read. The relevant arXiv Section3.2 was later read successfully.

# Phase-sensitive seed amplification: source and scope check

Contextual source screen,2026-09-27; not an accepted Danus theorem.

[Liu,Chen,Jiang,Wang,arXiv2609.11788v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1)
uses leaf count N. Proposition2.5 identifies U_N with the symmetric
recursive primitive scalar. Section6 reciprocates OBS-F rates into W_N
with W_1=1 and join A(u,w)=(4u+w+sqrt(w*w+8u*w))/2. Theorem6.3 gives a
positive Lipschitz periodic asymptotic profile for N^p times the OBS-F rate,
uniform over dyadic blocks. Theorem6.5 states nonconstancy and strict
separation from its homogeneous support barrier. Remark1.2 explicitly
limits optimality to recursively generated classes. Application here
requires matching U_N=P_(N-1),source W_N=B_(N-1),p=our rho, normalization,
and every index shift. Neither that paper's theorem names nor its reported
formalization are treated as verifier acceptance by this project.

C72 d4ccd3fd9ce605f1 proves a DRAFT sufficient amplification threshold
A_seed>(r_seed+1)^rho by replacing primitive leaves and comparing through
one GLOBAL limsup. That criterion deliberately avoids a phase theorem.
It does not establish necessity of that threshold, or rule out useful
non-dyadic seeds below the power envelope. Main's new proposed question is
whether an exact phase-resolved comparison gives a strictly weaker usable
threshold. This is an inference and research request, not a derived claim.
It must retain the physical output length m_seed*n and the actual terminal
leaf loss from C72.

A useful answer needs a proved phase/DP matching, a legitimate input
subsequence, a quantitative strict margin and an actual full certificate.
An unevaluated infimum involving an unknown phase is not a found seed.
Nor is a small finite gain above P_r automatically enough.

[Chikhi,arXiv2609.13765v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v1)
was also screened. Its Theorem1's exact objective is F(z_N), where
z_N=prox_(lambda F)(x_N), in relaxed proximal point. The sharp eta/4 factor
there is not the original smooth f(x_N) objective coefficient. The paper's
smooth constant-step optimality statement concerns final gradient norm.
Neither result directly provides an improving schedule for this goal.
This is an applicability boundary, not a criticism of those results.

No new optimizer, phase scan, installation, verifier, or external publication
was made. The full-primitive44-evaluation batch is closed; its28 unused
evaluations and unused time have expired.

# Finite q-product identities: source pointers for the actual birth kernel

Context only; no Danus fact or matched theorem. Main read
[NIST DLMF17.2](https://dlmf.nist.gov/17.2) and17.5 on2026-09-27.
DLMF17.2.1 defines the finite q-Pochhammer product. Equations17.2.7--17.2.20
give finite product transformations, and17.2.35 gives the finite q-binomial
theorem. The series identity17.2.37 carries a convergence condition on z;
infinite-product conventions must also be checked. Finite polynomial
identities should be preferred when they suffice.

The published worker draft cefde310a2e5268e already has an explicit inverse
birth-coordinate formula using q_c=1/c and products
P_r=product_(s=1)^r(1-q_c^s), derived there by interpolation. This note
does NOT claim that NIST supplies the six missing coefficient inequalities.
A possible next mechanism is to use exact finite product/cancellation
identities to expose the actual row sums and signed tail terms before
estimating them. This is a research suggestion, not a derived result.

Required boundaries: retain the physical additive1 in d_j, the same
enclosing-m parameters, exact finite truncation (especially the penultimate
row), all alternating signs and the full constant load. Replacing every
signed term by an independent absolute bound may lose the needed margin.
Positivity of Gaussian binomial coefficients alone does not imply positivity
of the target quadratic form. Any infinite comparison needs uniform,
quantitative truncation bounds in BOTH row and horizon indices.

The six hypotheses in the terminal-charge package are still unproved.
Neither a source name nor a formal q-product rewriting is their proof.
No kernel replay, solver, installation, verifier or allocation change was
made by this source check.

measure and its hypotheses must not be substituted for the present arbitrary

# Primary-source scope refresh, 2026-09-30

This is a main-owned retrieval note, not a Danus fact or a proof review.
The search was bounded and does not establish absence of other literature.

## Lower bounds

Ye--Liu, [Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent,
v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2), was reopened on September30.
Theorem1.1 bounds every nonnegative finite-horizon word, but its displayed
exponent includes an additional positive term of order
sqrt(log log n/log n). Equation1 uses this project's W normalization.
Its stated result therefore does not supply the requested leading
constant or exact B_n profile. This is the same source already scoped
in SOURCE_NOTES.md, not a newly discovered target-resolution theorem.
Sections2 and3.1 identify checkpoint selection, changing directions and
combining local functions as the construction interfaces; AppendixB.3
retains compactness, zero coordinates and splitting at a tight checkpoint.
Any renewed route must overcome the actual quantitative loss with one
static function and true initial distance, rather than merely restating
the exponent theorem. This last sentence is a research requirement, not
a claimed mathematical improvement.

## Recursive benchmark

The primary abstract for Liu--Chen--Jiang--Wang,
[Optimal Recursive Composition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent
with Predetermined Stepsizes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11788), still
describes optimization inside recursive frameworks and log-periodic
profiles. Its scope supplies no automatic identification with arbitrary
predetermined schedules. The prior direct HTML scope check in
SOURCE_NOTES.md remains the relevant warning about ambient certificate
classes versus recursively generated classes.

No source was imported into the fact graph. No new model consult,
mathematical replay, formal verifier call or worker round was opened by
this retrieval. All project resource closures remain in force.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000410 -->
# Primary-source tools for the current structural routes

Checked September27,2026. Main-authored source triage, not a proof, imported
fact or external consult. These optional tools do not change the assignments,
launch limits or closed numerical/verification allowances.

## Symmetry and matrix algebra

Primary source: de Klerk, Pasechnik and Schrijver, *Reduction of symmetric
semidefinite programs using the regular star-representation* (2007),
[author-hosted paper](https://homepages.cwi.nl/~lex/files/symm.pdf).
Read Section2, Theorem1 and equations17-19. The regular representation of the
commutant is a star-isomorphism, preserving positive semidefiniteness. In the
displayed SDP application, a permutation group commutes with the objective
and constraint matrices; averaging therefore preserves feasibility and cost.

Possible use for max: test whether a proposed bulk tensor coefficient family
has an actual closed matrix algebra with a smaller faithful representation.
This is a research suggestion, not a verified match. Repeated step values or
a dyadic indexing set alone do not establish the source's invariance
hypotheses for chronological GD, its optimum edges, or its last objective.
A Walsh/Haar coordinate change alone supplies no new feasible array. Keep
actual coefficient nonnegativity separate from PSD of a transformed matrix.

## Chordal PSD versus polynomial certificates

Primary source: Zheng and Fantuzzi, *Sum-of-squares chordal decomposition of
polynomial matrix inequalities*,
[full paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2007.11410).
Theorem2.1 states the classical clique-supported PSD-sum characterization for
a constant matrix with chordal sparsity. Proposition2.1 shows that even an
everywhere positive definite polynomial matrix need not have a polynomial
clique decomposition of the naive same form. Theorem2.4 obtains weighted SOS
clique decompositions on an Archimedean compact semialgebraic domain under
strict positive definiteness.

Possible use for max/max3: an explicitly structured full remainder might
admit clique-sized storage/Schur certificates. First identify its ACTUAL
matrix sparsity and retained separator variables. A graph of interpolation
edges is not automatically the sparsity graph of the full quadratic matrix.
Neither pointwise constant-matrix decomposition nor an existence theorem
supplies a uniform-degree all-depth formula. Singular PSD boundaries and a
parameter tending to zero require their own proof; strict positivity cannot
be silently supplied by a numerical check. This is conditional source triage.

## Spectral sparsification has a load-bearing PSD-atom hypothesis

Primary source: de Carli Silva, Harvey and Sato, *Sparse Sums of Positive
Semidefinite Matrices*,
[paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1107.0088), Theorem3 and AppendixD.
For PSD summands B_i of size d, their sum B admits a nonnegative weighted
O(d/epsilon^2)-term approximation lying between B and (1+epsilon)B. The paper
explicitly says the assumption that the summands are PSD cannot be removed.

Possible use only after an actual positive-block decomposition exists.
C55's individual added-pair update is indefinite, so this theorem does not
justify sparsifying those updates as if they were PSD atoms. Exact value-flow
and interpolation-coefficient constraints also require an explicitly matched
argument; approximate spectral preservation alone does not preserve them or
the exact target constant. No randomized or deterministic solver is allocated.

## Arbitrary spectra do not substitute for gradient legality

Primary source: Greenbaum, Ptak and Strakos, *Any Nonincreasing Convergence
Curve is Possible for GMRES* (1996),
[publisher abstract](https://epubs.siam.org/doi/abs/10.1137/S0895479894275030).
The abstract permits a prescribed positive nonincreasing residual history
with arbitrarily specified eigenvalues for a constructed linear system.
Only the publisher statement was used here; no full proof was retrieved.

This is an analogy/caution for max2's temporal-span work, not a theorem about
GD. GMRES residual minimization and a general linear system do not establish
cyclic smooth-convex interpolation, gradient integrability, or the correct
objective/initial-distance normalization. The current legal finite example
c5a096d73740bf81 already demonstrates the relevant radius-only failure within
this project. No additional GMRES detour is requested absent a concrete
legality-preserving construction.

# Future-only original source three-entry cut

UNVERIFIED MAIN ANALYTICAL DERIVATION. No mathematical program, numerical replay or formal call. This note is not a new worker allocation and is not an accepted fact. Independently rederive it after FULL reading of max93's final report before using it to close any route.

For the current architecture retain h0=h2=b=1+a, h1=g=1+beta, Ltri as usual, P=K+v v^T+eta z z^T, N=Ltri^T(P+A), A skew, Ae=F(v-z), Ev+Fz=Pe, E=pi+s, F=pi-s, eta=F/E and q=pi^2. Assume alpha0=0, v0=bs/a, z0=bE/a, e^Tz=E, e^Tv=E, and Ke=0. These hold for the literal max93 common column; preserving z1 is not needed for this proposed cut.

The ORIGINAL first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e0^T. Consequently, for EVERY j,

 N0j=b*s*v_j+b*F*z_j-a*K0j-a*A0j,
 Pj0-A0j=2*Kj0+N0j/a.

The latter also holds at j=0: P00=K00+b^2*q/a^2 and N00=b^2*q/a-a*K00. Thus the COMPLETE source column satisfies the exact identity

 N_:0=2*Ltri^T*K_:0+(Ltri^T*N_0:^T)/a.

Since mu0=2bq, put r0=N00-mu0=(1/a-a)q-a*K00. At original row2, multiplication gives

 a*N20=2a*(Ltri^T K)_20+N02+b*sum_(j>2)N0j.

Using sum_(j>0)N0j=-r0 and b=1+a yields the THREE ORIGINAL ENTRY identity

 a*N20+b*N01+a*N02=2a*(Ltri^T K)_20-b*r0.          (S3)

All three weights are positive and all three entries are off diagonal. If the right side is positive, no compatible skew can make every original finite multiplier nonnegative. This is stronger than a failure of a sparse particular completion, but only for the specified source column of K and the specified common-source normalization.

For the currently frozen metric, the only source-reaching detail is d0=(g,-b-g,b,0,...). All remaining details, including the terminal-six Rtest block, have zero source coordinate. Let rho=rho_clock and Kclock=rho*(diag(h)-h h^T/S). Since K00=3q and omega0=(3q-Kclock00)/g^2,

 (Ltri^T K)_20
 =3bq/g-rho*b^2/g*(1-b/S)
          -rho*b^2*[1+(1-2b-g)/S]
 >3bq/g-rho*b^2*(1+1/g).

The established scalar enclosures for this architecture are a>2/5, a<1/2, b>7/5, b<3/2, 1<g<2, rho<125/S^2, q>1/(3S), S>2^20. With r0=(1/a-4a)q, the proposed bound from(S3) is

 2a*(Ltri^T K)_20-b*r0
 >b*(7a-1/a)*q-5rho
 >(21/50)q-625/S^2
 >(41/100)q > (2/5)q.

Here 7a-1/a increases for positive a and exceeds3/10 at a>2/5. Also 625/S^2<(1875/S)q<q/100 because S>2^20>187500. These comparisons are textual derivations, not executed arithmetic.

If this identity and bounds survive independent checking, they exclude the ENTIRE max93 fixed-metric completion, including arbitrary global skew, despite its repaired global prefix/clock tests. They do not exclude a changed source-reaching metric or changed common source coordinate. In particular adding only an omitted0-to4 skew coupling cannot repair a positive(S3) invariant. A source metric that touches earlier coordinates in a new way or a non-optimal z0 would need all moments, charge, source/clock conditions and the full PSD rederived; no such repair is constructed here.

Important provenance correction: the max93 TASK's suggested expanded Csrc numerator contained a sign typo. The literal definition uses p1*w1=+g*a^2/(1+s), as the worker reported, and the frozen common-column law itself was unchanged. This source-cut derivation uses only z0 and does not use either expansion of Csrc. Do not cite the erroneous minus-sign expansion in a later proof.

# Tree embedding as a possible reserve-payment tool

Primary-source method lookup, October8. Contextual lead only; no imported Danus fact, matched GD theorem, mathematical program or verification request.

Arcozzi, Holmes, Mozolyako and Volberg, [Bellman Function Sitting on a Tree](https://academic.oup.com/imrn/article/2021/16/12037/5610523), Section1.1, Theorem1.3, gives a Carleson embedding on a dyadic tree with an explicit factor4. In finite-tree notation, let mu_v>=0 and let T_v be the descendants of v including v. A rescaling of the stated condition is

    sum_(v in T_u) t_v [mu(T_v)]^2 <= C mu(T_u),  for every u,

with t_v>=0. The conclusion is

    sum_v t_v [sum_(w in T_v) mu_w y_w]^2 <= 4 C sum_w mu_w y_w^2.

The paper proves its tree inequality using the Bellman majorant4(F-f^2/(v+A)); it also distinguishes the tree setting from the usual martingale setting. The relevant displayed hypotheses and Section1 proof setup were read. This source is an existing theorem, not a newly established research result.

## Proposed use and missing identification

The extension to finitely many Hilbert coordinates follows by applying the scalar inequality to each coordinate and summing. That extension is a direct inference, not a separately quoted theorem.

A possible application would identify the negative nested primitive terms as averages of ACTUAL auxiliary-edge differences, and positive bridge/radial squares as the right-hand energy. The exact new radial identity is in main_radial_auxiliary_reserve_20261008_r3.md. Any application must exhibit the actual mu,t, every physical/auxiliary input, all cross terms, the boundary conditions and a uniform finite-m bound on C. Its factor4 must fit the available target margin. No such identification or constant comparison has been obtained.

The completed right-suffix and initial-only chains have a zero-aggregate negative direction which all their existing reserves miss. Applying a generic embedding theorem to those same coordinates cannot repair that null-direction failure. New internal-block coordinates or changed original-row couplings are required before this source could be relevant. This is a reopening condition, not a new proof allocation.

The earlier SOURCE_HARDY_KERNEL_20260927.md already discussed one-dimensional weighted Hardy bounds and their factor losses. The present lookup concerns an actual branching-tree theorem; neither note proves a constant-preserving GD comparison. No search for a posted solution of the exact benchmark was made.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000412 -->

# Exact support bookkeeping for an already realized static pasting

SELF-AUTHORED manual calculation; UNVERIFIED. This is a concrete construction input, not a proved original-target advance. Do not report the recurrence itself as a closure.

Take the R4 static pasting in zhang_jiang_direct_closure_20261008_r4/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md, equations3-11, and use a zero horizontal left child. Let the actual prefix mass be sigma, C=1+sigma, bridge alpha=1+d>1, suffix mass tau. The suffix is one fixed projection-gradient function F_B with start v0, full physical trajectory and generating gradients b_j. Include zero in that body and put

    M=max_j <v0,b_j> >0,
    R_B^2=||v0||^2, a_B=R_B^2/M, z_B=2F_B(v_end)/M.

Choose theta>0,r>=0 with theta+(tau+1)r<=d and set

    p^2=M/[theta(1+r)], T=(C+theta)p.

The same explicit hull construction from R4 satisfies every original projection row, including zero and all cross-history rows. Its initial point is X0=(v0,T), prefix gradients are(0,p), suffix gradients are(b_j,-rp). It is one static finite-dimensional convex1-smooth function selected before the run. It uses every original prefix step even though the horizontal left coordinate is trivial.

The initial support of its full gradient body is exactly

    M_new=T p=J M, J=(C+theta)/[theta(1+r)].

Indeed the competing suffix supports are bounded above by M-rTp, and (1+r)Tp>M. Its original selected-origin radius and terminal value therefore give

    a_new=C+theta+a_B/J,
    z_new=z_B/J+[2r(d-theta)-(2tau+1)r^2]/(C+theta),
    actual normalized payoff=z_new/a_new.

The full minimum cone could only improve this valid selected-origin lower witness. No upper bound on a whole witness family follows from this normalization alone.

All quantities are properties of the same realized function and chronology. M is not a free variable: its bound must be proved for the selected child. A universal closure requires selecting actual children, cuts and theta,r for every word and obtaining (1-o(1))/B_n with word-independent loss. The R4 failure on pasted quadratic Silver children does not settle a strategy that also chooses global Huber/quadratic witnesses. Conversely, merely postulating a favorable reachable set of(a,z,M) would restate the missing problem. No program, model or formal allocation is granted by this note.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000414 -->
# An affine ellipse and the zero-terminal-star endpoint

SELF-AUTHORED direct mathematical lead; JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original Goal OPEN.
This note changes no active worker TASK or computational allocation. It is
an analytical interface for a future bounded call, not a verified fact.

Take a fixed original word with mass S and D=1+2S. Use the original ordered
rows Qij=fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2<=0. Assume no incoming terminal
rows and terminal weights lambda_n,*=2pi, lambda_n,i=2alpha_i, with
alpha_i>=0 and sum alpha_i=1-pi. In the no-incoming-optimum convention,
retain exact function flow and the terminal square

    ||gn-pi*x0+sum_i r_i gi||^2,
    r_i=h_i*(pi+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i.

Let t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j and V=sum_i r_i=S-sum_i alpha_i*t_i.
The full remaining quadratic, restricted to a common formal physical
gradient including gn, has matrix

    B=[[q-pi^2, pi*u-1],[pi*u-1, D-u^2]],  u=V+1.

This restriction is only necessary. It does not determine all original
row signs or the full matrix. The formula and conventions must be
independently derived from the complete polynomial before application.

## 1. Dimensionless form of the necessary affine test

Put Q=qD, A=pi*sqrt(D), and Bsc=u/sqrt(D). Congruence of the displayed
matrix gives

    [[Q-A^2, A*Bsc-1],[A*Bsc-1,1-Bsc^2]].

Its determinant equals

    Q-1-A^2-Q*Bsc^2+2*A*Bsc
      =(Q-1)*(1-Bsc^2)-(A-Bsc)^2.

For Q>=1 the PSD conditions can therefore be written as

    A^2<=Q, Bsc^2<=1,
    (A-Bsc)^2<=(Q-1)*(1-Bsc^2).

At Q=1 this forces A=Bsc with |A|<=1, that is u=D*pi and pi^2<=1/D.
At saturation pi=sqrt(q), the first diagonal is zero, so u=1/pi is
forced instead. This saturation is a boundary of the affine feasible
region, not a necessity proved for the whole certificate cone.

For Q=17/16 the ellipse width is explicitly

    |A-Bsc| <= (1/4)*sqrt(1-Bsc^2).

In particular pi=0 is allowed by this necessary screen whenever
u^2<=D/17. This is not a sufficient dual construction.

## 2. A literal interpretation of pi=0 with last-query terminal contact

Suppose the final physical step is b=1+a in(0,2), l=n-1. Set
pi=0, alpha_l=1, other alpha_i=0. The only terminal row is
lambda_n,l=2, and the terminal square is ||gn+a*g_l||^2. Direct expansion
using x_n=x_l-b*g_l gives the exact identity

    -2f_n+2Q_(n,l)
      =-2f_l+(1-a^2)||g_l||^2+||gn+a*g_l||^2.

Thus an original-prefix certificate for

    2f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2 <= q||x0-xstar||^2

would extend to the complete word with this terminal contact. The desired
prefix certificate is not obtained in this note. Merely adding a free
x0 square to an old sharp-q certificate does not supply it.

There is a second, projection-specific sufficient route to the same
prefix quantity. For F_D with gradient P_D and g_l=P_D(x_l), put
d=2-a^2>=1. Then

    2F_D(x_l)-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2
      =2<x_l,g_l>-d||g_l||^2 <= U_d(x_l).

If the ORIGINAL prefix of mass S-b had a universal U_d endpoint law,
this would give denominator

    d+2(S-b)=D-b^2.

This is a conditional interface only: that prefix is not automatically
one of the accepted H3 blocks. No unproved all-prefix U propagation is
being invoked. The complete polar minimum cone must be retained, since
U_N(x0)<=dist(x0,C_D)^2/N uses an arbitrary a_star in C_D via
<a_star,g><=0. A bound against ||x0|| alone would not cover the lifted
selected minima in the universality theorem.

If this prefix law were eventually proved and D>=17*b^2, then
1/(D-b^2)<=17/(16D). No claim is made that the missing prefix law holds.

Resources: direct text reasoning only; zero mathematical programs,
numerical/formal calls, truth-store edits, publication or Goal completion.
