# Source-driven start for a direct converse proof

SELF-AUTHORED analytical note, JOINT UNVERIFIED. This is a starting calculation and scope check, not new progress on the original theorem. The operator explicitly asks to prioritize an actual closure over more equivalent representations and fixed-horizon models.

The primary source reread is Zhang and Jiang, [Accelerated Gradient Descent by Concatenation of Stepsize Schedules, v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2), Sections2.2,3.1,3.2. Their primitive/dominant definitions retain joint endpoint information; Lemma15 transfers it across a bridge. Equations22-24 and38-45 select the bridge using exact quadratic identities and complete a square. These are sufficient composition theorems. They do not assert that every nonnegative word belongs to that class or that every optimal full certificate has their rank-one form. No such converse may be imported.

## Direct calculation of the local roots from static hard functions

Fix ANY predetermined nonnegative word with a selected step alpha>1. Let x be its total mass before that step, y its total mass after it, C=1+x, k=alpha-1. For r>0 define one fixed global gradient g(z)=clip(z,-r,1), with f(z)=integral_0^z g(t)dt and original start z0=C. This f is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with unique minimum0. Every prefix query stays at least1 and has gradient1, so the pivot input is1 and its output is-k.

Choose r=k/(2y+1). Every remaining query and the terminal stay at most-r, since the terminal is-k+y*r=-(y+1)*r. Their gradient is-r. Direct integration gives

    2f_terminal/C^2 = k^2/[C^2(2y+1)].

The Huber lower witness for the whole word has value1/D, where D=1+2(x+alpha+y)=2C+2k+(2y+1). These two actual full-word witnesses balance exactly when

    k^2 D=C^2(2y+1).

With B=2y+1, the difference factors as

    k^2(2C+2k+B)-C^2B=(k+C)(2k^2+Bk-CB).

The positive root of the second factor gives alpha=psi(x,y). Thus the objective bridge root has a concrete two-ray hard-function interpretation, with all chronology and original radius paid. This is the familiar single-cut type of lower witness, not a claimed new complete adversary.

For the primitive endpoint test, instead choose r=k/(1+y). The same fixed-function trajectory ends exactly at-r. Its primitive quadratic is

    V=C^2-[(x+alpha+y+1)r]^2.

Writing C_R=1+y, its sign changes at

    k(C+C_R+k)=C C_R,

which is alpha=phi(x,y). On this projection-Huber function the original minimizer interpolation rows are zero, so the primitive retained energy is V/2. A negative primitive energy is NOT a useful objective lower bound by itself.

## Actual closure task, not a reformulation

These local roots explain what must be preserved in a recursive adverse construction, but do not supply it. Repeating independent caps after each cut changes the function; directly adding independent functions makes all their coordinates evolve during every step, so it does not concatenate trajectories. Optimizing only the above one-cut witnesses must not be asserted sufficient.

The intended substantive step is an actual finite-dimensional static interpolation construction that retains every earlier query when a primitive-type and dominant-type adverse history are joined. It must pay the cross-history constraints, true initial distance and final objective, and prove a loss that stays1+o(1) after an arbitrary-word decomposition. A second necessary step is to prove that an appropriate split/decomposition exists for EVERY nonnegative word, including zeros and extreme steps; the schedule is not assumed recursive.

If those steps are not proved, naming a typed Bellman value, an equivalent SDP, or a 'gluing lemma' of the same strength as the original target is not a result. The current two displayed local calculations are only calibration for a direct proof attempt. No new numerical job, formal submission or independent model is authorized by this note.

# R5 activation handoff

Status: **UNVERIFIED. Original exact-profile target OPEN.** One fresh Astra/ultra analytical invocation completed; two substantive lines; mathematical programs/arrays/scans/solvers 0; formal submissions 0; additional models/agents 0. Parent owns ledger and any future grant.

Read PROOF_ATTEMPT.md for complete derivations and scope. The performance normalization is exactly \(\mathcal W=2\mathcal R\), and the benchmark remains \(B_n=2S_\bullet(n)+1\), with every integer split. The proof separately uses \(R^2\) for the initial squared radius. No claim of completion, asymptotic disproof, or new accepted fact was made.

1. **Silent ridge bank.** Independent fresh normals force the full global curvature sum, even if their quadratic regions are visited at different times. A nonnegative convex bank zero at the original minimizer and every old query is zero on their full convex hull. The first-activation estimate is \(2H(x-hg)\le h^2g^T(I-A)g\). If the old collinear direction has curvature \(1-\delta\), every such bank satisfies the SHARP transverse bound \(\|P_\perp\nabla H(-re)\|\le r\sqrt\delta/2\). The bound is attained by an explicit two-dimensional static function with closest original minimizer 0 and radius 1. This does not bound arbitrary future amplification or the whole old objective.

2. **Actual high-dimensional Huber relay.** Equations (10)--(22) specify one finite-dimensional static function for any finite number of checkpoints, prove its global smoothness, complete minimum set and exact original radius, and track ALL actual regions under explicit return guards. The exact terminal witness includes both the minimum-preserving first-crossing subtraction \(b_0-1/d\) and the spectral coupling coefficient \((c/d)^{2K}\). Dropping either is invalid. All ordinary entries, empty blocks and zero steps are accounted for.

3. **Decisive constant check inside line 2.** For the ENTIRE admissible central one-transfer relay family, with equal side sums \(s\),

   \[
   E/H_{\rm base} < (b/s)^2(2+b/s+1/(2s)).
   \]

   On the inherited-m, parameter-1/4 Gray word of the supplied report, \(b/s\to\sqrt2-1\), giving a uniform limsup at most \(\sqrt2-1<1\). This subfamily cannot repair that word's known Huber gap. The parent freshly confirmed accepted fact 5fd396eba75651e7 for that Huber comparison and two specified spiral families. It does not certify the complete quadratic pool; no such assertion was added.

No all-word checkpoint selection or constant-preserving Zhang--Jiang recursion follows from this relay. Such a missing result cannot be treated as routine. The central one-transfer family is closed for this route; merely changing its d, c or cap scale is already covered by the analytic bound. Many-transfer relays and other static functions are not excluded, but would require a genuinely new closure argument, including every backward return.

No mathematical program, recurrence replay, numerical experiment, formal submission, private worker-memory read, service action or truth-store edit was performed. The artifacts are drafts, not verifier-accepted authority. Stop this invocation here; no automatic renewal.

# Static activation R5: curvature paid at birth and a complete Huber relay

Status: **UNVERIFIED direct analytical draft. Original target OPEN.**

Project: `gd-exact-profile-20260926`. Allocation: one fresh Astra/ultra invocation, at most two substantive lines, mathematical programs 0, numerical arrays/scans/solvers 0, formal submissions 0. No new model calls, agents, installs, service actions or truth-store mutations were made. The parent owns the allocation ledger. All new mathematics below requires independent verification before use as a fact.

The current `OPERATOR.md`, the COMPLETE `PROBLEM.md`, the supplied task, the scoped spiral activation obstruction, Sections 1--3 and the conclusion of the specified strong-reactivation report, and the relevant shared-history warning in R4 were read. No closed numerical data were opened.

## Target and source use

Put (B_n=2S_\bullet(n)+1), with the exact integer-split sequence in `PROBLEM.md`. The requested lower bound is

\[
 \forall\varepsilon>0\ \exists N_\varepsilon\ \forall n\ge N_\varepsilon\ \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:
 \quad \mathcal W_n(h)\ge (1-\varepsilon)/B_n.
 \tag{T}
\]

Here (\mathcal W=2\mathcal R). All functions below have advertised smoothness 1. Multiplication of the function by (L>0), with physical steps (h_t/L), preserves their iterates and normalized payoff. A restricted-family upper bound below is never an upper bound for (\mathcal W_n(h)).

[Zhang--Jiang v2, Sections 3.1 and 3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2) was read through both concatenation proofs. Its primitive certificate retains distance, value and terminal-gradient energy together; the dominant concatenation finishes a square using the same minimizer and weighted future gradients. This motivates accounting for the full curvature matrix and the complete original trajectory here. Those upper certificates do not supply a converse construction for arbitrary words. No decimal asymptotic constant is used.

## Line 1. A fresh bank of positive ridges cannot borrow saturated old curvature

### 1.1 The actual global condition, including unvisited regions

For fixed finite vectors (v_j\in\mathbb R^d), thresholds (t_j\ge0), and a symmetric (A\succeq0), consider the SINGLE static function

\[
 F(x)=\tfrac12x^TAx+H(x),\qquad
 H(x)=\sum_{j=1}^m \tfrac12(\langle v_j,x\rangle-t_j)_+^2,
 \qquad M=\sum_jv_jv_j^T.
 \tag{1}
\]

Every coefficient is frozen before the run. Positive scalar ridge weights can be absorbed into (v_j,t_j). The sufficient global condition is

\[
 A+M\preceq I. \tag{2}
\]

Indeed, the gradient difference between any two points equals

\[
 \nabla F(x)-\nabla F(y)=(A+\sum_j\theta_jv_jv_j^T)(x-y),
 \qquad 0\le\theta_j\le1,
\]

using scalar divided differences of (z_+). The matrix is positive semidefinite and at most (I); this proves global convexity, differentiability and 1-Lipschitz gradient. Moreover (F\ge0=F(0)), and its complete minimum set is

\[
 \arg\min F=\ker A\cap\{x:\langle v_j,x\rangle\le t_j\ \text{for every }j\}.
 \tag{3}
\]

If the ridge normals are linearly independent, (2) is also NECESSARY for this representation to be 1-smooth. The map (x\mapsto(\langle v_j,x\rangle)_j) is onto, so some open region has every ridge strictly active. Its Hessian is exactly (A+M).

The same necessity holds for independent one-sided Huber ridges, with any strictly positive cap for each ridge: choose all residuals strictly within their quadratic intervals. Therefore disjoint curvature intervals ALONG THE RUN do not remove the full global curvature condition when the fresh normals are independent. This observation concerns this additive representation, not all smooth convex functions.

### 1.2 The whole old hull, not only the last query

Suppose the proposed new bank has (H(x_i)=0) at all earlier actual queries and at the selected original minimizer 0. Then it vanishes on

\[
 K=\operatorname{conv}\{0,x_0,\ldots,x_k\}. \tag{4}
\]

This follows directly from convexity and nonnegativity. In particular (\nabla H=0) on (K), since every point of (K) is a global minimizer of (H). An actual first activation can occur only at a point outside (K). A fresh orthogonal coordinate does not on its own establish such an escape.

For a unit normal (u), a squared hinge zero on (K) needs a threshold at least (\sup_{z\in K}\langle u,z\rangle). Its positive residual at (y) is therefore at most (\operatorname{dist}(y,K)). This is sharp over unit normals for (y\notin K): take the outward normal from the Euclidean projection of (y) onto (K). The projection inequality supplies the supporting threshold, and Cauchy--Schwarz supplies the reverse bound.

The aggregate bank has the stronger matrix bound

\[
 2H(y)\le\inf_{z\in K}(y-z)^TM(y-z).
 \tag{5}
\]

For each (z\in K), each positive residual is at most (\langle v_j,y-z\rangle_+); sum its square and then discard the positive-part restriction. This proves (5), with no assumptions on the dimension or number of hinges.

If (x=x_k\in K), (g=Ax) is the old gradient at the last query, and the next ACTUAL point is (y=x-hg), then

\[
 2H(y)\le h^2g^TMg\le h^2g^T(I-A)g,
 \qquad 2H(y)\le y^TMy.
 \tag{6}
\]

The second inequality uses (0\in K). Also

\[
 \|\nabla H(y)\|^2\le h^2g^T(I-A)g, \tag{7}
\]

because (\nabla H=V^Tr), (r_j=(\langle v_j,y\rangle-t_j)_+), (\|V\|^2=\|M\|\le1), and the same residual estimate bounds (\|r\|^2). These statements remain valid for one-sided Huber profiles, whose value is at most the squared hinge and whose derivative is between 0 and the positive residual.

### 1.3 A sharp transverse budget, including its actual attained example

Let (e) be a unit vector, (0<\delta<1), and let

\[
 A=(1-\delta)ee^T,\qquad D=I-A.
\]

Consider a possible first crossing to (y=-re), (r>0), from an earlier collinear history on which the new bank was zero. For every static bank of the preceding type satisfying (2),

\[
 \|P_{e^\perp}\nabla H(-re)\|\le \frac{r\sqrt\delta}{2}.
 \tag{8}
\]

This includes arbitrary nonnegative thresholds and arbitrary positive Huber caps. To derive it, for each ridge write its derivative at (-re) as (\theta_j(-r\langle v_j,e\rangle)), where (0\le\theta_j\le1); ridges with nonnegative (\langle v_j,e\rangle) have derivative zero. Set (N=\sum_j\theta_jv_jv_j^T). Then (0\preceq N\preceq M\preceq D), and (\nabla H(-re)=-rNe).

For (T=D^{-1/2}ND^{-1/2}), one has (0\preceq T\preceq I), hence (T^2\preceq T). Writing (a=\langle e,Te\rangle),

\[
 \|P_{e^\perp}Te\|^2\le a-a^2\le\tfrac14.
\]

Since (P_{e^\perp}Ne=\sqrt\delta P_{e^\perp}Te), this proves (8).

The constant is attained by the explicit TWO-dimensional function

\[
 F(x)=\frac{1-\delta}{2}\langle e,x\rangle^2
       +\frac12\left\langle-\sqrt{\delta/2}\,e+\frac{w}{\sqrt2},x\right\rangle_+^2,
 \qquad w\perp e,\quad\|w\|=1. \tag{9}
\]

Its global curvature condition follows from

\[
 v^TD^{-1}v=1,\quad v=-\sqrt{\delta/2}\,e+w/\sqrt2,
\]

which gives (vv^T\preceq D). Starting at (x_0=e), every positive collinear query has an inactive hinge. Any actual step (h>1/(1-\delta)) taken from (e) lands at (y=-re), where (r=h(1-\delta)-1). The new transverse gradient is (r\sqrt\delta,w/2). The minimum set is

\[
 \{x:\langle e,x\rangle=0,\ \langle w,x\rangle\le0\},
\]

with value 0, and 0 is its closest point to (x_0=e). Thus the original radius is exactly 1; there is no source reset or prepaid fresh initial coordinate in this attainment example.

Equation (8) rules out obtaining an order-one transverse gradient at a bounded crossing distance by adding arbitrarily many silent positive ridges to an old direction whose curvature tends to 1. It does NOT bound the eventual objective of the whole function: old energy remains, and an arbitrary future word may amplify the new component. The attempt to obtain a lossless recursive child simply by increasing the fresh bank's dimension therefore stops at this quantified cost. It does not exclude a globally chosen, substantially different prefix.

## Line 2. A static arbitrary-depth relay that pays every return guard

The second line avoids a near-unit source curvature. It gives a literal high-dimensional static function and complete chronology for a stated word, then tests its paid constant. It is not a freely selectable state recurrence.

### 2.1 Word, normals, and global function

Fix an integer (K\ge1), and partition the original chronological word as

\[
 h=(a^{(0)},b_0,a^{(1)},b_1,\ldots,b_{K-1},a^{(K)}),
 \qquad s_j=\sum a^{(j)}\ge0.
 \tag{10}
\]

Every (a^{(j)}) is the complete original intervening subword, including zero entries. No entries are removed. Choose (d,c>0) with

\[
 2c\cos\frac\pi{K+2}<d,\qquad
 d+2c\cos\frac\pi{K+2}\le1. \tag{11}
\]

There are explicit independent vectors (v_0,\ldots,v_K\in\mathbb R^{K+1}) with

\[
 \langle v_i,v_j\rangle=
 \begin{cases}d&i=j,\\-c&|i-j|=1,\\0&|i-j|>1.\end{cases} \tag{12}
\]

For completeness put (\vartheta=\pi/(K+2)), and for (1\le\ell\le K+1) define coordinates

\[
 (v_j)_\ell=\sqrt{d-2c\cos(\ell\vartheta)}\,
 \sqrt{\frac2{K+2}}\sin((j+1)\ell\vartheta).
 \tag{13}
\]

The finite sine basis diagonalizes the tridiagonal matrix in (12): the identity (\sin((j+2)t)+\sin(jt)=2\cos t\sin((j+1)t)), with the two zero boundary values, gives its eigenvalues (d-2c\cos(\ell\vartheta)). Orthogonality of distinct eigenvectors and the identity (\sum_{j=1}^{K+1}\sin^2(j\ell\vartheta)=(K+2)/2) give the normalization in (13). Thus every eigenvalue lies in ((0,1]), and

\[
 \sum_{j=0}^K v_jv_j^T\preceq I. \tag{14}
\]

These formulas define finite vectors analytically; no array or recurrence was executed.

For (\mu>0), use the explicit one-sided Huber function

\[
 \phi_\mu(z)=
 \begin{cases}
 0&z\le0,\\
 z^2/2&0\le z\le\mu,\\
 \mu z-\mu^2/2&z\ge\mu.
 \end{cases}
 \quad q_\mu(z)=\phi_\mu'(z)=\min\{\mu,\max\{z,0\}\}.
 \tag{15}
\]

Assume (b_j>1/d). Choose an arbitrary scale (\mu_0>0). Define

\[
 A_1=c(b_0-1/d)\mu_0,
 \qquad
 \mu_j=\frac{A_j}{1+ds_j}\quad(1\le j<K),
 \qquad A_{j+1}=cb_j\mu_j\quad(1\le j<K),
 \qquad\mu_K=\frac{A_K}{1+2ds_K}. \tag{16}
\]

All these quantities are positive, including when a block is empty. Set

\[
 t_0=t_1=0,\qquad t_j=c\mu_{j-1}s_{j-1}\quad(2\le j\le K),
 \quad x_0=\frac{\mu_0(1+ds_0)}d v_0,
 \quad F(x)=\sum_{j=0}^K\phi_{\mu_j}(\langle v_j,x\rangle-t_j).
 \tag{17}
\]

This is one function fixed from the entire word before the run. Equations (14)--(15) prove global convexity and 1-smoothness by the divided-difference argument used in Line 1. Its complete minimum set and minimum value are

\[
 \arg\min F=\{x:\langle v_j,x\rangle\le t_j\ \forall j\},
 \qquad F_\star=0. \tag{18}
\]

All thresholds are nonnegative, so 0 is a minimizer. The halfspace (\langle v_0,x\rangle\le0) alone forces every minimizer's distance from the positive multiple (x_0) of (v_0) to be at least (\|x_0\|). Equality is attained at 0. Consequently the TRUE original squared radius is

\[
 R^2=\operatorname{dist}(x_0,\arg\min F)^2
 =\frac{\mu_0^2(1+ds_0)^2}{d}. \tag{19}
\]

### 2.2 Exact guards, including backward reactivation

The required conditions are

\[
 c\mu_{i+1}(s_{i+1}+b_{i+1})\le\mu_i(db_i-1)
       \quad(0\le i\le K-2),
 \qquad
 c\mu_Ks_K\le\mu_{K-1}(db_{K-1}-1).
 \tag{20}
\]

For (K=1), the first set is empty. These are conditions on (10)--(17), not assumptions that future queries may be selected independently. Under (20), the COMPLETE ACTUAL chronology is:

* Throughout (a^{(j)}), and at its following bridge query if (j<K), only ridge (j) has positive derivative. Its derivative is exactly (\mu_j).
* Every earlier ridge has derivative zero and stays off; every later ridge has derivative zero until the corresponding bridge has physically crossed its threshold.
* The last ridge remains in its linear Huber region through the terminal point.

Here is the induction including all region boundaries. Write (r_j=\langle v_j,x\rangle-t_j). While only ridge (j) is active at cap (\mu_j), an actual step (h\ge0) changes the residuals by

\[
 r_j\longmapsto r_j-hd\mu_j,\qquad
 r_{j-1}\longmapsto r_{j-1}+hc\mu_j,\qquad
 r_{j+1}\longmapsto r_{j+1}+hc\mu_j,
 \tag{21}
\]

with every other residual unchanged. These are the inner products of the actual update (x\mapsto x-h\mu_jv_j).

Initially (r_0=\mu_0(1+ds_0)), (r_1=-c\mu_0(1/d+s_0)), and (r_j=-t_j\le0) for (j\ge2). During the first ordinary block, (r_0\ge\mu_0), while (r_1\le-c\mu_0/d<0). At the first bridge the old residual becomes (\mu_0(1-db_0)<0), and the next one becomes (A_1>0). This proves both the first crossing and the subtraction (1/d) in (16). Omitting that subtraction would fail to preserve the original minimum and radius in (17)--(19).

For (1\le j<K), the entry residual is (A_j=\mu_j(1+ds_j)). During its ordinary block it remains at least (\mu_j); the next ridge starts at (-c\mu_js_j) and reaches exactly 0 at the bridge query. At the bridge, ridge (j) becomes negative and ridge (j+1) obtains (cb_j\mu_j=A_{j+1}>0). Any still later ridge is unchanged and remains nonpositive.

The only possible return is to ridge (j-1). It entered this block with residual (\mu_{j-1}(1-db_{j-1})). Its total increase over the block AND bridge is at most (c\mu_j(s_j+b_j)). The appropriate condition in (20) keeps it nonpositive at every actual query, because the partial sums of nonnegative steps are at most the full block sum. After this stage it never changes again, by (12). This proves every old-return guard; no assumption of one-way activation is hidden in the construction.

At the final block, (r_K=A_K=\mu_K(1+2ds_K)), so after any partial sum (s\le s_K), it equals (\mu_K(1+2ds_K-ds)\ge\mu_K). The final condition in (20) keeps ridge (K-1) off. Boundary residual 0 has derivative 0, and residual (\mu_j) has derivative (\mu_j); hence equality guards, empty blocks and zero steps cause no ambiguity.

### 2.3 Exact normalized terminal objective

The terminal residual is (r_K=\mu_K(1+ds_K)). Every other ridge is zero in value as well as gradient. Therefore

\[
 2F(x_n)=\mu_K^2(1+2ds_K)=\frac{A_K^2}{1+2ds_K}.
\]

Combining this with (16) and the ORIGINAL radius (19) gives the complete witness payoff

\[
 \boxed{
 E(h)=\frac{2F(x_n)}{R^2}
 =\frac{d\,c^{2K}(b_0-1/d)^2\prod_{i=1}^{K-1}b_i^2}
 {\left[\prod_{i=0}^{K-1}(1+ds_i)^2\right](1+2ds_K)}.
 } \tag{22}
\]

This is a valid lower witness for the ORIGINAL (\mathcal W_n(h)) whenever the displayed hypotheses and guards hold. No interpolation oracle, origin replacement, new initial component, unproved active-set law or infinite-dimensional limit occurs in this calculation.

It does not establish (T). In particular, positivity and the global norm alone do not imply the guards. For example, for (K\ge2) the first return guard, after cancellation of the positive (b_0-1/d), is exactly

\[
 c^2(s_1+b_1)\le d(1+ds_1). \tag{23}
\]

If this is violated, the old ridge really reactivates before the claimed second bridge has finished. Its nonzero derivative changes the actual update; formula (22) is then inapplicable.

The globally paid coupling also satisfies

\[
 \frac cd<\frac1{2\cos(\pi/(K+2))}. \tag{24}
\]

After rewriting (1+ds_i=d(s_i+1/d)), (22) contains ((c/d)^{2K}). As (K\to\infty), the upper edge of this coefficient behaves as (4^{-K}) up to a factor tending to 1. The statement follows from (cos x=1-x^2/2+O(x^4)), so (-2K\log\cos(\pi/(K+2))\to0). This is a REAL paid factor, not an (o(1)) that can be discarded. It is not by itself a proof that every choice of checkpoints fails: their numerators can grow too. It does show that this static relay cannot be presented as a cost-free realization of the Zhang--Jiang split using the same source budgets.

### 2.4 A uniform failure of the entire central one-transfer subfamily

This tests the preceding exact function family on a relevant unbounded sequence of words, without evaluating a finite trajectory. For (K=1), let the two side sums be equal to (s>0), and write the central bridge as (b). The Gram conditions imply (c<d), (d+c\le1). The last return guard is automatic:

\[
 \frac{c^2s}{1+2ds}\le d,
\]

using (c\le d). Formula (22) is valid for every (b>1/d), and the same-word standard Huber witness is (H_{\rm base}=1/(1+4s+2b)).

Put (z=ds>0), (r=b/s). Since (c\le d) and (db>1),

\[
 \frac{E}{H_{\rm base}}
 \le (4+2r+1/s)\frac{z(rz-1)^2}{(1+z)^2(1+2z)}
 < r^2\left(2+r+\frac1{2s}\right).
 \tag{25}
\]

The last step uses (0<rz-1<r(1+z)) and (z/(1+2z)<1/2). Thus (25) is UNIFORM over all admissible (d,c), including choices depending on the enclosing horizon. The arbitrary scale (\mu_0) has already canceled. This is not one tuned two-dimensional example: it bounds every central one-transfer member of the stated relay architecture.

For the inherited-(m), parameter-(1/4) word identified in the supplied historical report, define analytically

\[
 \lambda=1+\sqrt2,\quad c_m=\lambda(1+1/(4m)),\quad
 a_m=\frac{1-1/(4m)}{c_m},\quad
 H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a_mc_m^j,H_j)
 \quad(0\le j<m).
 \tag{26}
\]

All children use the SAME enclosing (m). The two sides of the central bridge have sum

\[
 s_m=v_mc_m^{m-1}+(1-v_m)2^{m-1}-1,
 \quad v_m=\frac{a_m}{c_m-2},
 \quad b_m=1+a_mc_m^{m-1}. \tag{27}
\]

Equation (27) follows by summing the explicitly defined binary concatenation, or by substituting into its sum recurrence. Since (v_m\to1), (c_m\to\lambda>2), and (s_m\to\infty),

\[
 \frac{b_m}{s_m}\longrightarrow \lambda-2=\sqrt2-1=:q.
\]

Equations (25)--(27) yield

\[
 \limsup_{m\to\infty}\sup_{\substack{d,c\text{ admissible}\\b_m>1/d}}
 \frac{E(H_m)}{H_{\rm base}(H_m)}
 \le q^2(2+q)=q<1. \tag{28}
\]

Thus this central relay cannot improve even the same-word Huber baseline on these horizons, regardless of its admissible curvatures and cap scale. If (b_m\le1/d), this architecture does not perform its first activation and is outside the relay hypothesis; it cannot be counted as an unproved alternative history satisfying (22).

The historical `strong_reactivation_and_failed_screens.md`, Section 1, attributes \(H_{\rm base}(H_m)/U_{2^m-1}^{\rm ZJ}<237/250\) for \(m\ge1536\) to accepted fact `5fd396eba75651e7`. The parent subsequently confirmed this exact scope by a fresh `fact_search`: the fact treats the same enclosing-m word, the Huber comparison, and two specified spiral-family optima; it does NOT certify the complete scalar-quadratic pool. Equations (25)--(28) were derived here and do not require that fact. Importing its Huber comparison places this relay at most \((237/250)(\sqrt2-1)+o(1)\) of the target on that word. No assertion that this word defeats the complete quadratic baseline pool is made. No raw data were reopened.

The bound in (28) covers the central one-transfer subfamily. It does not cover every partition in (10), other sign patterns of the Gram matrix, all additive ridge functions, or the unrestricted smooth convex class.

## Closure audit

The two lines produce no complete candidate for either answer to the original problem.

* Line 1 settles the birth budget for this silent positive-ridge architecture and supplies an exact attained transverse constant, with the original minimum and radius. It leaves the future terminal amplification unresolved.
* Line 2 constructs one static finite-dimensional function for arbitrary depth and proves all physical activation, return, minimum, radius and terminal-value assertions under explicit guards. Its entire central one-transfer family fails on an unbounded sequence of the relevant Huber-gap words. Its many-transfer version still pays the spectral coupling factor and needs an actual all-word selection and terminal comparison; those are not proved and are not called technical lemmas.
* Neither line constructs a witness meeting ((1-o(1))/B_n) for every sufficiently large (n) and every (h\ge0). Neither supplies a full-function upper separation. No recurrence was substituted for the original minimax theorem.

The present allocation ends after these two lines. The original Goal stays OPEN, and no verifier call is warranted for an alleged full solution. A subsequent grant should not merely retune the one-transfer example or drop condition (20). A new mechanism would need to pay curvature and backward returns while retaining the whole joint distance/value/gradient budget, or provide a different complete candidate.

# Fresh R5 direct closure attempt: static convex activation without a reset

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Original Goal ACTIVE/OPEN. ONE NEW gpt-6-astra/ultra analytical invocation, consult OFF, up to TWO substantive proof lines. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays/scans/solvers and formal submissions ZERO. Parent owns ledger. Read current OPERATOR.md and COMPLETE PROBLEM.md. The user now prioritizes direct closure inspired by Zhang-Jiang, not more equivalent representations or fixed-horizon toy models. All old grants are CLOSED.

## Original theorem, not a restricted diagnostic

Target W_n(h)>= (1-o(1))/B_n uniformly for EVERY sufficiently large n and EVERY predetermined h>=0, with the exact Zhang-Jiang integer-split profile and W=2R. Alternatively a genuine full-function asymptotic upper separation would disprove the claim. Keep finite dimension, convexity, advertised L-smoothness, attained minimum, actual GD, original radius and last objective. Original PROBLEM gives all definitions; do not replace its profile by0.4239 or the exponent alone.

## Concrete different mechanism

Try to build one static globally convex1-smooth adversary from positive quadratic/Huber ridge terms that are inactive on the full earlier trajectory and activate at a known later step. The function and every threshold/direction are chosen after the whole word is known but before the run. This is a direct function construction, not an unspecified interpolation oracle.

A starting formula is

    F(x)=0.5 x^T A x + sum_j beta_j phi_j(<u_j,x>-t_j),

where A>=0, beta_j>=0 and phi_j are explicit convex C1 scalar hinge/Huber profiles with derivative Lipschitz<=1. For squared positive hinges phi(z)=0.5(z_+)^2. The sufficient GLOBAL smoothness condition is A+sum_j beta_j u_j u_j^T<=I; less restrictive active-set arguments are allowed only if proved globally, not just along sampled iterates. Thresholds or a translated selected minimum must ensure an attained minimum and an exact initial-radius calculation. No new direction orthogonal to the whole past can magically activate under old gradients; prove the physical first crossing of every activation boundary.

Use Zhang-Jiang v2 https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2 Sections3.1/3.2 as inspiration for the retained joint distance/value/gradient budget. Try to choose directions, thresholds, curvatures and a decomposition so the activation cost is paid recursively with the exact phi/psi scale or a genuinely uniform1-o(1) loss. Finite-field/orthogonal-frame or probabilistic tools may be used analytically if they give the actual real PSD/smoothness and activation inequalities, not an analogy.

The decisive object is a complete static history with all steps paid and a benchmark-scale terminal objective for arbitrary words surviving the legal Huber/quadratic baselines. Small fixed-dimensional or fixed-count examples are not the deliverable. If an induction relies on a new geometric lemma, prove the lemma with the same function, old queries and radius; do not present the missing theorem as a reformulation.

## Prior boundaries to avoid repeating

Read ONLY the relevant scope of research/global_checkpoint_geometry_20261001/activation_obstruction.md. It proves a specific spiral, zero-height reset architecture needs an exponentially large activation allowance; it is not an exclusion of all convex activation functions. Do not restore the old zero minimum without paying its original optimum row.

Read research/strong_reactivation_fields_20261002/strong_reactivation_and_failed_screens.md Sections1-3 and its conclusion as needed: three specified low-dimensional static fields have complete scoped failures. The old weak_ridge_forward numerical batch was confined to dimension<=2,<=3 ridges and a few finite depths, and is CLOSED. DO NOT open its raw data or rerun it. Merely changing one angle/weight of those examples is not a new closure line.

The R4 actual static-pasting report research/zhang_jiang_direct_closure_20261008_r4/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md may be used for its precise warning: preserving unit-curvature old jets can force the entire old convex segment, and adding only a terminal transverse gradient uses terminal-scale interpolation slack. A globally chosen ridge field must alter compatible shared history or activate genuinely outside the old constrained region; no endpoint impulse or silent source reset is allowed.

## Direct proof standard and resources

At most TWO genuinely different attempts within this activation mechanism. Aim to close the original target; retain both answer possibilities. No finite toy experiments or free state-equivalence output. Give explicit function, Hessian/Lipschitz bound, complete minimum/radius, actual region guards, recurrence and full uniform objective bound. If it fails, isolate the actual obstacle and whether it occurs on words where the baseline pool also fails; no broad original-target conclusion from a family failure.

Text/primary-source reads, hand derivation, Markdown writes and hashes only. No mathematical programs or calculators, recurrence/symbolic replay, numerical files/arrays, additional models/agents, paid consults, installs, worker/service actions, private memory, truth-store edits, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal mutation. ONE invocation/<=TWO lines/program0/formal0. Write PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md in this new directory, all new math UNVERIFIED, then STOP. Parent closes ledger; no automatic renewal.

# Actual retained values: two endpoints paid, the middle remains open

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL PROOF ATTEMPT, with a quantitative
partial terminal estimate. No formal gate was allocated or called. This
report does not establish a complete obstruction or a benchmark witness
for the frozen policy, and does not resolve the original minimax problem.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; completed baseline60;
one round, completed ceiling61 / attempted ceiling62. TASK.md and latest
read guidance56f0c49630564f24 apply. Plan finding9569f5e8ea1c9a9e.
The full evidence84a4435a88a9f487 and a9423b621329882d was read. Those
drafts are provenance, not accepted premises. The construction and all
estimates used below are derived jointly here. The only accepted input
is the benchmark/ray conclusion of fact04219554e41d9750.

The new partial conclusion is that the FIRST and LAST actual circular
component values have vanishing original-benchmark normalization. Both
proofs use the actual ordered suffix, all preceding physical updates,
and state-dependent signs. They do not require the accumulated positive
charge X_m to be small. The middle k-2 components remain unpaid.

## 1. The unchanged word, function, and initialization

For integers m>=16 set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,
    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,
    d=c-2, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1.

All children use the SAME enclosing m. Select precisely the first
occurrences of levels J,...,m-1, at zero-based positions
2^(J+i-1)-1, i=1,...,k. The word is

    (H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1),...,b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).       (1.1)

Let s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k, and s_(k+1)=S_(m-1).
Selected step i is b_(J+i-1). No extra checkpoint, step deletion,
reordering, state reset, or modification of the function is made.

For 0<=q<=1 define

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),
    C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).

For a compact convex body C containing0 put

    E_C(w)=max_(g in C){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.

The one-sided Huber function Hub_delta is0 on(-infinity,0], t^2/2
on[0,delta], and delta*t-delta^2/2 on[delta,infinity).

Here is the exact symbolic local definition, included to fix the SAME
static function. For each ordinary block a_1,...,a_N in its physical
order, put alpha_j=a_j/2. Starting from (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), choose
q_(j-1) in[0,1) backwards by

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
      =max{beta/(C_beta-beta),
               (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/(u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},
    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*v(q_(j-1)).         (1.2)

The word has strictly positive steps. The ratio on the left increases
from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity, and the denominator on the right
is positive. The formula therefore uniquely specifies a finite set of
local data without executing a recurrence. Denote the starting local
coordinates for block i by u_i,y_i and put M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.

Set ell_1=0,D_1=1 and recursively define static constants

    T_i=b_(J+i-1)/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)).              (1.3)

For sufficiently large m all T_i are positive. The fixed function and
initial point are

    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                  +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),
    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (1.4)

The physical update is x^+=x-h grad F(x). Multiplying F by an arbitrary
advertised L>0 and dividing the physical gradient step by L gives the
same trajectory and normalized objective.

## 2. Geometry, chronology, and true radius, derived jointly

Completing the square shows grad E_C(w)=Pi_C(w), the Euclidean
projection onto C. Projection optimality at w and w' gives
||Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w')||^2<=<Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w'),w-w'>. Comparing the two
maximization formulas gives differentiability with this gradient.
Thus E_C is convex and1-smooth. It is nonnegative, and is zero exactly
on the polar cone of C: a positive support value remains positive in
the envelope at a sufficiently small positive multiple of the
supporting vector.

The horizontal sections of K are

    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                               (2.1)

The lower boundary is the segment joining0 to(beta,-1), and the
concave upper boundary is the displayed circular arc. The endpoints
are(C_beta,0),(beta,-1). In particular (p,0) belongs to K whenever
(p,-q) does. Its cone has those two extreme rays. Therefore

    E_(sigma K)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U-V<=0.              (2.2)

The disk center(beta,beta), radius1+beta contains0. Its outward radial
vector at(p(q),-q) supports K. In (1.2), u_j>=beta,y_j>=1 and the
q's are nondecreasing in forward order. Indeed, before a backward
step the uncapped supporting ratio is(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta); the
ratio defining its predecessor reduces the numerator by alpha_j*beta
and increases the denominator by the same amount. At q=0 the capped
normal-cone inequality is y_j<=beta*(u_j-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta),
which is preserved by further capped backward steps. Formula(1.2)
puts the vector from the candidate projection to the new point on
the circular outward normal, or in the endpoint normal cone when
q=0. Projection optimality gives

    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=v(q_j),
    u_i=beta+sum_j alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),
    y_i=1+sum_j alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.              (2.3)

The local states(A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j)
start at(ell_i+D_i,0), follow every step -(a_j/2)grad Phi_i,
and have last gradient sigma_i*(beta,-1). The checkpoint produces

    (x_i,x_(i+1))=(A_i-beta*D_(i+1),ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1)).     (2.4)

At that instant Phi_i is zero by(2.2), but it may reactivate later.
The construction does not reset it.

For EVERY state write

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

Then every physical update, including all prehistory, is

    x_i^+=x_i-(h/2)(P_i-Q_(i-1)), 1<=i<=k+1.              (2.5)

If x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing(p,-q) by(p,0) improves the
projection objective by q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+q^2/2 for q>0.
Consequently Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and the output
is nonnegative, Phi_i is zero by(2.2). These two properties prove
inductively that the designated active pair follows its local states:
the previous component contributes no derivative in its input
coordinate; the next component and all subsequent components vanish.
Every earlier retired coordinate still follows(2.5). This proves the
chronology without substituting post-checkpoint snapshots for the
actual later states.

The map stacking adjacent pairs and the last coordinate has squared
operator norm at most2. The factor1/2 in(1.4) therefore makes F
globally convex1-smooth. Also F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its complete minimum
set is

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                         x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.

The point x_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) is feasible. Since
1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1 and
u_1>=beta*y_1, A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). For a feasible point with
x_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1. On[0,A_1] the
lower bound (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2 decreases up to A_1 and is
attained by x_*. Thus the true closest radius is

    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2
         =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.            (2.6)

Convex1-smooth interpolation, in both directions and including all
samples and minimizers, follows directly: apply the smooth descent
inequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, whose minimum occurs at y, to get
F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
All trajectory Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices.

## 3. Uniform scales and the accepted benchmark input

We give the local estimates because earlier unverified drafts are not
premises. In a backward block let w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta).
At an uncapped point u_j-beta=(p(q_j)-beta)*w_j. A cross product
in(1.2) gives

    w_(j-1)=w_j*(1+
      [p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j)]
                  /[beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1))]).             (3.1)

At a transition to q_(j-1)=0 the same identity follows from
y_(j-1)=y_j, and if both q's are zero it is equality. Its numerator
is the integral of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t) between the two q's.
After division by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1), this integrand is at most
max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplication of(3.1), w_N=1 and 1+z<=exp(z)
therefore yield

    y_i<=2 exp(2k)=:Y_k,
    integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.     (3.2)

For the integral, split at t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta and integrate
-p' on the second interval. At an uncapped point,
u_j<=beta+2k*(Y_k+beta). Such backward steps form one contiguous
portion; their total u increment is at most2k*(Y_k+beta), and
their p is at least beta. The remaining steps have p=C_beta.
Thus their total deficit from C_beta times half the block mass is
at most4k^2*(Y_k+beta). Combining this with(2.3) and y_i/beta
gives, uniformly in i,

    |M_i-C_beta*s_i/2|<=K_k, K_k=20k^2 exp(2k).              (3.3)

The concave function p(q)+q/beta has smaller endpoint C_beta.
Consequently (2.3) also gives the useful exact lower bound

    M_i>=beta+1/beta+C_beta*s_i/2
        >=C_beta*(1+s_i/2).                                (3.4)

Put v_mass=a_word/d. One has0<v_mass<1, since c*(c-2)>1>eta_word.
Solving S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j by substitution gives the exact identities

    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d*S_j+c-1-d*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                         (3.5)

They imply uniformly for J<=j<=m-1 that S_j/(v_mass*c^j)->1,
k*K_k/S_J->0, and

    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),
    S_(m-r)/lambda^(m-r)->exp(1/4) for fixed r>=1.           (3.6)

For these claims, the relative error in the first limit is bounded
by a constant times c^(-J)+(2/c)^J. The logarithm of k*K_k is
O(k+log k), whereas J log(lambda) is of order m. Finally use
0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 with x=1/(4m), and k/m->0.

The first transfer ratio T_1/M_1 equals(d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)); all
later ones equal(c*d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)). Their relative errors are
uniformly O(K_k/S_J+c^(-J)+(2/c)^J), and k times this bound
tends to zero. Thus

    D_(k+1)=(d/C_beta)*(c*d/C_beta)^(k-1)*(1+o(1))
            ->(lambda-2)*exp(-2),
    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).                        (3.7)

Here c*d=1+O(1/m), and log(C_beta)/beta->2, so C_beta^k->exp(2).
The radius follows from(2.6),(3.2),(3.3). For every i<k,

    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2               (3.8)

uniformly. Eventually all these ratios lie in[1/3,1/2], so
sum_i sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2.

The ORIGINAL all-split benchmark is C_0=B_0=1 and

    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Only fact04219554e41d9750 is used for

    0<Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1)<infinity.              (3.9)

Its statement and proof Section7 were read. Its enclosure at b=1,j=0
also gives Z_1<5 from B_1=4. No seed assumption or unverified
construction is imported from that fact.

A growth estimate needed below is

    (B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m)->0.              (3.10)

Indeed B*sigma_1^2=O(lambda^(-m+2k)). Multiplication by K_k is
still exponentially decreasing in m because k=O(sqrt(m)); the term
2^m gives O((2/lambda)^m*lambda^(2k)), which tends to zero since
lambda>2. No numerical evaluation or recurrence replay is used.

## 4. Actual retired dynamics and screen I: the accumulated charge

Index the actual final suffix H_(m-1) by t=0,...,N,
N=2^(m-1)-1. Its input x(0) is the result of EVERY earlier step(2.5).
The last excess evolves as D_(k+1)-delta times half the mass already
read. It remains at least delta, and Q_k=0 by the output-isolation
property. The fixed function governing the first k coordinates is

    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                     +1/2 Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k).    (4.1)

The retired update is still(2.5), with Q_0=Q_k=0, and
P_k=clip(x_k-A_k,0,C_beta*sigma_k). Write

    r_i=x_i-A_i,
    z_i=beta*(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1), i<k,
    U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i for i<k, U_k=P_k,
    kappa=1+beta^2, theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta).

In the cone coordinates P=U+beta*Q, the exact maximization formula is

    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta*Q)^2+Q^2]/2:
      0<=Q<=sigma_i,
      0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta*Q}.                    (4.2)

Hence U_i>=0. If r_i<=0 then U_i=0 and

    Q_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i),
    Phi_i=Hub_(kappa*sigma_i)(z_i)/kappa.                    (4.3)

There are also the unconditional complementary sign facts

    U_i>0 implies x_i>A_i>=ell_i and Q_(i-1)=0;
    Q_i>0 implies x_(i+1)<ell_(i+1) and U_(i+1)=0.          (4.4)

The exact residual equations, including BOTH neighbors, are

    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta*Q_i-Q_(i-1)),
    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)[kappa*Q_i-beta*Q_(i-1)-beta*Q_(i+1)
                                             +beta*U_i-U_(i+1)]. (4.5)

The retained value and the total normalized payoff are exactly

    V_m=2G_ret(x(N))=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x(N)),
    B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2=T_m+B_(n_m)*V_m/R_m^2,
    T_m=B_(n_m)*D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))*R_m^2]
        ->T_infty=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda).                 (4.6)

In the sum, Phi_k means exactly the last horizontal envelope, equal
to the original circular value at the actual state. The limit follows
from(3.6)--(3.9) and (lambda-2)^2=lambda^(-2). In particular
T_infty<5/64<1, using exp(17/4)>16 and2lambda>4.

Screen I assesses the previous sufficient NONNEGATIVE charge

    X_m=C_beta*sum_i sigma_i*(r_i(N))_+
         +(1/2)sum_t h_t sum_(i<k) sigma_i
                                     [theta*U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)]. (4.7)

The elementary cap estimate for its flux part is only

    X_flux <= (C_beta*S_(m-1)/2)
                   sum_(i<k) sigma_i*(theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i+1)).

The RIGHT SIDE after normalization has order lambda^(2k), by the
positive limiting ratio sigma_2/sigma_1 and(3.6)--(3.9). It does not
vanish or supply the required strict constant. This is a failure of
that upper comparison; it is NOT a lower bound on actual X_m.

Signed coordinate budgets do not automatically improve it. Define
H_i^U=(1/2)sum_t h_t U_i(t) and H_i^Q=(1/2)sum_t h_t Q_i(t),
with H_0^Q=H_k^Q=0. Then

    x_i(0)-x_i(N)=H_i^U+beta*H_i^Q-H_(i-1)^Q.              (4.8)

The telescoping positive weights that eliminate every H_i^Q are
proportional to beta^(i-1), since their adjacent ratio must be beta.
The bulk weights in(4.7) have adjacent ratios asymptotic to lambda-2,
with different harmless endpoint factors. Explicitly their coefficients
on H_i^U are theta*sigma_1, theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i-1) for
2<=i<k, and sigma_(k-1) at i=k. They are not those telescoping
weights. Converting between the two
can cost ((lambda-2)/beta)^O(k), and the signed endpoint displacement
has not itself been bounded on the required scale.

The crucial actual restriction is stronger than this budget for the
first coordinate. At its own checkpoint x_1=A_1-beta*D_2. Thereafter
x_1 never increases, since Q_0=0 and P_1>=0 in EVERY physical step.
Thus throughout the suffix

    r_1<=-beta*D_2<0, U_1=0.                               (4.9)

This is a property of the initialized trajectory, not invariance of
the entire input slab. Even after dropping U_1 from the cap estimate,
the sigma_1*U_2 charge retains the same lambda^(2k) scale in that
upper bound. The actual barrier will remove the U_2 charge from the FIRST
terminal component by a last-entry argument. We have not proved
that X_m itself fails to vanish, nor supplied a lower bound showing
that it overcounts by a nonvanishing amount. Its status remains open.
Only the unnecessary use of all-time U_2 to bound Phi_1 is eliminated.

## 5. Screen II: quantitative actual terminal bounds at both ends

This is the viable part of the round. A last entry uses an occurrence
of b_j in H_(m-1). Immediately following that occurrence lies an entire
H_j inside the remaining suffix. This property follows by induction
from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), and keeps the original order and enclosing
m. It is used only on samples AFTER the last entry, so the claimed
region persists by the choice of the last entry, not by an assumed
invariance theorem.

### 5.1 First circular component: the apparent source switches off

By(4.9), formula(4.3) applies to component1 at EVERY suffix sample.
The exact scalar residual update is

    z_1^+=z_1-(h/2)(kappa*Q_1-P_2).                        (5.1)

It includes the full P_2=U_2+beta*Q_2. If z_1>kappa*sigma_1,
then Q_1=sigma_1 and

    x_2=beta*x_1-z_1
       <ell_2-beta^2*D_2-kappa*sigma_1<ell_2.

Therefore r_2<0, U_2=0 and P_2=beta*Q_2<=beta*sigma_2.
Define gamma_1=kappa*sigma_1-beta*sigma_2. In this region every
physical step loses at least h*gamma_1/2 in z_1, with no restriction
on h. In contrast, an entry step from z_1<=kappa*sigma_1 can add
at most h*C_beta*sigma_2/2. These are different, justified caps.

Eventually gamma_1>0 and

    d*C_beta*sigma_2<=gamma_1,                              (5.2)

because the ratio of the left side to sigma_1 tends to
(lambda-2)^2<1, whereas gamma_1/sigma_1 tends to1.

If z_1(N)>kappa*sigma_1 and there was an entry during the suffix,
take its last occurrence, at b_j. All later samples remain above the
threshold. Pay the entry with C_beta*sigma_2 and the following H_j
with gamma_1. Every subsequent step has nonnegative loss. Thus

    z_1(N)<=kappa*sigma_1
                      +(b_j*C_beta*sigma_2-gamma_1*S_j)/2
            <=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2,         (5.3)

where(3.5) and(5.2) pay every large bridge.

The case of no entry must use the ACTUAL full prehistory. Let
S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1) be the mass before the final suffix. From
(2.5), x_1(0)<=1 and x_2(0)>=-C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.
Hence

    z_1(0)<=beta+C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.                    (5.4)

If all samples from t=0 were above the threshold, summing(5.1)
would give z_1(N)<=z_1(0)-gamma_1*S_(m-1)/2. This is negative
eventually: S_pre/S_(m-1)->lambda-1 and

    C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/(gamma_1*S_(m-1))
                  ->(lambda-2)*(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1,
    beta/(sigma_1*S_(m-1))->0.

So the no-entry case cannot leave a terminal positive residual above
the threshold. If the terminal residual is below the threshold, (5.3)
already bounds its positive part. Consequently, for every sufficiently
large m, on the prescribed actual trajectory,

    (z_1(N))_+<=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2
               <=2sigma_1,
    0<=Phi_1(x(N))<=2sigma_1^2.                             (5.5)

This is a full terminal bound for that component. No U_2 integral,
independent two-Huber trajectory, or alternative state was substituted.
By(3.10), B_(n_m)*Phi_1(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero.

### 5.2 Last circular component: the small cap mismatch is paid

Write L_k=C_beta*sigma_k for the horizontal gradient cap. The actual
checkpoint formula for the active kth coordinate gives

    r_k(0)=-beta*D_(k+1)<0.                                (5.6)

This statement concerns only coordinate k at its OWN checkpoint; it
makes no assertion about the other retired coordinates. During its
active ordinary block and checkpoint Q_(k-1)=0 by output isolation,
so preceding gradients do not alter this particular formula.

At any suffix sample with r_k>L_k, x_k>ell_k, whence Q_(k-1)=0
and P_k=L_k. Thus r_k loses h*L_k/2 there. At an entry from
r_k<=L_k its increase is at most h*sigma_(k-1)/2. If the terminal
residual exceeds L_k, (5.6) guarantees an entry. Its last occurrence
at b_j and its following H_j give

    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(1/2) max_(0<=j<=m-2)
                                      [b_j*sigma_(k-1)-L_k*S_j]_+.
                                                                  (5.7)

No assumption of distance descent is used. Put s=S_(m-2),
T=b_(m-2)/2-1 and Delta_k=d-C_beta*T/M_k. The EXACT ratio
sigma_k/sigma_(k-1)=T/M_k follows from(1.3). Thus(3.5) implies

    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(sigma_(k-1)/2)
                                      [c-1+(Delta_k)_+*s]. (5.8)

To bound the possible positive cap defect, expand it BEFORE estimating:

    d*M_k-C_beta*T
       =d*(M_k-C_beta*s/2)+C_beta*(3-c)/2
                   +(C_beta*d/2)*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).

By(3.3),(3.4), s/M_k<=2/C_beta. Therefore

    (Delta_k)_+*s
       <=(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).            (5.9)

Define the explicit nonnegative error allowance

    A_err,m=c-1+(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3
                                  +d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).

Since Hub_(L_k)(r)<=L_k*(r)_+, the last retained value satisfies

    0<=Phi_k(x(N))
       <=L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m.                 (5.10)

All constants c,d,C_beta remain bounded, and sigma_k,sigma_(k-1)
are at most sigma_1 eventually. Formula(3.10) pays (5.10), including
the changed cap ratio and its 2^(m-2) mass correction. Thus
B_(n_m)*Phi_k(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero uniformly along the frozen
growing policy. The bound is quantitative and applies to every physical
bridge; it does not require exact absorption of the final component.

## 6. Screen III: why the same comparison does not close the middle

This screen was rejected as a complete uniform comparison. The exact
failure is stronger than merely saying that the middle is coupled.

Fix 2<=i<=k-1 and put L_i=C_beta*sigma_i. At a sample with r_i>L_i,
output isolation gives Q_(i-1)=0. Maximizing over P at fixed Q in
(2.1), r_i>L_i forces the UPPER circular boundary, so

    P_i=sigma_i*p(Q_i/sigma_i),
    L_i-P_i=sigma_i*[sqrt(1+2beta)
               -sqrt((1+beta)^2-(beta+Q_i/sigma_i)^2)].     (6.1)

Unlike at i=k, this cap loss need not be zero. Concavity of p gives

    0<=L_i-P_i<=(C_beta-beta)*Q_i.                          (6.2)

After the last entry into r_i>L_i, let s_entry be its step index.
As long as that terminal excursion persists, its exact signed loss is

    r_i(N)=r_i(s_entry+1)
                   -(L_i/2) sum_(t>s_entry) h_t
                   +D_i^cap(s_entry),
    D_i^cap(s_entry)=(1/2)sum_(t>s_entry)h_t*(L_i-P_i(t)).   (6.3)

This is a tail-specific cap loss, not the all-time source X_m. The
entry is bounded by L_i+b_j*sigma_(i-1)/2. Its following H_j pays
the full horizontal-cap part exactly as in(5.7). However D_i^cap
has not been paid. If Q_i>0, formula(4.4) does give U_(i+1)=0,
but the next coordinate then follows

    x_(i+1)^+=x_(i+1)+(h/2)(Q_i-beta*Q_(i+1)).              (6.4)

Thus bounding the accumulated Q_i in(6.2) requires its actual
downstream negative excursions and the NEXT neighboring gradient.
Deleting beta*Q_(i+1), or assuming a frozen output, is not legitimate.
When Q_i=0, later horizontal motion of component i+1 can recreate a
negative output at a large bridge. No entry-and-persistence statement
for a fixed quadratic core or a fixed face has been proved here.

There are two explicit quantitative failures of a cap-only closure.

First, even the always-full-cap idealization cannot pay the available
incoming horizontal estimate for a middle coordinate. The full
prehistory bound from(2.5) is

    r_i(0)<=sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2-A_i, i>=2.                 (6.5)

Dropping -A_i and comparing its leading coefficient to L_i*S_(m-1)/2
gives the limiting ratio

    sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/(C_beta*sigma_i*S_(m-1))
                   ->(lambda-1)/(lambda-2)=2+sqrt(2)>1.    (6.6)

The bound in(6.5) is not the actual value, and does not show that an
always-positive horizontal excursion occurs. It shows why one cannot
discard that branch from a last-entry proof without a stronger actual
prehistory estimate. The endpoint proofs avoided this: the first used
its permanent negative input, and the last used its own exact negative
incoming residual. Neither argument applies automatically to a middle
input after all later checkpoints.

Keeping the omitted A_i does not repair this particular comparison:
A_i=sigma_(i-1)*y_(i-1)+sigma_i*y_i/beta, so(3.2),(3.8)
give A_i/(sigma_(i-1)*S_(m-1))->0 uniformly. Thus the positive
leading allowance in(6.6) survives that exact static threshold.

Second, the best state-independent lower bound for P_i on r_i>L_i
from this same circular geometry is its infimum beta*sigma_i. To see
why a larger fixed lower bound is unavailable, fix r>L_i and q in(0,1).
For q sufficiently close to1 put

    t=(r-sigma_i*p(q))/(p(q)-beta)>0,
    w=sigma_i*q+t*(q+beta).

At the centered point(r,-w), the displacement from sigma_i*v(q) is
t*(p(q)-beta,-q-beta), an outward circular normal. Hence the actual
projection for the SAME envelope is sigma_i*v(q). Letting q approach1
gives P_i approaching beta*sigma_i while r remains fixed above L_i.
Other coordinates can be chosen with the permanent x_1 bound intact.
These are alternative states of the unchanged function, NOT states
proved reachable from initialization. Their purpose is only to test a
uniform cap-floor lemma, not to refute trajectory-specific control.

Using that valid floor in a last-entry comparison leaves the positive
coefficient

    d*sigma_(i-1)-beta*sigma_i.

For i=2 it is a fixed positive fraction of sigma_1 eventually, by
(3.8) and beta->0. The entry at level m-2 in the maximum of the
comparison therefore leaves an allowance of order
sigma_1*S_(m-2). Multiplying the horizontal-value allowance by
C_beta*sigma_2 and the original benchmark gives order lambda^(2k),
by(3.6)--(3.9). This comparison does not even give a bounded normalized
upper estimate, let alone one below1-T_infty. This is the scale of a
FAILED UPPER BOUND, not an attained terminal lower bound.

The cap-only method is therefore rejected without pretending that
(6.3) is a closed recursion. A viable replacement must simultaneously
pay the actual incoming middle residuals and the ordered cap losses
D_i^cap, or directly bound the complete actual middle value. Both
neighbors must be retained in that estimate. No universal distance
descent or large-step Markov positivity was assumed: expansion of
distance would retain h*(2-h) times the gradient-square term, with
its negative sign for h>2. The present endpoint proofs instead pay
each large step through its following physical subtree.

## 7. Exact new handoff and limits of the result

Let

    V_mid,m=sum_(i=2)^(k-1) Phi_i(x(N)),
    epsilon_m=(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*
       [2sigma_1^2+L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m].

Every quantity uses the same unchanged static function and its actual
full-prehistory trajectory. The jointly derived estimates prove, at
the UNVERIFIED DRAFT level,

    0<=epsilon_m->0,
    T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m
       <=B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2
       <=T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m+epsilon_m.             (7.1)

This is a quantitative removal of two terminal contributions from
the comparison, not physical deletion of either component. They
remain in the actual gradient throughout. One CANNOT repeat the
endpoint argument by renaming component2 as component1: U_2 has not
been shown zero, and its incoming Q_1 has not been removed. Similarly,
component k-1 continues to have its original moving output and Q_(k-1).

The remaining actual quantity is V_mid,m for k-2 growing middle
components. No bound below1-T_infty for its normalized limsup is
established, and no normalized lower witness is established. The
defect D_i^cap and the incoming r_i(0) are precise unpaid mechanisms
in the failed third comparison, not a claimed substitute theorem.
We have also NOT decided whether B_(n_m)*X_m/R_m^2 tends to zero.

Accordingly the frozen policy remains OPEN. The original question
over all words and all horizons remains OPEN even if this policy were
eventually decided. The assigned one round ends with the quantitative
endpoint progress and the explicit middle comparison failure; it does
not automatically renew the allocation.

## 8. Retrieval, resources, and persistence

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,
search-math-results. The formal gate required by the usual worker
promotion workflow was explicitly disallowed by TASK.md and current
guidance, so every assertion in this report remains unverified.

Full shared evidence84a4435a88a9f487 and a9423b621329882d was read,
as was obstacle03228f06cb53bad3. The first broad recall displays were

sections. Fact search found no accepted result for this coupled

Retrieval stage: mature_subproblem. Search mode: repair, directed at

DC, small-step self-contraction and line-search results. Their

located the existing Silver and composition literature, but supplied
no justified transfer to the perturbed word. No external theorem was
imported, and no new technical literature branch was opened. In
particular a smaller effective smoothness was not assumed to make

Three comparisons were screened: (I) accumulated/signed flux;
(II) state-dependent terminal last entries, fully developed at the
two endpoints; (III) extension to middle components via cap loss,
rejected as a complete cap-only uniform comparison. No fourth
comparison, construction repair or additional checkpoint was used.

Mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
executed recurrences0; solvers0; benchmark DP0; formal submissions0;

Only administrative text reads/writes, memory operations, retrieval,

# Independent analytical audit of the complete actual two-family candidate

Worker: max4. One allocated audit round, after completed61, completed
ceiling62 / attempted ceiling63. Status: ANALYTICAL AUDIT ONLY; no
verifier verdict, accepted fact, or original-target completion.

## 1. Verdict, frozen source, and exact scope

**The full stated L=1 candidate survived this independent analytical
attack.** I found no substantive mathematical error or missing estimate
in its claimed two-family theorem. In particular, the new Section9A
discrete turning estimate and its deletion comparison survive changes
in adjacent levels and incoming-child sizes. PASS below means that this
analytical attack found no defect; it does not mean verifier acceptance.

There is one notation clarification outside the fixed-L=1 calculation:
the general-L scaling sentence must use the normalized payoff with L in
the denominator. This is separated from the theorem audit in
`notation_recommendation.md`. It does not alter a function, selection,
parameter domain, or constant in the assigned theorem. There is no
substantive repair recommendation and no iterative repair round.

Frozen source, read in its entirety (all 1223 lines):

    shared finding bfff4aa387a9d818, author max2
    /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/bfff4aa387a9d818.md
    53449 bytes
    SHA256 bb0fb6c31e6f2711493fec2558266e896b5c0c42a8f70c76e83c4bcf7d767ca2

No source bytes were changed. Component drafts are provenance, not
accepted premises. The sole mathematical predecessor used by the frozen
candidate is accepted fact04219554e41d9750, for the original benchmark
and its certified saved interval table. I read its relevant statement,
ray proof, table-certificate scope, and the referenced saved row.

The physical word is defined, with one enclosing pair (m,tau), by

    m>=1536, 0<tau<=1/4,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, n_m=2^m-1.

Every child keeps these same enclosing parameters. A chronological
selection writes the original word as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),
    s_i=sum block_i.

There is ONE common pair kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2 for the whole selection.
It may depend arbitrarily on m; different stages do not get separate
pairs. Ordinary blocks retain all physical entries. The candidate's
general geometric construction also permits zeros and arbitrarily large
ordinary steps.

At advertised smoothness L=1, the payoff is

    E=2(f(x_(n_m))-min f)/dist(e_0,argmin f)^2,
    H_base=1/(1+2*S_m).

The audit covers every eligible ordered selection of every count. The
claimed bounds are MAIN nonempty E<(441/500)H_base and SOURCE nonempty
E<(9801/10000)H_base. The main and combined optima include their empty
selection and equal H_base; the source-only optimum is
1/[2(1+S_m)]. The original-B deficits and the fixed-tau limit are checked
below. Stage-dependent pairs, retained-circle components, unrestricted
W_n, and the original minimax question are outside this candidate.
