## 3. Geometry, chronology, and true radius

Put theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon) and

    C=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),
    R(theta)=C*exp(-kappa*theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)).

The supporting normal is n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa*t(theta). For
Delta=phi-theta, the derivative of
exp(-kappa*Delta)(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta) is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa*Delta)sin Delta. It changes sign in the correct
direction at0 for the entire permitted angle interval. This validates
support against both earlier and later arc points, not merely adjacent
sampled slopes. Support against0 is immediate from its smaller value.

For one local chronological ordinary list, the backward endpoint is
(epsilon,-1). The source's cap test is

    y<=kappa*(u+(alpha-1)*C).

If it holds, mu=u+(alpha-1)C>=y/kappa>0 and

    r_old-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-y).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. The resulting state satisfies
y_old<=kappa*(u_old-C), which proves persistence of every preceding
cap even for alpha=0. If the cap test fails, the root in(6) exists
uniquely: its left side increases strictly with theta, equals1 at the
current projected angle, and is below1-alpha at0. The root is allowed
to equal the current angle for a zero step. Its normal coefficient is
nonnegative because the state's polar angle is no smaller than the
root angle.

The uncapped normal representation is

    r=R(theta)*[(1+nu)e(theta)+kappa*nu*t(theta)], nu>=0.

The exact relation(8) proves the two stated bounds for nu. For
kappa<1 its exponential comparison telescopes; for kappa>=1 the
coefficient A_D is at most1 and the B_D contributions sum to at most
theta_sp/kappa. Thus the proof does not extrapolate the singular-looking
first branch through kappa=1. Its limit at1 is theta_sp, agreeing with
the second branch there.

For every local output P,Q, the pointwise inequalities p>=epsilon*q,
p<=C, p+q>=1 imply

    P>=epsilon*Q, 0<=P<=C*s_loc,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2 >= (1+epsilon+s_loc)^2/2.

The derivative of q locates its maximum at the endpoint when
kappa<=epsilon and at arctan(1/kappa) otherwise, giving the stated
qmax including the equality case. A negative horizontal normal can
occur only when p<kappa*q. Since u>=epsilon, it gives
u-p>=epsilon-kappa*qmax>=-N_sp. Otherwise u>=p. This checks the
correction used by the main threshold for all kappa, including kappa>1.

Both endpoint planes are present in every finite local maximum. Every
other slope satisfies

    (p,-q)=((p-epsilon*q)/C)*(C,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),

with nonnegative coefficients. This proves the complete zero wedge,
not just a convenient subset of minimizers. Conditions(11) make the
pre-activation quadrant and the entire post-checkpoint vertical ray
part of this wedge. The proximal subgradient test uses all sampled
planes through the supporting-normal inequality just checked.

For the SOURCE sum of local envelopes, the coefficient1/2 is essential:
the actual h_t update implements alpha=h_t/2. Its next amplitude is
D'_i*sigma'_i*b_i/2. Its shifts use Q_i, and its final hinge has the same
coefficient1/2. The derived exact terminal numerator is
(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)]. All preceding components are at minima and all
future components are at minima along the stated physical trajectory.
Each coordinate belongs to at most two half-weighted 1-smooth terms,
including the terminal hinge. This proves global 1-smoothness. The
resulting global interpolation inequality applies to every ordered
pair, the terminal sample, and every minimizer; physical vectors also
give a positive semidefinite Gram matrix without an added assumption.

The SOURCE minimum set is exactly(19). Let
z_bar=(c'_(0,1),c'_(0,2),0,...) and w=e_0-z_bar. Its first-row normal
representation is

    w=sigma'_0*(P_0-epsilon*Q_0)*e_0
       +sigma'_0*(1+Q_0)*(epsilon*e_0-e_1).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. Every point x in the full minimum
set satisfies <w,x-z_bar><=0 even if z_bar violates later constraints.
Hence

    ||e_0-x||^2
      =||w||^2+||x-z_bar||^2-2<w,x-z_bar> >=||w||^2.

This is exactly the direction needed for an upper payoff bound. It
does not reuse the larger, generally invalid one-source-checkpoint
radius. With the half-step mass, ||w||^2 is at least
(sigma'_0)^2*(s_0+2)^2/8. Cancellation with the exact first transfer
gives precisely(23), including its factor1 rather than an extra2 or1/2.

The exact all-row source identity(20) is also consistent: each
nonnegative independent-row multiplier gives a dual distance lower
bound, and the true projection has a normal generated by active rows.
An independent active subcollection attains the distance. The argument
does not require every maximizing candidate center to be feasible.

For the MAIN envelope, ordinary steps use their full physical values.
The proximal first coordinate is at least U_i>=0 because
epsilon*(b_i-1)>=N_sp. Its proximal second coordinate is between0 and
sigma_i*(1+Q_i). These inequalities respectively preserve the previous
zero wedge and the next pre-activation wedge. Farther terms vanish as
well. The current local slope is therefore an active subgradient of
the maximum defining J. The checkpoint leaves next amplitude
D_i*sigma_i*(b_i-1) and shift D_i*sigma_i*(1+Q_i), as stated.

Here the first center z_bar IS feasible: its second coordinate is
ell_1, so the next local input is(0,0); every subsequent input is in its
pre-activation wedge. The same nonnegative normal proves exact closest
distance, giving(43). This difference between the two realizations is
preserved throughout the proof. The terminal gradients and values follow
from D_k-(u+1)*delta>=0 for every tail prefix mass u<=s_k.

## 4. Actual upper functionals and the high-C/source comparisons

Use r_h=c(c-2), v_h=beta/r_h, d_j=a*c^j, and u_j=d_j/M_j. Direct
substitution in the mass recurrence gives

    M_j=v_h*c^j+(1-v_h)*2^j,
    0<v_h<1, 1<=M_j<=c^j, a<=u_j<=c-2<21/50.

Every globally minimum selected level j has both full H_j children
unselected. Its adjacent ordinary sums satisfy s,t>=M_j-1, including
first/last selections and ties. Removing the checkpoint reclassifies
its full physical value b=1+d_j as ordinary. No trajectory is preserved
by this operation; only an already proved upper functional is compared.

For SOURCE, the remaining denominators in(23) are exact transfer
denominators C*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i. The first deletion reduces to(28).
At s=t=M-1 it has the difference(29) when C>=r_h, with first coefficient
C-u(2+u)>=0 and the other terms positive. The interior difference is
(32); q=2A-C>=3C makes its remaining coefficients positive. If C<r_h,
Section8 proves A>2*C*M_max, and(30),(33) prove these same deletions.
The last deletion has ratio at most5/8 by(34)-(35), with no dichotomy
needed. All monotonicity reductions use positive derivative signs over
the original domain s,t>=M-1, rather than at only one selected word.

The SOURCE dichotomy is valid on the full parameter domain. If C<r_h,
then epsilon<1/16, theta_sp>23/16 and kappa<1/768. In particular kappa<1
has been proved before using its exponential T branch. The exponent
X_sp=(1-kappa^2)*theta_sp/kappa exceeds m, and

    1+(1+kappa)*T=(exp(X_sp)-kappa)/(1-kappa)>=exp(X_sp).

This yields A>16*C*exp(m)>2*C*M_max. It does not imply any corresponding
lower bound by A for an actual MAIN transfer denominator.

For MAIN, differentiating p+q/epsilon shows either monotone decrease
or a single interior maximum. Its minimum is therefore the smaller
endpoint C_floor=min(C,A_floor), where A_floor=epsilon+epsilon^(-1).
Consequently the ACTUAL denominator is at least C_floor*s_i+A_floor.
When C>=r_h, C_floor>=r_h and A_floor>=C_floor. The three deletion
calculations in Section9 use those actual lower bounds with the
exceptional first radius bounded by(s_0+1)^2/2. Their first/interior
differences have nonnegative leading coefficient
C_floor-u(2+u), and the last ratio is at mostu^2*(3+2u)<=1.

After either source or main deletion reaches one checkpoint, X=1+s
and Y=1+t are independently at least M. The relevant ratio decreases
in each. SOURCE eligibility gives d>=9, hence

    (d+1)/(M+1)<=10*u/(9+u)<70/157<9/20,

and its ratio is less than 2*(121/50)*(9/20)^2=9801/10000.
MAIN eligibility gives d>=4 and M>200/21>19/2. Its ratio is at most

    2*u^2*[2+u+(1+u)/(2*M-1)] < 2*u^2*(5/2)<441/500.

All strict comparisons hold at the eligibility thresholds themselves.

## 5. Attack of Section9A: discrete angles and caps

This is the central new interface. Assume C<r_h and write
eta_turn=999/1000 and Delta_C=r_h-C. The proven bounds include

    epsilon<1/16, theta_sp>23/16, kappa<1/768,
    C<769/768, 0<Delta_C<2/m<1/4,
    kappa*m*theta_sp<44/25, log c<9/10.

Run the actual backward process on one complete H_j, j<=m. Immediately
before a step alpha, let y_mass=1 plus the actual mass strictly after
it within that child. A step of level ell has its full right H_ell
after it, so

    alpha/y_mass=(1+d_ell)/y_mass
        <=c-2+1/y_mass<3/2.

For x=alpha/y_mass, log(1+x)>=2*x/(2+x). Thus

    x<=(c/2+1/(2*y_mass))*log(1+x).

Moreover

    log(1+x)/y_mass <= x/y_mass
       =(1+x)*(1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha))
       <=(5/2)*(1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha)).

Both expressions telescope in the exact reverse order. This proves
Hharm_j=sum alpha/y_mass <= (c/2)*log M_j+5/4 without a flow or an
order-free model. The empty child is included.

Let the pre-update state have radius rho_loc and polar angle phi_pol,
and let theta be the slope angle actually added. Set gamma=phi_pol-theta.
It lies in[0,pi/2) in both branches. The uncapped root equation gives

    sin gamma=kappa*cos gamma
                 +kappa*(alpha-1)*R(theta)/rho_loc.

The cap test gives exactly this inequality with <= instead of equality
and R(theta)=C. Thus there is no omitted sign or branch in(46e).
The crude radius bound rho_loc>=y_mass/sqrt(2), already supplied by
p+q>=1, gives sin gamma<(11/2)*kappa<1/100. Therefore
cos gamma>eta_turn. Projection of the exact vector addition onto the
old radial direction then proves

    rho_new>=rho_loc+eta_turn*alpha,
    rho_loc>=eta_turn*y_mass

by induction starting from the endpoint radius sqrt(1+epsilon^2)>=1.
This induction is cap-inclusive.

For z=alpha*R(theta)/rho_loc, the exact polar-angle decrement delta is

    tan delta=z*sin gamma/(1+z*cos gamma).

The branch-independent inequality above gives sin gamma<=kappa*(1+z).
Since 1+z*cos gamma>=cos gamma*(1+z),

    delta<=kappa*z/cos gamma
      <=(C/eta_turn^2)*kappa*alpha/y_mass
      <=(101/100)*kappa*alpha/y_mass.

The post-update state has theta>=phi_new-kappa: for an uncapped state
this follows from arctan(kappa*nu/(1+nu))<=kappa; at a cap it follows
from y_new<=kappa*(u_new-C). Thus the inequality remains usable even
at a putative first cap. Summing the actual decrements gives

    theta>=theta_sp-kappa*((101/100)*Hharm_j+1)
      >theta_sp-1221/(625*theta_sp)>1/16.

The quantitative margins check: the bracket divided by m is less
than111/100 using j<=m, log c<9/10, c<121/50 and m>=1536; and
(23/16)*(22/16)=253/128>1221/625. Only now are caps ruled out on a
complete H_j. They are not ruled out on arbitrary preceding blocks.

Every slope processed on H_j therefore has q>1/32 and p+q>=1. Since
epsilon^(-1)-1>15,

    p+q/epsilon>47/32,
    P(H_j)+Q(H_j)/epsilon-C*S_j>=S_j/4.

This is a lower estimate on actual discrete outputs. At j=0 the
statement is weak equality, so no positive surplus is invented for
an empty child.

## 6. Attack of Section9A: arbitrary blocks and changing adjacent levels

Let lev_i be the level of selected checkpoint i. For i>=1 set
j_i=min(lev_(i-1),lev_i). At position t, the tree level is the exponent
of2 in t+1. Two positions of level at least j_i differ by at least
2^(j_i), and the final 2^(j_i)-1 positions before the later checkpoint
are exactly H_(j_i). The previous selected checkpoint is no later than
the position immediately before that child. Thus the trailing child
actually lies inside block_i, with unchanged enclosing parameters.

The backward construction processes this child first from the same
endpoint(epsilon,-1). Its contribution is exactly the one just bounded.
Earlier steps may have different angles or may cap. For every one of
them p+q/epsilon>=C, because C<A_floor. Hence

    P_i+Q_i/epsilon-C*s_i>=S_(j_i)/4.

The constant reserve A_floor-C>1/4 and Delta_C<1/4 now imply

    M_i^tr>=C*(s_i+1)+Delta_C*M_(j_i)=:D_i^cmp.

This retains the actual max in M_i^tr; it only lowers the denominator.
It does not replace M_i^tr by the much larger fixed A_sp. Inserting
this inequality into the exact payoff(43) gives Psi_low(46k).

For deletion, choose a globally minimum selected level j, and set
M=M_j, d=d_j, u=d/M, X=1+s>=M, Y=1+t>=M. The merged block mass plus
one is X+Y+d.

For a FIRST removal, the next comparison denominator is C*Y+Delta_C*M.
The required inequality is

    d*(X+Y+d)<=X*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).

Its difference increases in X and Y since C>u. At X=Y=M it is
M^2*(r_h-u*(2+u))>=0.

For an INTERIOR removal, both old comparison denominators use M_j.
The newly adjacent selected levels can be larger. Their new complete
trailing child is contained in the merged block, so its mass plus one
is at most X+Y+d. Consequently the NEW denominator is at most
r_h*(X+Y+d). It is therefore sufficient to compare

    d*r_h*(X+Y+d)
       <=(C*X+Delta_C*M)*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).

The X derivative of the difference is at least
r_h*M*(C-u)>0, and similarly for Y. At X=Y=M the difference is
r_h*M^2*(r_h-u*(2+u))>=0. This explicitly pays for every increase of
the new adjacent level. It never preserves the old P,Q or old M_j
for the new block.

For a LAST removal, the local selected-to-deleted ratio is at most

    d^2*(1+2*(s+b+t))/((s+1)^2*(1+2*t)).

It decreases in s,t. At their lower bounds it is
u^2*((4+2*u)*M-1)/(2*M-1), bounded by u^2*(3+2*u)<=1. The first
cross-multiplied slack is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. The last comparison uses
u<=1/2. Ties in the minimum level do not affect any of these arguments.

Every remaining subset has its own recalculated comparison functional.
The deletions terminate at one checkpoint, where the Section9 bound
uses only d>=4, u<21/50 and X,Y>=M. It does not require C>=r_h there.
Thus the low-C proof closes the entire missing band, with the same
441/500 constant. There is no finite-count restriction hidden in it.

## 7. Boundary attacks, benchmark evidence, and final optima

The source's two-checkpoint test epsilon=kappa=1/2, b_0=b_1=10 and zero
ordinary mass correctly has squared true radius5*sigma^2/4 and actual
payoff250*sigma^2. Formula(23) gives625*sigma^2 in the required upper
direction. It does not use the invalid50*sigma^2 estimate from extending

The reordered local lists(A_*,1) and(1,A_*) in Section10 have the same
mass but respectively Q=q_*>0 and Q=0. Their written cap tests and
actual projections agree. Nothing in the deletion proofs equates these
outputs. A huge immediately capped step likewise has P=C*alpha,Q=0;
all inequalities used outside the complete-child surplus still hold.
These attacks distinguish the permitted arbitrary-prefix caps from the
impossible caps on a whole H_j in the small-C band.

For the benchmark I used fact04219554e41d9750, not a new recurrence
calculation. Its Section8 certifies every row of the table

    /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv
    SHA256 2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.

The finite physical mass formula is

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
      =2*v_h*(1+tau/m)^m+2*(1-v_h)*(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m).

The stated bounds v_h>255/256, lambda^(-m)<1/256 and
(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4 give a value greater than5/2 at tau=1/4,
and greater than12/5 throughout[1/5,1/4]. Combined with Z_1<237/100

All arithmetic here is a written symbolic comparison of the displayed
constants; no arithmetic program or interval-table replay was executed.

For k=0, the MAIN hinge gives exactly H_base. The SOURCE half-weighted
hinge gives exactly1/[2(1+S_m)], whose ratio to H_base is
1-1/(2*M_m). Since M_m>=2^m>=32, this ratio is at least63/64>9801/10000.
Thus every nonempty source selection lies below its own k=0 member,
not merely below Huber. Every nonempty main selection lies below
Huber by the uniform441/500 factor. These uniform pointwise bounds
establish the stated suprema even when the parameter pair and selection

For each fixed tau, v_h tends1 and the displayed physical mass divided
by lambda^m tends2*exp(tau). The accepted ray tendsZ_1. Exact family

limits are Z_1/(2*exp(tau)); for the source-only value its ratio to
Huber tends1. No exchange of a parameter supremum with a limit occurs.

# Consolidated notation recommendation; frozen source unchanged

Source: bfff4aa387a9d818, 53449 bytes,
SHA256 bb0fb6c31e6f2711493fec2558266e896b5c0c42a8f70c76e83c4bcf7d767ca2.

The independent analytical audit found no substantive repair needed
for the assigned L=1 two-family theorem. This file records the sole
editorial clarification and does not propose a changed theorem or
authorize another review/submission round.

In Section1, E is displayed with denominator dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 and
is explicitly introduced at advertised smoothness1. The next scaling
sentence says that multiplying f by L and using h_t/L preserves E.
Section12 repeats the scaling claim. Taken literally with the displayed
L-free definition, the numerator and hence E are multiplied by L.

Make the intended normalized extension explicit:

    E_L(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*dist(x_0,argmin f)^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t).

Then use the sentence:

    For any L>0, replacing the constructed function f by L*f and
    using the physical update h_t/L preserves every point and gives
    E_L(L*f,h)=E_1(f,h).

With the original L=1 iteration notation instead, the literal identity
is E_1(L*f,h/L)=L*E_1(f,h), where the formula for E_1 is viewed just as
a raw gap/distance ratio. For example the candidate's own main k=0
Huber member has E_1(f,h)=1/(1+2*S_m); doubling the function and halving
the physical updates preserves the points and doubles that raw ratio.
Division by the advertised L restores the invariant normalized payoff.

This clarification is outside the fixed-L=1 comparisons. It requires
no change to the parameters, eligibility thresholds, static functions,
closest radii, finite constants, or family optima. No source file was
edited and no repaired candidate was subjected to another audit.

# Exact absorption of the actual two-Huber ruler tail

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit was
allocated or called; there is no new fact_id. A mathematical statement in
this document is a candidate with its full proof, not verifier-accepted
truth. The original all-word/all-horizon minimax problem remains OPEN.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; one allocated round after
completed56, completed ceiling57 / attempted ceiling58. Assignment TASK.md;
guidance5f75454952cf10c5; plan8ad15c87bcebe740. No automatic renewal.
Latest guidance reread7b252271b54d4ab9 leaves this assignment unchanged.

Frozen source candidate: shared finding4c4a21a484564bdc, complete public
text research/shared_evidence/4c4a21a484564bdc.md, 39298 bytes,
SHA25607140ce4efba7c8237ae2dfff447fe2afbadbbe94154a82db82d504640f2b79b.
It was read in full. It is UNVERIFIED and is not an accepted predecessor.
All construction, smoothness, trajectory, local-bound and radius ingredients
used here are proved jointly below. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used
ONLY for the original benchmark ray limit and its stated enclosure.

## 1. The resulting theorem and its exact scope

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), d=lambda-2=1/lambda. For integer m>=32 and

    1/5 <= tau <= 1/4,       8/m <= beta <= 1/4,

use the SAME enclosing parameters throughout the physical word:

    eta_m=1-tau/m, c_m=lambda(1+tau/m), a_m=eta_m/c_m,
    b_j=1+a_m c_m^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, J=m-2, L=m-1.             (1.1)

Here beta is beta_arc, not eta_m. Select ONLY the first occurrences of
levels m-2 and m-1. The entire physical word is exactly

    H_m=(H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1)).                (1.2)

Use the direct circular construction in Sections2--4, unchanged from the
frozen candidate. This is an actual convex1-smooth function F on R^3,
initialized at e_1, and all steps are physical GD steps x^+=x-h grad F.
Write E_m(tau,beta)=2F(x_(n_m))/dist(e_1,argmin F)^2.

There is one finite integer m_0 such that for EVERY m>=m_0 and EVERY
(tau,beta) in the displayed rectangle, the two old circular components
are both exactly zero at the actual FINAL iterate. In fact the old pair
enters its moving flat interval by the end of H_(m-1) and thereafter is
stationary. No tail step, old component or radius term is discarded.
Consequently its full actual payoff is exactly

    E_m(tau,beta)=D_3^2/[2(1+S_(m-1)) R_true^2],      (1.3)

with D_3 and R_true defined in Sections3--4.

Let B_n be the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark in Section8, and let the
positive finite Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m be supplied by accepted
fact04219554e41d9750. Set C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta). Uniformly on the
same moving parameter rectangle,

    B_(n_m) E_m(tau,beta)
       - Z_1 exp(-tau)/(2 lambda C_beta^4) -> 0.      (1.4)

In particular, for every FIXED beta in(0,1/4],

    lim_m B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)
       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda C_beta^4)<7/10.       (1.5)

The same argument controls the explicitly specified nonfixed scaling
beta_m=8/m. More precisely,

    lim_m sup_(8/m<=beta<=1/4) B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)
       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda)<7/10.               (1.6)

Thus the supremum in(1.6) is below7/10 for every sufficiently large m.
Over the full tau rectangle the corresponding limit of the supremum is
Z_1 exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)<3/4, also giving an eventual uniform deficit.

These are upper bounds on this ONE unchanged three-coordinate witness
family. They are NOT upper bounds on W_n, and do not settle the original
all-n/all-nonnegative-word minimax question. They do not control beta<8/m
(for example beta=1/m), other checkpoint selections or counts, or other
static functions. The smaller-beta region remains open in this report.
No positive pre-tail limit has been added to the terminal payoff.

## 2. The actual circular body and finite local construction

Fix beta in(0,1/4]. Define

    R_circ=1+beta, C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),
    p(q)=beta+sqrt(R_circ^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K_ctr=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).           (2.1)

The circular center is(beta,beta); its disk contains0 because
2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. Thus its radial normals support this convex hull
on the displayed arc. The endpoints are(C_beta,0) and(beta,-1).
For every(p,-q) in K_ctr,

    0<=p<=C_beta, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta*q,
    (p,0) belongs to K_ctr.                         (2.2)

For any compact convex K containing0 put

    E_K(r)=max_(g in K){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}.             (2.3)

Completing the square identifies its unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection Pi_K(r). Its characterization is

    <r-Pi_K(r),g-Pi_K(r)> <=0 for every g in K.       (2.4)

Adding this inequality at two points shows
||Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s)||^2<=<Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s),r-s>.
The projection is therefore1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing
expressions in(2.3) gives the derivative Pi_K(r) and a remainder bounded
by ||s-r||^2. Hence E_K is convex, nonnegative, differentiable and1-smooth.
Its zero set is the polar cone of K: positive support in a direction
produces a positive value using a sufficiently small multiple of that
supporting vector, while nonpositive support makes0 the maximizer.
The cone of K_ctr is generated by its two nonzero endpoints, since

    (p,-q)=((p-beta*q)/C_beta)(C_beta,0)+q(beta,-1).

For sigma>0 it follows that

    E_(sigma K_ctr)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U<=V.   (2.5)

Use the one-sided Huber function

    Hub_delta(z)=0 for z<=0;
                 z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta;
                 delta*z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta.    (2.6)

It is convex and1-smooth, with derivative min(delta,max(z,0)).

Here is a finite construction for the ACTUAL ordinary block H_J, with
its ordered steps h_1,...,h_N, N=2^J-1. Set alpha_i=h_i/2. Construct
r_i=(u_i,-y_i) backward from

    r_N=(beta,-1), q_N=1.

For each i from N down to1 choose the unique q_(i-1) in[0,1) satisfying

    (q_(i-1)+beta)/(p(q_(i-1))-beta)
       =max{ beta/(C_beta-beta),
               [y_i+(1-alpha_i)beta]
                    /[u_i-(1-alpha_i)beta] },      (2.7)

and set

    r_(i-1)=r_i+alpha_i v(q_(i-1)).                 (2.8)

All our physical steps are positive. The denominator in(2.7) is positive
because u_i>=beta and alpha_i>0. Its left side is continuous and strictly
increasing from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity. Thus every term is defined.
The symbols specify a finite mathematical recursion; it was not executed.

For clarity, the projection checks underlying this construction are as
follows. Inductively u_i>=beta and y_i>=1, and the updates increase both
when read backward. At an interior projected arc point the centered ray
has ratio(y_i+beta)/(u_i-beta). Subtracting alpha_i*beta from its numerator
and adding alpha_i*beta to its denominator can only decrease that ratio.
Thus(2.7) gives q_(i-1)<=q_i. At q_i=1 this upper bound is immediate. At
the capped endpoint q_i=0 the supporting condition is

    y_i<=beta(u_i-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta).             (2.9)

It makes the second entry in the maximum in(2.7) no greater than the first,
so q_(i-1)=0 as well. At an uncapped new point, (2.7)--(2.8) place r_(i-1)
on the outward radial ray of v(q_(i-1)): the cross-product condition is
exactly(2.7), and y_(i-1)>=1>=q_(i-1) gives a radial factor at least1.
The circular normal then proves(2.4). At the capped new point, the maximum
condition instead gives(2.9) for i-1. The displacement from(C_beta,0) is a
nonnegative combination of the circular endpoint normal and(0,1), which
also proves(2.4). The terminal r_N is on K_ctr. We have proved

    Pi_(K_ctr)(r_i)=v(q_i),
    u_i>=beta, 1<=y_i<=y_0, 0<=q_i<=1.             (2.10)

In particular, with u=u_0, y=y_0 and S=S_J,

    u=beta+sum_i alpha_i p(q_(i-1)),
    y=1+sum_i alpha_i q_(i-1),
    u>=beta*y, M=u+y/beta>0.                       (2.11)

The inequality is termwise p>=beta*q. For a general incoming threshold
ell>=0 and amplitude D>0 define

    sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y,
    A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K_ctr)(X-A,Y-ell_out).        (2.12)

This is one fixed finite smooth convex function, zero whenever X<=ell
and Y>=0 by(2.5). Also its OUTPUT derivative is zero whenever Y>=ell_out,
for every X. Indeed replacing a gradient(p,-q) by(p,0) increases the
maximized expression by q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2 if q>0.

The physical ordinary states are

    (X_i,Y_i)=(A+sigma*u_i,ell_out-sigma*y_i).       (2.13)

They start at(ell+D,0), and(2.8)--(2.10) show that each physical step h_i
updates them by -(h_i/2)grad Phi. Each query has X_i>ell and
0<=Y_i<=ell_out-sigma<ell_out. The final local gradient is
sigma(beta,-1). A physical checkpoint b>2, with T=b/2-1, sends it to

    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*sigma*T,ell_out+sigma*T).      (2.14)

This is an exact half-step identity for the original physical b. It is
in the zero set(2.5). Nothing here substitutes a shortened physical word.

## 3. One static global function, every step, and its radius

For our identical ordinary blocks H_J, use the SAME u,y,M in both.
Put

    T_1=b_J/2-1, T_2=b_(J+1)/2-1,
    sigma_1=1/M, D_2=T_1/M,
    sigma_2=D_2/M, D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2,
    ell_2=y/M, ell_3=D_2*y/M,
    A_1=ell_2/beta, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta,
    delta=D_3/(1+S_L).                            (3.1)

For sufficiently large m the two checkpoints exceed2; all the parameters
are then positive. Define, before starting GD,

    Phi_1(X,Y)=E_(sigma_1 K_ctr)(X-A_1,Y-ell_2),
    Phi_2(Y,Z)=E_(sigma_2 K_ctr)(Y-A_2,Z-ell_3),
    F(X,Y,Z)=(Phi_1(X,Y)+Phi_2(Y,Z)
                                      +Hub_delta(Z-ell_3))/2. (3.2)

The function is convex and1-smooth. To check the constant, the linear map
stacking(X,Y),(Y,Z),Z has norm sqrt(2), since its squared output norm is
X^2+2Y^2+2Z^2. The separable sum of the three1-smooth functions has gradient
Lipschitz constant1 on the product space. Composition and the factor1/2
therefore give at most(1/2)*2=1. Every summand is nonnegative and F(0)=0.

At any point write the two projections as(P_1,-Q_1),(P_2,-Q_2), and put
P_3=min(delta,max(Z-ell_3,0)). The exact full update is

    X^+=X-h P_1/2,
    Y^+=Y-h(P_2-Q_1)/2,
    Z^+=Z-h(P_3-Q_2)/2.                            (3.3)

All components in this formula belong to the SAME static function.
During the first H_J and b_J the first pair follows(2.13)--(2.14);
its output is at most ell_2 before the checkpoint. The next component
and final Huber are zero there, so they do not change these updates.
The first checkpoint gives

    X=A_1-beta*D_2, Y=ell_2+D_2, Z=0.

During the next H_J and its root checkpoint b_(J+1), the second pair
follows(2.13)--(2.14), with ell=ell_2,D=D_2. The first component stays
zero in the second ordinary block: its input is fixed at or below A_1,
and its output is at least ell_2. In particular Q_1=0 at every active
query. The final Huber is inactive while the third coordinate is below
ell_3. Therefore the actual state just after the root checkpoint is

    v_in=A_1-beta*D_2,
    w_in=A_2-beta*D_3,
    Z_in=ell_3+D_3.                                (3.4)

This derives the incoming data without importing an unverified trajectory.

The ENTIRE minimum set is the simultaneous zero set of the summands:

    X<=A_1, Y>=beta*X,
    Y<=A_2, Z>=beta*(Y-ell_2),
    Z<=ell_3.                                     (3.5)

Here(2.5) and beta*A_1=ell_2 were used. From(2.11),

    1-A_1=sigma_1*u, ell_2=sigma_1*y,
    0<A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).                           (3.6)

The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0) belongs to(3.5). For any point of(3.5),
if X<0 its squared distance from e_1 is at least1. For 0<=X<=A_1,
its squared distance is at least(1-X)^2+beta^2 X^2, because Y>=beta X.
This quadratic decreases until X=1/(1+beta^2), so it is minimized on
[0,A_1] at A_1. The displayed x_star attains this bound, which is at most1.
Thus the TRUE closest-minimizer squared radius is exactly

    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2
            =sigma_1^2(u^2+y^2)>0.                 (3.7)

This uses every constraint in the actual minimum set. The radius has not
been normalized using a freely chosen more distant minimizer.

Every smooth-convex interpolation row follows from this actual function.
For completeness, subtract <grad F(y),z> from F(z). Its minimum is attained
at y. The1-smooth descent bound at x with displacement
-(grad F(x)-grad F(y)) then yields
F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y> >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
This holds for every ordered pair, including x_star and the terminal sample.
Actual Euclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite.
For general smoothness L_smooth>0, take f=L_smooth F and physical updates
x^+=x-(h/L_smooth)grad f; the same trajectory and normalized payoff result.

## 4. Exact tail dynamics and all terminal terms

Throughout the entire remaining H_L the third-coordinate excess is

    Z-ell_3=D_3-s'*delta/2>=delta,
                         0<=s'<=S_L,              (4.1)

where s' is the physical mass already traversed. This statement is an
exact trajectory identity, justified inductively: it keeps Q_2=0 by the
output-isolation property in Section2, so P_3=delta and the displayed
update continues. In particular the old second component has the exact
horizontal expression

    Phi_2(w,Z)=Hub_(C_beta*sigma_2)(w-A_2).          (4.2)

The first coordinate never increases by(3.3), and hence v<=A_1. For any
such v its projection lies on the sloped segment p=beta*q: for fixed q,
reducing p to beta*q increases the expression being maximized, since
v-A_1<=0 and p>=0. The segment is in K_ctr. Maximization along it gives

    Phi_1(v,w)=Hub_((1+beta^2)*sigma_1)(beta*v-w)/(1+beta^2). (4.3)

Put sigma=sigma_1, kappa_tail=1+beta^2, and cap_ratio=C_beta*D_2.
The exact dynamics for EVERY physical tail step h are therefore

    q=min(sigma,max(beta*v-w,0)/kappa_tail),
    r=min(cap_ratio*sigma,max(w-A_2,0)),
    v^+=v-h*beta*q/2,
    w^+=w+h*(q-r)/2.                               (4.4)

There is a closed flat interval beta*v<=w<=A_2, with actual width

    g=A_2-beta*v,
    g_in=ell_3/beta+beta^2*D_2>0,
    g^+=g+h*beta^2*q/2>=g.                         (4.5)

Thus q and r cannot be positive together. When the pair enters that
interval both vanish, the pair is stationary, and(4.1) keeps it decoupled
from the still evolving third coordinate. This is exact absorption.

At the final point the ENTIRE payoff is

    E_m=[Phi_1(v_fin,w_fin)+Phi_2(w_fin,Z_fin)
                           +D_3^2/(2(1+S_L))]/R_true^2. (4.6)

Indeed substituting(4.1) at s'=S_L into(2.6) gives the displayed last
term. Until absorption is proved, both old terms in(4.6) are retained.

## 5. A finite last-crossing absorption lemma

This is the comparison used in this round. It treats the true quadratic
caps, the moving interval and all large steps exactly. It is NOT a
continuous-time limit or an approximation by the total word mass.

Consider(4.4) on a finite recursive word H_L with positive step values
b_0,...,b_(L-1), for fixed sigma>0, cap_ratio>0, beta>0 and A_2. Suppose
its incoming state has w_in<A_2 and positive width g_in=A_2-beta*v_in.
Let

    P_j=sum_{h in H_j, h>=2} h,
    z_in=beta*v_in-w_in,
    kappa_tail=1+beta^2.                            (5.1)

Empty sums are0. Suppose the following FINITE inequalities hold:

    max(z_in,0) <= kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2;          (5.2)
    sigma*(b_j-cap_ratio*P_j)/2 <= g_in,
                                     0<=j<L;       (5.3)
    sigma*(cap_ratio*b_j-kappa_tail*P_j)/2 <= g_in,
                                     0<=j<L.       (5.4)

Then the actual pair has entered its flat interval by the end of H_L.

Proof. If the incoming point is in the interval, absorption already holds.
Otherwise w_in<A_2 forces it to be on the left, with z_in>0. Assume for
contradiction that the terminal pair is active. Since a point in the closed
flat interval is absorbing, EVERY sample, including every post-step sample,
has then been strictly on the left or strictly on the right. There are
finitely many samples, so if a side change occurs it has a last occurrence.

While consecutive samples stay on the left, z=beta*v-w>0 evolves by

    z^+=z-h*kappa_tail*min(sigma,z/kappa_tail)/2.    (5.5)

If h>=2 and z<kappa_tail*sigma, the right side is(1-h/2)z<=0,
contradicting staying on that side. Consequently every such high step
has q=sigma and decreases z by h*kappa_tail*sigma/2. This also covers
the cap boundary; if it would land at0 that would be absorption or a
side change, which is excluded on a constant-side segment. Low steps
h<2 still cause nonnegative decrease. Likewise, on a constant right-side
segment, t=w-A_2>0 evolves by

    t^+=t-h*min(cap_ratio*sigma,t)/2,               (5.6)

so every h>=2 must have r=cap_ratio*sigma and decreases t by precisely
h*cap_ratio*sigma/2. This proves both necessary high-step mass losses
without neglecting any low step or near-boundary query.

If there is no side change, the whole H_L stays left, and(5.5) gives
z_in>kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2, contrary to(5.2).

Otherwise let the last side change occur at a step of level j. Every
occurrence of b_j in H_L is immediately followed by a full H_j. This
follows by induction from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j): a node's right subtree
is read immediately after the node, before any ancestor or later subtree.
That complete following H_j lies in the original remaining tail.

For a left-to-right change, at the preceding sample write z=beta*v-w>0
and g=A_2-beta*v>=g_in. The new right margin satisfies exactly

    t^+=h*q/2-z-g < b_j*sigma/2-g_in.              (5.7)

By lastness of the crossing the following H_j remains strictly right.
Equation(5.6) therefore requires t^+>cap_ratio*sigma*P_j/2.
Combined with(5.7) this contradicts(5.3).

For a right-to-left change, at the preceding sample put t=w-A_2>0.
The left coordinate v is unchanged on this step, and the new left margin
satisfies

    z^+=h*r/2-t-g < b_j*cap_ratio*sigma/2-g_in.     (5.8)

The following H_j remains strictly left, so(5.5) requires
z^+>kappa_tail*sigma*P_j/2, contradicting(5.4).
All cases contradict terminal activity. The flat interval is reached,
which proves the finite lemma.

This proof is valid even if the gap increases substantially and if many
earlier crossings occurred. It does not compare two different words of
equal mass. The exact following-subtree property is indispensable.

## 6. Uniform control of the local backward data

We now derive the bounds needed to verify ALL inequalities(5.2)--(5.4)
uniformly over growing m. No prefix-to-infinite-composition exchange occurs.
For fixed beta write

    I_beta=sqrt(2)(1+beta)-beta<2,
    Y_beta=beta(exp(I_beta/beta)-1),
    U_beta=beta+(C_beta-beta)(Y_beta+beta)/beta.     (6.1)

For the finite backward trajectory in Section2 put
w_i=(y_i+beta)/(q_i+beta). At each uncapped point the radial equation is
u_i-beta=(p(q_i)-beta)w_i. Taking a cross product of(2.8) with
v(q_(i-1)) cancels the alpha_i term and gives

    w_(i-1)=w_i[1+
       (p(q_(i-1))*q_i-q_(i-1)*p(q_i))
            /{beta*(p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1))}].         (6.2)

At a transition to q_(i-1)=0 this same formula follows from y_(i-1)=y_i;
if both q values are0 it is equality. Thus all endpoint cases are covered.
The numerator is

    integral_(q_(i-1))^(q_i)
                 [p(q_(i-1))-q_(i-1)*p'(t)] dt.

After dividing by p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1), the integrand is a weighted mean
of1 and -p'(t), hence is at most max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplying(6.2), using
1+x<=exp(x) and w_N=1, gives

    y+beta <= (q_0+beta)
       exp(beta^(-1) integral_(q_0)^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt)
       <= beta exp(beta^(-1) integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt). (6.3)

For the second inequality, log(1+q_0/beta)<=q_0/beta and the integrand
is at least1. The derivative is
p'(t)=-(t+beta)/(p(t)-beta). It crosses -1 at
(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Integrating on the two sides yields exactly I_beta.
In particular

    1<=y<=Y_beta<=beta*exp(2/beta).                 (6.4)

For any q_i>0, the radial relation and y_i<=Y_beta imply u_i<=U_beta.
The steps with q_(i-1)>0 form one contiguous final part of the ordinary
block. Their total sum of alpha_i p(q_(i-1)) is at most U_beta-beta,
by telescoping u_i. Since p>=beta and C_beta<2,

    u=beta+C_beta*S/2-E_gap,
    0<=E_gap<=C_beta*(U_beta-beta)/beta
                       <=4 beta^(-1) exp(2/beta).  (6.5)

All other steps have q_(i-1)=0 and p=C_beta, so they contribute no gap
in(6.5). Define the explicit bound

    K_bound(beta)=6 beta^(-1) exp(2/beta).          (6.6)

Combining M=u+y/beta with(6.4)--(6.5) proves

    |M-C_beta*S/2|<=K_bound(beta),
    |u-C_beta*S/2|<=K_bound(beta),
    y/beta<=exp(2/beta).                           (6.7)

These are estimates for arbitrary ordered finite ordinary blocks. They
have been derived jointly, rather than borrowed from a source exponent
or from the earlier unverified draft.

They suffice uniformly for the moving beta range in Section1. Since
beta^(-1)exp(2/beta) decreases with beta, beta>=8/m implies

    K_bound(beta)<=(3m/4)exp(m/4)<=m(4/3)^m.        (6.8)

The last estimate follows from exp(1/4)<sum_(r>=0)(1/4)^r=4/3.
It is a symbolic exponential bound, not a numerical evaluation.

For the actual word put v_mass=a_m/(c_m-2). Since
c_m(c_m-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>eta_m, one has0<v_mass<1. The finite mass
identity, proved by substitution into S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j and S_0=0, is

    1+S_j=v_mass*c_m^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j.              (6.9)

As m grows, v_mass->1 and c_m->lambda uniformly for tau in[1/5,1/4].
The identity m log(1+tau/m)->tau is uniform there; for example integrating
1/(1+x) gives 0<=tau-m log(1+tau/m)<=tau^2/(2m).
Consequently, for J=m-2 and L=m-1,

    S_J/lambda^J -> exp(tau),
    S_L/lambda^L -> exp(tau)                       (6.10)

uniformly in tau. In particular S is bounded below by a positive constant
times lambda^m for all sufficiently large m. Because4/3<2<lambda,
(6.8)--(6.10) imply, for EVERY fixed nonnegative integer r,

    m^r(1+2^J+K_bound(beta))/S ->0                 (6.11)

uniformly on the rectangle. Polynomial factors times a fixed geometric
ratio below1 tend to0; this follows, for instance, by taking the ratio of
successive terms and bounding it eventually by a constant below1.
Equation(6.11) is the full uniform error estimate used below.

## 7. Every crossing level satisfies the finite comparison

Write d_m=c_m-2. Formula(6.9) gives the exact identity

    b_j-d_m*S_j=c_m-1-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^j
                 <=c_m-1.                         (7.1)

The sum P_j in(5.1) obeys

    0<=S_j-P_j<=2(2^j-1)<=2^(j+1),                (7.2)

because each discarded step is below2 and the word has2^j-1 entries.
This is an inequality used within the last-crossing proof, not a claim
that the actual tail depends only on its mass.

Set epsilon_M=M-C_beta*S/2, so |epsilon_M|<=K_bound(beta). From the
EXACT cap_ratio=C_beta*(b_J-2)/(2M) and(7.1),

    cap_ratio-d_m
      ={ C_beta[(c_m-3)-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^J]
                              -2d_m*epsilon_M }/(2M).       (7.3)

For all sufficiently large m, 2<c_m<3, 0<d_m<1 and1<C_beta<2. Therefore

    |cap_ratio-d_m|<=Xi_m,
    Xi_m=(1+2^J+K_bound(beta))/M.                   (7.4)

By(6.7) and(6.11), M>=S/4 eventually, uniformly. Hence S/M<=4 and
m^r Xi_m->0 for every fixed r. In particular cap_ratio->d uniformly,
and cap_ratio*d_m<kappa_tail eventually, since d^2<1<=kappa_tail.
We may also use cap_ratio<=2 and kappa_tail<=17/16<2 uniformly.

For EVERY j with0<=j<=J=L-1, (7.1)--(7.4) imply

    sigma*(b_j-cap_ratio*P_j)/2
      <=[|d_m-cap_ratio| S+c_m-1+2cap_ratio*2^J]/(2M)
      <=4 Xi_m.                                   (7.5)

Indeed the first term is at most2Xi_m using S/M<=4, and the other
terms are at most(1+2*2^J)/M<=2Xi_m. Likewise, using
cap_ratio*d_m-kappa_tail<0,

    sigma*(cap_ratio*b_j-kappa_tail*P_j)/2
      <=[cap_ratio(c_m-1)+2kappa_tail*2^J]/(2M)
      <=2 Xi_m.                                   (7.6)

These estimates cover all levels simultaneously, including small levels
whose steps could never cross the positive-width interval in the first
place. No large-bridge transition is left to a pointwise limiting map.

We now compare them to the ACTUAL incoming gap. Equations(6.7),(6.9)
and(6.11) give the following uniform asymptotics, where the difference
from each displayed beta-dependent expression tends to0:

    D_2 - d/C_beta ->0,
    D_3 - d/C_beta^2 ->0,
    A_1 ->0, ell_2 ->0,
    R_true^2 ->1.                                  (7.7)

For D_3, use b_(J+1)=c_m*b_J-(c_m-1), so T_2/S->1/2 uniformly, while
T_1/S->d/2 and M/S-C_beta/2->0. For A_1 use the stronger bound
y/(beta*M)<=exp(2/beta)/M from(6.7)--(6.8); this tends to0 uniformly.
The radius convergence follows from its EXACT identity(3.7).
Since C_beta<2, eventually D_2>=d/4 uniformly. Consequently(4.5) gives

    g_in>=beta^2 D_2>=16d/m^2.                     (7.8)

Together with m^2 Xi_m->0, (7.5)--(7.8) establish(5.3)--(5.4) uniformly
for every level of every sufficiently large word.

It remains to check the incoming condition(5.2). The incoming right
margin is w_in-A_2=-beta D_3<0. The incoming left margin is exactly

    z_in=beta D_3-beta^2 D_2-ell_3/beta
              <=beta D_3.                        (7.9)

By(7.7), D_3<=1 eventually, so max(z_in,0)<=1/4 uniformly. Meanwhile
(6.10),(7.2) and(6.7) yield

    kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2
          - kappa_tail*lambda/C_beta ->0           (7.10)

uniformly. The second expression is larger than lambda/2>1 for all our
beta. The left side of(5.2)'s bound is thus at most1/4 while its right
side exceeds1 for all sufficiently large m. This proves(5.2).
If the incoming point were already flat, no comparison would be needed;
otherwise all hypotheses of the finite last-crossing lemma now hold.
The two retained components therefore vanish at the ACTUAL final state.
This proves(1.3), with every physical step accounted for.

The estimates explicitly pay the width scale of order m^(-2), the full
exponentially growing word, the small-step mass omitted only from a lower
bound, every possible last-crossing level, and all quadratic cap queries.
There is no unproved interchange of a fixed-prefix limit with a growing
composition. No comparison repair or change of the function was used.

## 8. Original benchmark normalization and the genuine deficit

Retain the original all-split definitions

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                  (8.1)

No denominator is recomputed or replaced. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750
states that Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1) exists and is positive and
finite. Its enclosure at b=1,j=0 gives

    Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2<21/5,      (8.2)

where B_1=G(1,1)=4 follows directly from(8.1). This is the only accepted
input used beyond elementary statements proved jointly in this report.

From(6.10), uniformly in tau,

    B_(n_m)/(1+S_L) -> Z_1*lambda*exp(-tau).        (8.3)

Substitute(7.7), the EXACT zero retained values, and R_true^2->1 into
(1.3). Since d=1/lambda, (8.3) proves exactly the uniform approximation
(1.4). For a fixed beta>0 the moving rectangle includes it once m>=8/beta;
this proves(1.5). For beta_m=8/m, C_beta tends to1. The function C_beta
is strictly increasing in beta, so the uniform approximation also proves
the supremum limits in(1.6) and over the full tau rectangle. This uses
uniform control, not an exchange of an uncontrolled supremum and limit.

For explicit benchmark margins, lambda>12/5 because sqrt(2)>7/5, and
exp(1/4)>1+1/4 gives exp(-1/4)<4/5. Thus

    Z_1*exp(-1/4)/(2lambda)
          < (21/5)(4/5)/(24/5)=7/10.              (8.4)

Similarly exp(-1/5)<5/6 gives

    Z_1*exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)
          < (21/5)(5/6)/(24/5)=35/48<3/4.        (8.5)

The strict limiting margins and uniform convergence imply the stated
eventual finite-horizon deficits. These are symbolic inequalities, with
no saved benchmark arithmetic replay, numerical evaluation or new DP.

There is no conflict with the positive post-root retained value in the
source candidate. For fixed beta, (3.4) and(7.7) give
z_in->beta*d*(1-beta*C_beta)/C_beta^2>0. The finite tail proved above
erases that value exactly. The equal-mass tail counterexample from the
source candidate also remains intact: the present argument uses the
specific recursively nested following blocks, not a mass-only law.

## 9. Source provenance, residual scope and resource handoff

The unchanged physical construction originated in the direct convex
reconstruction of the shifted circular mechanism in Kaizhao Liu and

# Advertised objective slack and three changed finite/optimum supports

JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT — UNVERIFIED. Worker max, allocated invocation74
after completed baseline73, attempted ceiling75. This is research evidence,
not a fact. No formal gate, mathematical program, numerical replay,
trajectory/recurrence execution, optimizer or subagent was used.

The interface is q=zeta/D, 1<zeta<250/237. Exact Huber contact and zero
incoming optimum rows are NOT imposed on the changed laws. The complete
relaxed matrix and exact constant-slack expenditure are derived first.
Three explicit changed supports then fail in precisely stated original
directions. Prepaying the previously missing orientations does not by
itself pay the whole gradient matrix. No general support-changing
impossibility or unrestricted W upper/lower bound is asserted.

## 1. Target, fixed word, and authority

For a nonnegative word h of physical length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions,
convex differentiable L-smooth objectives with an attained minimum, L>0,
and x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.
The original all-split benchmark is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split is an original integer split. The negative route needs a full
all-function upper on unbounded actual horizons with a fixed relative
improvement. A failed dual is not a primal function or a W conclusion.

Fix ONE enclosing m>=1536, tau=1/4, and set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+a*c^j,Hword_j),
    S_j=sum Hword_j, A_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, D_j=2A_j-1,
    n_j=2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.

Every child keeps these SAME enclosing parameters. Hword_m is the
original H_(m,1/4), with no altered entry or horizon reset. Epsilon=0 is
the Silver diagnostic, never a substitute for the actual positive epsilon.
All recurrences below are finite definitions/identities; none was executed.

Accepted inputs are fact1252b8823bb5a290 (original normalized interpolation,
full formal space and certificate convention), fact04219554e41d9750
Sections10–11 (primitive history and full-space composition conventions),
and fact5fd396eba75651e7 Section10 ONLY for B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250.
The latter's witness-family optimum is not an upper bound for W.
Full findings1a79c9fd34296d77 and aed1ed37a3df33d4 have identical evidence
and were read in full, together with89a0ae3ddc4fe35a,
elaboration971c9cb839662d46 and guidancef51ffc777372c82f.
They are awareness, never predecessors. All unverified identities used
here are derived in this ONE joint draft, rather than adopted as facts.

The operational advertised level for all three laws is

    zeta_0=101/100, q_j=zeta_0/D_j.

Their zero-initial-point failures actually apply to every real q.
Incoming optimum multipliers are written xiCert_i, distinct from the
project's unrelated complex-prefix notation xi_i.

## 2. Full rows, exact flow and the complete relaxed matrix

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain ALL independent original
formal vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, including bridge and terminal gradients.
For any physical word put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h, D=1+2S, h_n=0,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

All actual rows satisfy Q_ij<=0 by fact1252b8823bb5a290.
Let K_ij be finite multipliers (K_ii=0), nu_i the i->star multiplier,
and xiCert_i the star->i multiplier. All FINAL multipliers are required
nonnegative. Exact value cancellation is

    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xiCert_i=2*1_(i=n).       (1)

Write out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij. The entire original certificate is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)K_ij Q_ij
                    +sum_i nu_i Q_(i,star)
                    +sum_i xiCert_i Q_(star,i)
        =q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<x_0,g_i>+Cgrad(g),        (2)

where Cgrad(g)=sum_(i,j)Cgrad_ij<g_i,g_j> and

    Cgrad_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2Cgrad_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                         +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (3)

The full matrix is [[q,-nu^T/2],[-nu/2,Cgrad]]. Incoming optimum rows
are present: their diagonal contribution is included through (1), while
they create no x_0 mixed term. Half the incident mass at i equals
out_i-1_(i=n). Forward position terms subtract h_i K_iv from the source
diagonal; difference squares and both prefix position terms give (3).
This derivation covers nonzero xiCert and every original off-diagonal.

For q>0, completing the initial-point square proves full PSD equivalent to

    Cgrad-(nu nu^T)/(4q) >=0 on ALL gradient coordinates. (4)

No selected-plane, child-quotient or diagonal positivity replaces (4).
If this full matrix is PSD, then every actual legal transcript satisfies
q||x_0||^2-2f_n>=M(q)>=0 because all multiplied Q rows are nonpositive.
Thus full PSD is a sufficient all-function certificate for W_n(h)<=q.
No such sufficiency is inferred for any failed array below.

## 3. Exact constant-slack expenditure; no forced contact

Let e be the all-ones gradient-index vector, csum=Cgrad e, and define

    X=sum_i xiCert_i,
    E=sum_i(1+Ppos_i)xiCert_i,
    Hprice=sum_i(2S-Ppos_i)xiCert_i=D X-E,
    delta=zeta-1>0, q=zeta/D.

Summing (1) gives sum nu_i=2+X. Its position-weighted sum gives
sum K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j)=2S-sum(nu_i-xiCert_i)Ppos_i.
Expanding the common-gradient polynomial consequently yields

    e^T Cgrad e=D+E.                                  (5)

Use the explicit one-sided Huber function Hub_1(z), equal to zero for
z<=0, z^2/2 on [0,1], and z-1/2 for z>=1. It is convex and 1-smooth.
Starting at D gives x_i=D-Ppos_i>=S+1>=1 at every query.
The actual scale-D Huber transcript is x_0=D, g_i=1,
f_i=D-Ppos_i-1/2. Its finite and outgoing optimum rows have residual
zero; its incoming residual is -(2S-Ppos_i). Hence exactly

    M(D,e)=delta D-Hprice.                            (6)

Necessary WHOLE-word Huber expenditure is Hprice<=delta D.
Since Ppos_i<=S, Hprice>=S X and X<=delta D/S when S>0.
This is not a renewable allowance for each child or outgoing row.

The full common-gradient/initial-point restriction is

    M(x_0=r,g=t e)=q r^2-(2+X)r t+(D+E)t^2.            (7)

Its exact PSD condition is

    zeta >= D(2+X)^2/[4(D+E)].                         (8)

It retains the changed outgoing mass2+X and can be stronger than (6).

The complete common-gradient derivative is

    2(csum)_i=2h_i+xiCert_i+h_i sum_(t>i)xiCert_t
       +(1+Ppos_i)nu_i+sum_j K_ij(Ppos_i-Ppos_j).       (9)

For finite rows the source derivative is the last term. Their physical
position derivative is h_i times later finite-node flow. Adding later
outgoing optimum contributions makes that sum 2+sum_(t>i)xiCert_t;
the incoming square contributes xiCert_i. This proves (9), including
the original terminal coordinate h_n=0.

For arbitrary incoming support put

    Acommon=[[q,-(2+X)/2],[-(2+X)/2,D+E]],
    DeltaCommon=q(D+E)-(2+X)^2/4.

If DeltaCommon>0, write the scalar gradient vector uniquely as t e+v,
e^T v=0. Its coupling to (x_0,t) is
l(v)=(-nu^T v/2,csum^T v). Exact expenditure on an arbitrary direction is

    Price(v)=l(v)^T Acommon^(-1)l(v)
      =[(D+E)(nu^T v)^2/4
          -(2+X)(nu^T v)(csum^T v)/2
          +q(csum^T v)^2]/DeltaCommon.                (10)

Under DeltaCommon>0, full original-space PSD is equivalent to the stated
positive definiteness of Acommon
and v^T Cgrad v>=Price(v) for EVERY v in e-perp. This is an invertible
decomposition retaining all coordinates, not a selected quotient theorem.
If DeltaCommon=0, the coupling must annihilate Acommon's kernel and the
semidefinite Schur condition remains necessary; no singular direction is
silently removed.

For zero incoming mass let

    dcontact=csum-D nu/2, e^T dcontact=0.

Then DeltaCommon=delta and (10) is exactly

    Price(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D
                       +(dcontact^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)]. (11)

Thus relaxed q does NOT force dcontact=0. The contact-defect square spends
1/[D(zeta-1)] on the entire transverse space. At the tight level zeta=1
the coupling would force zero defect; importing that kernel at larger q
would improperly discard allowed constructions. A fixed negative direction
of Cgrad at x_0=0 is nevertheless unaffected by any q.

Equations(1)–(11) are full necessary conditions and exact square-completion
identities, jointly UNVERIFIED here. They are not an accepted intermediate
or an unrestricted W upper.

## 4. Explicit baseline, actual innovation plane and calibrations

All coefficients required to make the three changed laws concrete are
given here and rederived within this draft.

### 4.1 Complete finite source

For a depth-j child terminal z define the primitive history

    V_j=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)
         +sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
         -A_j(A_j-1)||g_z||^2.

Start Lambda^0=0. At Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+d_j,Hword_j), with bridge
p and terminal z, embed Lambda^j on the left with weight1 and on the
right with weight k_j. Add precisely

    u_j sum_(i right,i<z)h_i Q_(i,p)
     +v_j sum_(i right,i<z)h_i Q_(i,z)
     +w_j Q_(z,p)+z_j Q_(p,z),
    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j=[u_j d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=1+(u_j+v_j)/2,
    z_j=2A_j,
    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-u_j(A_j-1).                         (12)

All unlisted entries are zero; repeated original rows are summed.
The identities u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1) and
u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j) give nonterminal divergence -2h_i and
terminal divergence2(A_(j+1)-1). Put
Psrc_j=V_j+sum_(i!=v)Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.
With t_j=d_j/A_j, t_star=1/lambda,
r=sum_(i right,i<z)h_i g_i, Y=r-(A_j-1)g_z, Z=g_p-g_z,
the full identity is

    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right
       +(t_star-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2
              -2lambda A_j<Y,Z>+A_j^2(2+t_star+t_j)||Z||^2]. (13)

For an independent coefficient audit, the four new unweighted gradient
polynomials, in (12)'s order, are

    d_j<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                      +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;
    -||r||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                      +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>;
    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.

Their weighted r^2,r*g_p,r*g_z coefficients are
(u_j-v_j)/2, u_j d_j-z_j, w_j-v_j. The other two diagonals after V are

    A_j(A_j-1)-d_j-d_j^2+[u_j(A_j-1)+w_j]/2-(d_j+1/2)z_j,
    -A_(j+1)(A_(j+1)-1)+k_j A_j(A_j-1)
                                   +v_j(A_j-1)/2+(w_j+z_j)/2.

Substitution yields (13) on ALL original gradients. Its bracket is
positive definite: the determinant divided by A_j^2 is
(lambda+1)(2+t_star+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0.

Finite summation of the actual physical word gives

    v_h=a/(c-2), 0<v_h<1,
    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (14)

Since c(c-2)>1>beta, the ratios increase. The first ones satisfy
t_0<t_star, t_1<t_star, t_2>t_star, as witnessed by

    lambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0,
    c(lambda d_2-A_2)=epsilon[(lambda+4)
                      -(4lambda+2)epsilon-(5lambda+2)epsilon^2]>0.

For the last sign use 12/5<lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/6144:
the positive constant exceeds6, while both removed terms together are
less than1. Thus all later t_j exceed t_star. In (12), u,v,k,z are
positive. Also w_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda)>0:
w_0=4+2a, A_1<lambda gives w_1>2lambda-2, and j>=2 gives w_j>2lambda.
At epsilon=0, t_j=t_star and Psrc_j=0 identically by (13).

### 4.2 Complete full objective array

Initialize Kbase^0=0, mubase^0_0=2, xiCert=0. At a join j->j+1,
k=n_j, use L_l=l for0<=l<=k, bridge p=L_k,
R_i=k+1+i for0<=i<=k, terminal R_k. Set

    A=A_j-1, d=1+d_j, V=3A+2d,
    D=D_(j+1), D_c=D_j,
    s_j=(A+d)/V, theta_j=1-s_j.

Define EVERY parent row by

    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/D,
    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,
    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mubase^j_i,
    mubase_(L_l)=2h_l/D for l<k,
    mubase_p=2s_j-2A/D,
    mubase_(R_i)=theta_j mubase^j_i,
    all other finite rows=0, all incoming optimum rows=0. (15)

For A=0, s_0=1/2; for A>0, 1/3<s_j<1/2. All final signs hold:
the bridge attachment is positive since
(A+d)D-A(3A+2d)=A^2+4Ad+2d^2+A+d>0.
Source flow is canceled by left2h_l/D attachments; its terminal flow2A/D
is canceled by the bridge attachment and incoming right mass2s_j.
Each right node restores its child flow because theta_j+s_j=1.
This proves (1), sum mubase=2 and exact value cancellation.

Let Cbase^j be the gradient matrix,
x_p=x_0-sum_(l<k)h_l g_(L_l), r_R=sum_i mubase^j_i g_(R_i),
Rchild_R=Cbase^j(g_R)-D_c||r_R||^2/4 and
kappa_j=D_c-D theta_j^2. The full original identity is

    Mbase_(j+1)(1/D)=Psrc_j(g_L)/D+Rchild_R
       +||x_p+(A-D s_j)g_p-(D theta_j/2)r_R||^2/D
       +(kappa_j/4)||r_R-2g_p||^2.                   (16)

Neither Rchild_R nor kappa_j is presumed nonnegative on the actual word.
For checking, source plus left optimum rows gives Psrc_j/D and
||x_p-(A+1)g_p||^2/D. Replacing each right optimum row by
theta_j Q_(R_i,star)+s_j Q_(R_i,p) adds
s_j[-f_p+<g_(R_i),x_p-g_p>+||g_p||^2/2].
After value cancellation the remaining polynomial is

    ||x_p||^2/D+2(A/D-s_j)<x_p,g_p>
      +(A^2/D+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,r_R>
      +(d-s_j)<g_p,r_R>+D_c||r_R||^2/4.

Using s_j V=A+d completes it to (16). This keeps original x_0 and all
inherited gradients without a child PSD assumption. It also proves
baseline exact contact csum=D mubase/2 by induction: source derivatives
vanish at common gradients, the displayed square and difference vanish
at x_0=D,g_i=1, and the inherited child remainder annihilates common
gradients when the child has this contact identity.

Put b=1+a, so 7/5<b<sqrt(2)<10/7. At depth1,
Kbase_1,0=1, mubase_0=mubase_1=1 and

    Mbase_1(1/D_1)=||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1
                         +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4,
    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.                      (17)

Both are PSD. At depth2, d=2-epsilon, D_c>19/5, D<75/7,
theta_1<59/100 and D theta_1^2<15/4, so kappa_1>1/20.
These follow from b<10/7 and d>199/100:
theta_1=(2b+d)/(3b+2d) increases with b and decreases with d,
and substitution at those rational endpoints gives theta_1<59/100.
Identity(16), positive Psrc_1, and (17)'s ENTIRE Schur remainder give
full actual H2 PSD, including ALL initial-point mixed directions.

For Silver let u_star=1/lambda, so d=1+u_star(A+1) and
u_star^2+2u_star=1. Exact expansion gives

    V^2 kappa_j=2(1-u_star)A^3+(6-8u_star)A^2
       +(1+u_star)(8u_star-2)A+(1+u_star)^2(1-2u_star)>0. (18)

Every coefficient is positive because1/4<u_star<1/2.
At epsilon=0 sourcePsrc=0, so the empty square||x_0-g_0||^2 and (16)
prove full Silver PSD inductively. This particular child PSD induction
is justified: full child PSD with contact implies its entire Schur
remainder Rchild_R>=0. No such induction is assumed on positive epsilon.

### 4.3 Actual innovation plane and the sole decrement capacity

Set

    Wloc=A_2+d_2, Uloc=A_2+d_3, Tloc=Wloc+Uloc=A_3+d_3,
    Edef=(lambda d_2-A_2)(A_2+d_2/lambda)>0,
    delta3=t_3-t_star>0.

In a one-dimensional ORIGINAL formal-vector test, assign
g_0,...,g_3=u; g_4,...,g_7=v0;
g_8,...,g_11=w; g_12,...,g_15=0. Its displacement is
Wloc(u+w)+Uloc v0. The two-dimensional zero-displacement test plane N is

    u=-w-(Uloc/Wloc)v0.                               (19)

This chooses test vectors; it identifies no independent formal coordinates.
Applying (13) at the left H3, right H3 and their H4 join gives

    P_N=Psrc_4|N=-Edef(u-v0)^2-k_3 Edef w^2
       -delta3[(lambda+1)Wloc^2 w^2
          -2lambda A_3 Wloc w v0+A_3^2(2+t_star+t_3)v0^2]<0 (20)

at every nonzero point of N. Left H3 has Y=0,Z=u-v0, right H3 contributes
-Edef*w^2, and the final bracket is positive definite by (13).
Place N in the FIRST actual H4 of Hword_r, all later gradients zero and
x_0=0. Zero displacement and terminal make all higher source Y,Z zero
with left embedding weight1. At the final objective join all boundary
and right variables vanish. Consequently, for EVERY r>=5,

    Mbase_r(q)|N=P_N/D_r, for every real q.             (21)

Define actual physical gaps

    ell=Ppos_7-Ppos_5=b+(2-epsilon),
    rlen=Ppos_5-Ppos_2=2b+1+d_2,
    Tpair=ell+rlen=Ppos_7-Ppos_2,
    Ppos_2=ell, Ppos_3=A_2-1,
    Ppos_5=Wloc+b, Ppos_6=Wloc+ell,
    Ppos_7=Wloc+A_2-1.

The sole decremented source entry needed below is exactly

    B67=Lambda^4_6,7=k_2(2k_1+v_1 b)+v_2 b>0.           (22)

The first term combines its right H2 embedding of original H1 terminal
transport and v_1*b; the second is the H3 v_2*b terminal row.
Higher all-left embeddings have weight1. Thus (15) gives
Kbase^r_6,7=B67/D_r at EVERY r>=4. Prescribe

    kseed=(epsilon/2)*min{B67/rlen,1},
    tau_r=kseed/D_r, r>=4.                             (23)

Then tau_r*rlen is at most epsilon/2 of that EXACT available entry.
Both seeding laws modify the first H4 exactly once; no higher level
renews this budget.

## 5. Law A: finite three-cycle seeds and a prepaid reversed channel

For r<=3 use the baseline. For r>=4, first add finite cycles

    tau_r*Tpair*(Q_3,5+Q_5,2+Q_2,3),
    tau_r*ell*(Q_6,2+Q_2,5+Q_5,6).                     (24)

These preseed the old zero entries3->5 and6->2. Add the reversed
six-row channel

    +tau_r ell at3->2, -tau_r Tpair at3->5,
    +tau_r rlen at3->7, -tau_r ell at6->2,
    +tau_r Tpair at6->5, -tau_r rlen at6->7.            (25)

Combining them, the FINAL finite changes are precisely

    +tau_r ell: 3->2, 2->5, 5->6;
    +tau_r rlen: 3->7;
    +tau_r Tpair: 6->5, 5->2, 2->3;
    -tau_r rlen: 6->7.                                (26)

All other changes, including optimum changes, are zero. The seeded
3->5 and6->2 return to their original values. All final signs are paid
by (22)–(23). Each three-cycle and (25) has zero divergence, so exact
value flow (1) holds. This really changes positive support; it is not
the formerly unsupported reversal or old twelve-row pair.

For the ENTIRE formal space set y_i=x_i-g_i. The channel has zero
row/column sums and ell Ppos_2-Tpair Ppos_5+rlen Ppos_7=0.
Expanding it and the two cycles gives

    DeltaM_A=tau_r*Tpair*(Q_3,5+Q_5,2+Q_2,3)
       +tau_r*ell*(Q_6,2+Q_2,5+Q_5,6)
       -tau_r<g_6-g_3,ell y_2-Tpair y_5+rlen y_7>.      (27)

Values cancel. The displayed bilinear and complete cycle expansions,
together with (2)–(3), retain ALL new directions.

X=E=Hprice=0, so exact Huber value is delta D_r. The contact defect is
generally NONZERO: its changed derivative 2DeltaCgrad e equals

    tau_r*Tpair*[(Ppos_3-Ppos_5)e_3
        +(Ppos_5-Ppos_2)e_5+(Ppos_2-Ppos_3)e_2]
     +tau_r*ell*[(Ppos_6-Ppos_2)e_6
        +(Ppos_2-Ppos_5)e_2+(Ppos_5-Ppos_6)e_5].        (28)

Here e_i is the original gradient-index basis, distinct from all-ones e.
For a flow-balanced cycle, derivatives through positions cancel and the
remaining source derivative is its position gap at each source.
The reversed channel's common derivative is zero. Thus
dcontact=DeltaCgrad e, with exact full-space expense (11).
No old contact kernel is imposed.

On ENTIRE N both finite cycles have the same polynomial

    Rcycle=(u-v0)^2-(u-v0)[(b+1+d_2)u+b v0]
          =(u-v0)[-(b+d_2)u-(b+1)v0].                 (29)

Their position terms both sum to (u-v0)(x_5-x_2);
x_5-x_2=-(b+1+d_2)u-b v0. The reversed channel contributes
+tau_r ell(b+d_2)(u-v0)^2, using
y_2=-(ell+1)u, y_5=-Wloc*u-(b+1)v0, y_7=-Wloc*u-A_2*v0.
Therefore at every r>=5,

    D_r M_A(q)|N=P_N+kseed[(Tpair+ell)Rcycle
                                      +ell(b+d_2)(u-v0)^2]. (30)

At u=1,v0=0,w=-1 the added term is -kseed*Tpair*(b+d_2)<0,
while P_N<0. The full original matrix fails for EVERY q and actual r>=5,
including m. The seed cycles invalidate the hoped-for positive payment.

Whole-depth D_r-normalized curvature (30) is exact and independent of r;
no attenuation, reset or per-level allowance is hidden. H2 is unchanged
and fully PSD by Section4.2. Silver kseed=0 gives full calibration(18).

For r>=2 the original first-gradient entries are
Cbase_00=2/D_r and mubase_0=2b/D_r: in (16) take only g_0 nonzero,
Psrc_j=(2-b^2)g_0^2 and x_p=x_0-b*g_0.
All changed sources are at least2, so x_0=b,g_0=1 gives

    M_A(zeta/D_r)=[zeta*b^2-2(b^2-1)]/D_r>0, zeta>1.    (31)

This one passing mixed direction does not mean all new mixed directions
pass; their entire expense is (27)–(28), (3) and (11).
The independent original zero-x_0 direction already rejects the law.
Scope is ONLY these finite seed cycles and their paid reversal.

## 6. Law B: optimum triangles, affordable common price, and a failed N mode

Through r=3 use the baseline. For r>=4, add instead

    tau_r*Tpair*(Q_3,5+Q_(5,star)+Q_(star,3)),
    tau_r*ell*(Q_6,2+Q_(2,star)+Q_(star,6)),             (32)

then (25). FINAL changes are exactly

    finite +tau_r ell at3->2, +tau_r rlen at3->7,
           +tau_r Tpair at6->5, -tau_r rlen at6->7;
    outgoing optimum +tau_r Tpair at5->star,
                     +tau_r ell at2->star;
    incoming optimum +tau_r Tpair atstar->3,
                     +tau_r ell atstar->6.            (33)

Every other entry is its baseline value. All signs are paid by (23);
triangles and channel have zero FULL divergence, so (1) holds with both
optimum directions. No xiCert=0 assumption is imported.

The complete original-space identity is

    DeltaM_B=tau_r*Tpair*Ftri(3,5)+tau_r*ell*Ftri(6,2)
             -tau_r<g_6-g_3,ell y_2-Tpair y_5+rlen y_7>,
    Ftri(i,j)=||g_i||^2+||g_j||^2-<g_i,g_j>
                          +<g_i-g_j,x_j>-<g_i,x_i>.    (34)

Ftri's initial-point contribution is EXACTLY -<x_0,g_j>.
New diagonal entries, retained rather than sold as PSD, are

    DeltaCgrad_33=tau_r[ell-rlen*d_2],
    DeltaCgrad_66=tau_r[ell+rlen*b],
    DeltaCgrad_22=tau_r*ell, DeltaCgrad_55=tau_r*Tpair,

with other diagonal changes zero. Initial mixed changes are
-tau_r*ell/2 at g_2 and -tau_r*Tpair/2 at g_5.
The full prefix/off-diagonal contributions remain in (34).

Its EXACT whole-word optimum and Huber prices are

    X_r=tau_r(Tpair+ell),
    E_r=tau_r[Tpair*(1+Ppos_3)+ell*(1+Ppos_6)],
    Hprice_r=tau_r[Tpair*(2S_r-Ppos_3)
                                    +ell*(2S_r-Ppos_6)]. (35)

Huber slack is delta D_r-Hprice_r; the common mixed required inflation
is D_r(2+X_r)^2/[4(D_r+E_r)]. All independent directions are charged
by (10) for the complete rows (33)–(34).

The advertised zeta_0 affords both common tests:
Tpair<10, ell<7/2 and kseed<=epsilon/2 imply
X_r<7epsilon/D_r<1/800 and Hprice_r<=D_r X_r<7epsilon<1/100.
Since D_r>1, delta D_r=D_r/100>Hprice_r. Moreover

    D_r(2+X_r)^2/[4(D_r+E_r)]<=(1+X_r/2)^2
                       <1+1/800+1/(4*800^2)<101/100.    (36)

Thus these seeds are affordable in the FULL common mixed space; an
independent original gradient direction still fails.

On ENTIRE N the two triangles have polynomials

    Ftri(3,5)=-d_2*u^2+(Wloc-b-1)u*v0+(1+b)v0^2,
    Ftri(6,2)=(1+ell)u^2+(b+d_2)u*v0+(1+ell)v0^2.

Together with the reversed channel they give

    Ropt=Aopt*u^2+Bopt*u*v0+Copt*v0^2,
    Aopt=ell*A_2-rlen*d_2,
    Bopt=ell*A_2+Tpair*d_2,
    Copt=Tpair*(1+b)+ell*(A_2+d_2),
    D_r M_B(q)|N=P_N+kseed*Ropt, r>=5.                 (37)

Every optimum contribution is present. These are full two-variable
plane forms, not separate diagonal tests. At the exact assignment
u=-2,v0=1,w=2-Uloc/Wloc,

    Ropt=3ell*A_2+Tpair*(1+b)-(6rlen+ell)*d_2<-28/5.    (38)

Here A_2<6, ell<7/2, Tpair<10, b<3/2 give positive terms<63+25=88.
The bounds rlen>6, ell>3, d_2>12/5 make the removed term
>(36+3)*12/5=468/5. Thus (38) is a written uniform inequality,
not an arithmetic program or recurrence replay.
These bounds follow from A_2=1+2b+2-epsilon and
d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2). For d_2>12/5 use lambda>241/100 and
epsilon<=1/6144, retaining the strict rational gap.
Also b<10/7, 2-epsilon<2, d_2<121/50 give
Tpair=3b+(2-epsilon)+1+d_2<10.

Both P_N and the new correction are negative at (38). Thus the complete
original matrix fails at every real q at each r>=5, including actual m.
Equation(37) proves exact whole-depth normalized retention of the failed
mode. It is not a deep PSD induction from unverified children.

The original g_0 mixed test(31) is unchanged, since new sources start at2.
H2 is unchanged and fully PSD. Silver epsilon=0 removes all seeds and
passes full calibration. The incoming support is legal and affordable;
its complete gradient payment nevertheless fails. Scope is ONLY these
two specified optimum triangles and their coupled reversal.

## 7. Law C: nonuniform adjacent-row spread and actual bridge failure

This differs from the local seeds and earlier scalar retention laws.
For a nonempty Hword_r, put n=n_r and define

    tpath_i=2Ppos_(i+1)/S_r, 0<=i<n_r,
    Kpath_(i+1,i)=tpath_i, all other finite entries=0,
    nupath_0=tpath_0,
    nupath_i=tpath_i-tpath_(i-1), 0<i<n_r,
    nupath_n=2-tpath_(n_r-1)=0,
    all incoming optimum rows=0.                      (39)

Every tpath_i is in(0,2] and nondecreasing. These path weights are distinct
from the source ratios t_j of (13)–(14). Nonterminal divergence is canceled
by nupath, while the terminal finite outgoing mass is2. Thus all signs
and EXACT flow (1) hold. Physical positions determine all weights.

For all depths r define

    alpha_r=epsilon*max{r-2,0},
    K_C^r=(1-alpha_r)Kbase^r+alpha_r Kpath^r,
    nu_C^r=(1-alpha_r)mubase^r+alpha_r nupath^r,
    xiCert_C^r=0.                                     (40)

At r=0 use the empty baseline. Since 0<=alpha_r<1/4, each baseline
decrement is paid by alpha_r times its SAME original entry, and every
path addition is nonnegative. Flow is linear, and the whole formal identity is

    M_C^r(q)=(1-alpha_r)Mbase_r(q)+alpha_r Mpath_r(q).    (41)

No changed child is presumed PSD or recursively reset.
X=E=Hprice=0, so exact Huber slack is delta D_r; the full contact defect
is determined by (9) and priced by (11), rather than set to zero.

All independent path gradient entries, from (3), are

    Cpath_ii=tpath_i for i<n_r, Cpath_nn=1,
    2Cpath_i,i+1=-tpath_i+h_i tpath_(i+1) for i+1<n_r,
    2Cpath_n-1,n=-tpath_(n-1)+2h_(n-1),
    2Cpath_ij=h_i nupath_j for j>i+1.                 (42)

With initial mixed coefficient -nupath/2, these specify the ENTIRE path
matrix, not just its root compression.

### 7.1 Actual H2, Silver, and whole-word initial mixed price

For r<=2, alpha_r=0; full actual H2 is unchanged.
At epsilon=0 every alpha_r=0, so full Silver PSD at tight1/D_r follows
from (18), with no per-level inflation.

For r>=2, baseline original first-gradient entries are those used in (31).
The path first entries are Cpath_00=nupath_0=2b/S_r. Therefore the
ORIGINAL mixed test x_0=b,g_0=1, all other gradients zero, gives exactly

    M_C^r(zeta/D_r)=[zeta*b^2-Rmix_r]/D_r,
    Rmix_r=(1-alpha_r)*2(b^2-1)
                          +alpha_r*(2D_r*b/S_r)(b-1). (43)

It is a complete initial-point/gradient compression, not an isolated diagonal.
Since D_r/S_r>2,

    Rmix_r>2(b^2-1)+2alpha_r(b-1)^2.                  (44)

At r=m, alpha_m=(m-2)/(4m)>=1/5 and b>141/100.
For the latter use b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
sqrt(2)>707/500, lambda>2 and epsilon<=1/6144.
At the operational zeta_0=101/100,

    Rmix_m-zeta_0*b^2
      >(99/100)b^2-2+(2/5)(b-1)^2
      >[99*19881+67240-2000000]/1000000
      =35459/1000000>0.                               (45)

Thus this original mixed direction fails at zeta_0.
The EXACT whole-word required mixed inflation is Rmix_m/b^2 from (43).
A larger permitted constant is not excluded by (45) alone; the separate
bridge failure below applies to every q, including the entire advertised
interval. No child allowance is reset.

### 7.2 Entire-space bridge/right-query failure at actual depth

First bound an ORIGINAL baseline diagonal without assuming its PSD.
Let pterm_j be Psrc_j with only its terminal gradient equal1.
Then pterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. At j=1,
t_star-t_1<epsilon/A_1. In (13), Y=-(A_1-1),Z=-1;
its bracket is less than28: drop the negative cross term and use
lambda+1<4,b<3/2,A_1<5/2,2+t_star+t_1<3.
Since k_1<7, pterm_2<49epsilon<1<A_2^2.

For j>=2, (13)'s bracket is positive and t_j>t_star, so
pterm_(j+1)<=k_j pterm_j. From (12),

    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,
    k_j-(2+t_j)^2=(t_star-t_j)(t_j+1)<0.

Induction on this UPPER estimate gives pterm_j<=A_j^2 for every j>=2.
This is not a PSD induction. Source terminal flow and h_terminal=0 yield
the exact outgoing finite mass Lambdaout_j=A_j^2-1+pterm_j.

At actual m, put j=m-1, Bmass=A_j>1250, bridge p=n_j,
right-first p+1, physical bridge h_p=1+d_j.
Since A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j and A_0=1, A_j>=2^j; j>=1535 and
the elementary written inequality2^11>1250 give the bound Bmass>1250.

For the original baseline root, (15)–(16) give

    Cbase_pp=2s_j+[(Bmass-1)^2+pterm_j]/D_m
               <1+2Bmass^2/D_m<Bmass,
    Cbase_p+1,p+1=2/D_j<2/Bmass,
    Cbase_p,p+1=b(h_p-s_j)/D_j>0.                     (46)

The first inequality uses D_m=4Bmass+2d_j-1>3Bmass and Bmass>3.
The right-first diagonal is its inherited entire child entry: the split
optimum and backward row restore its original outgoing mass.
For the off-diagonal, (3) uses
K_(p+1,p)=s_j*mubase_0, nu_(p+1)=theta_j*mubase_0,
mubase_0=2b/D_j and no root forward row, proving the third entry
with every original prefix contribution retained.

The path weights have tpath_p=2(Bmass-1+h_p)/[2Bmass-2+h_p]>1,
tpath_(p+1)>=tpath_p, both<=2. Therefore at r=m,

    C_C,pp<Bmass+2<2Bmass,
    C_C,p+1,p+1<2/Bmass+2alpha_m<=1,
    C_C,p,p+1>=alpha_m*(h_p*tpath_(p+1)-tpath_p)/2
                    >alpha_m*(h_p-1)/2>Bmass/25.      (47)

The last uses alpha_m>=1/5 and t_j>t_star>2/5, so
d_j>(2/5)Bmass. No optimistic child-PSD bound enters.

At the explicit ORIGINAL assignment

    x_0=0, g_p=1, g_(p+1)=-Bmass/25,
    every other independent original gradient=0,

for EVERY q the whole matrix obeys

    M_C^m(q)<2Bmass-2Bmass^2/625+Bmass^2/625
                  =2Bmass-Bmass^2/625<0.             (48)

This defeats every advertised constant, independently of (45).
It chooses a vector in the full original space; it deletes no row or
formal coordinate. Actual alpha_m is bounded below1/5, so it is not
silently treated as a tiny perturbation at every depth.
Both (43) and (47) use the SAME enclosing m and complete physical word.

This scopes the displayed mass-weighted adjacent flow and its specified
mixture only. It does not exclude a different nonuniform transport that
uses remote original rows to pay the bridge/right-query coupling.

## 8. Verdict, exact scope and original goal

Law A legally prepays the formerly missing finite orientations through
two explicit finite three-cycles. Its contact defect is exactly priced,
but its full original N mode u=1,v0=0,w=-1 remains negative at every q.

Law B legally prepays them through two explicit optimum triangles.
Incoming/outgoing prices and common mixed restriction are affordable
at zeta_0. Its full original N mode u=-2,v0=1,w=2-Uloc/Wloc is
nevertheless negative at every q.

Law C changes finite and optimum weights nonuniformly throughout the
word. Actual H2 and full Silver calibrations pass. Exact whole-word
initial mixed cost defeats zeta_0, and a separate actual root-bridge/
right-query mode defeats every real q.

All FINAL multipliers are nonnegative and full value flow is exact;
none of the three laws has full PSD. Support availability, affordable
optimum price and calibration do not jointly certify gradient positivity.
The general relaxed identities identify allowed contact defects and exact
budget expenditure, but do not construct a successful all-function upper.

Accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7 gives ONLY B_(n_m)/D_m<237/250 for the SAME
tau1/4 physical word, m>=1536. A genuine full upper
W_(n_m)(Hword_m)<=zeta_m/D_m, sup zeta_m<250/237, would imply a fixed
unbounded-horizon improvement. No such upper is supplied here.
No restricted-family optimum or failed dual is converted into a W bound.
The original exact-profile goal remains OPEN.

## 9. Search, resources, persistence and honesty

All new mathematics here is ONE jointly UNVERIFIED draft. No intermediate

One fallback web call with two queries recovered Silver/composability
